# GLM review 2026-10-13 (glm-5.3): 386 findings, 164 high severity, 12 shard(s)

Revision c0fdb5912a6e. Shards: agentbox#2, agentbox#3, dreamlab-ai-website, estate, knowledgegraph, nostr-rust-forum, sidestr-rs, solid-pod-rs, visionclaw, visionclaw#3, visiongraph, vowl-wasm. Unverified hypotheses: reproduce each with a failing test or check (build-with-quality) before acting.

## agentbox#2 / critical

### F-01 — One compromised agent process reads every key in the box: same-uid secrets plus 35 ambient credentials
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.7, AB-16.14, AB-16.5
- Evidence: `role_isolation = false` is "the shipped default — a same-uid boundary. Every supervised program, MCP server, skill and agent runs as devuser, so each can read every other's 0600 key file, the AoE daemon token and the mirror key" (AB-16.1 N4). AB-16.7 shows peer reads of the profile key, `~/.config/agent-of-empires/serve.url` and `/run/secrets/nostr.key` all SUCCEED, and GOVERNANCE-capabilities item 5 concedes "does not isolate same-uid peers". On top of file access, "the 35 DEVUSER_CLASS credentials stay in every agent process by exception, owner disposition Q8 pending" (`config/custody/env-classes.json:92`) — provider API keys are ambient in every spawned child. The gateway's default identity is `'operator'`, so out of the box it "holds and signs with the ROOT operator key" (`gateway.cjs:193`).
- Failure: one prompt-injected or malicious MCP server, skill or consultant subprocess (all devuser, all env-inheriting) reads the sovereign operator Nostr key, the bridge key, the AoE control bearer and every provider credential, and exfiltrates them. Combined with F-07 there is no rotation or revocation path once this happens.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes for each piece (DIVERGENCE AB-16.1 N4, AB-16.7; DEBT Q8 AB-16.14; DIVERGENCE AB-16.5) — the combined single-incident blast radius is not drawn anywhere

### F-02 — The governance action pipeline is dead code for every side-effect surface except one, and the monitoring surface cannot see even that one
- Topics: AB-14.1, AB-14.2, AB-14.8, AB-14.15
- Evidence: AB-14.1: the pipeline is a "designed target, never instantiated for this surface" for direct tool calls; code-mode has "own guard only, no interceptor"; ACI shell is "allowlist only, no interceptor" with "raw Bash still reachable outside it"; consultants have "spend cap only"; the alternate harness path has "no interceptor at all". AB-14.2 note: the 9-stage state machine is "fully implemented and contract-tested … but is never reached by real traffic". Meanwhile `routes/system.js:26,61` still calls the old `buildExecutionCoverage(live)` with `live={}` and `server.js` never supplies `options.execution.snapshot`, so "GET /v1/system still reports status: declared always, even though a live instance now genuinely exists" (AB-14.8). AB-14.15 Open: the EXP-B8 tick's signed forum post and `gh` PR are "recorded neither through POST /v1/exec/record or the authority journal".
- Failure: an agent tool call, code-mode exec or consultant action performs a mutate/egress/secret-class side effect with no approval, no capability token and no journal entry — exactly what ADR-059's NEVER_FAIL_OPEN was built to prevent — and the operator's own status endpoint reports the execution plane as "declared", so nobody can tell wired from unwired.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE1 AB-14.1; DOC-DRIFT AB-14.8; Open AB-14.15)

### F-03 — Break-glass bearer is a full identity bypass, and its value equals BRIDGE_TOKEN
- Topics: AB-16.6, AB-16.9
- Evidence: while configured, a single shared secret on LAN port 9096 "bypasses NIP-98 entirely … a full identity bypass while enabled", also reachable "via ?access_token= / ?bearer= on WS upgrades" (AB-16.6). "Unset expiry permits unbounded lifetime", "unset scope is unrestricted", and the sentinel identity forwarded downstream "is NOT a real pubkey, so every downstream attribution for this request is a placeholder" (`proxy.mjs:693-706`). AB-16.9 C3: the bearer is "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4" — and BRIDGE_TOKEN is handed to other components (the voice stack sets `KYUTAI_LLM_API_KEY=${BRIDGE_TOKEN}`, `agentbox.sh:2445`). ADR-2027's lifecycle acceptance "remains open", `verified_paths` is the empty list and `verified_commit` stays pinned at an older sha (`ADR-2027:193-198`) — "attested by reading, not by a gate".
- Failure: any holder of the widely-distributed BRIDGE_TOKEN — voice frontend, any LAN client — becomes an operator-equivalent identity on the management API whenever break-glass is on, with placeholder attribution in every downstream record and only a fingerprinted log line to trace.
- Confidence: high (conditional on break-glass being configured; the value-equality makes that the likely configuration)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OPEN in AB-16.6); the BRIDGE_TOKEN-equality consequence is stated as a fact in AB-16.9 but its bypass implication is not drawn

### F-04 — Unredacted session content is mirrored to a public cloud relay, fail-open
- Topics: AB-16.11
- Evidence: the per-turn mirror "composes UNREDACTED selected text", NIP-59-wraps it to the derived child and publishes to `wss://dreamlab-nostr-relay.solitary-paper-764d.workers.dev` — "the only agentbox egress to a NON-LAN destination in this domain"; the off switch is "fail-open on any publish error"; ADR-2026 is "proposed / partial / INACTIVE for the complete egress policy" and "there is no single answer to 'is session content leaving this box, and redacted how'".
- Failure: any session that touches secrets, keys or customer data is shipped verbatim (wrapped, not redacted) to a third-party cloud worker; a publish failure is silent, so turning it off by breaking it produces no signal.
- Confidence: high for the behaviour; the shipped on/off default of the `[sovereign_mesh.mobile_bridge]` block is not shown
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE and TRUST BOUNDARY notes in AB-16.11)

### F-05 — The skill router egresses full user prompts to an external vendor by default
- Topics: AB-22.2
- Evidence: "router jev is the default at this revision (`agentbox.toml:1022`), so a turn that routes at all and misses the cascade leaves the container" — cascade is "off by default" (`agentbox.toml:1038`), so every routable prompt ≥ 24 chars is sent as "ONE Choice question over every routable skill description" to TypeSafe System One. ADR-2090 accepts egress "for skill routing ONLY", but the DEBT note says "the per-project gate is deferred", and the OPEN note says whether the acceptance covers "the descriptions of the operator's synced claude.ai skills, now sent as candidate rubrics, is not recorded" (`ADR-2091:25`).
- Failure: the first confidential session after deploy — pasted secrets, customer data, incident details — leaves the container verbatim to an external LLM vendor with no per-session consent and no per-project opt-out implemented.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT and OPEN in AB-22.2)

### F-06 — The dream engine's default provider sends nightly private repo content to a third-party API
- Topics: AB-23.15, AB-23.2, AB-24.14
- Evidence: "agentbox.toml:2191 keeps `llm_provider = "zai"` as the default, so nightly repository content leaves the LAN on every unattended night; the LAN-only Loom path … is opt-in" (AB-23.15 Debt). The egress register's zai-night row carries "marker no-accepted-record, nothing accepts the corpus leaving" (`config/egress-policy.json:254-268`).
- Failure: entire private repositories (annexe archives of the workspace, shipped to the connected node for evaluation prompts) are transmitted unattended, nightly, to Z.AI with no accepted record — a standing NDA/compliance breach for any repo containing third-party or licensed material.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt AB-23.15; no-accepted-record marker AB-24.14)

### F-07 — Secret rotation, revocation and tested recovery do not exist; the running image still has the plaintext backup path
- Topics: AB-16.8, AB-16.9, AB-16.12, AB-14.9
- Evidence: rotation and revocation are "NOT IMPLEMENTED. ADR-2027 is proposed / none / inactive. No cadence, no custodian, no incident window, no revocation procedure … for any of the seven register rows"; every register row is "custodian UNCONFIRMED, deployed location UNCONFIRMED, rotation cadence UNCONFIRMED"; publisher revocation requires a nix rebuild so "the compromise window is one build-deploy cycle". Backup is "PARTIAL and unverified … ordinary zip and unzip … NO encryption flags and no explicit umask", and the packaged age-encrypted tool has not reached production: "The active container has not been rebuilt with this source change" (AB-16.12). The audit hash chain's own note: "deletion at the tail is the ONE mode a bare chain cannot see" (AB-14.9).
- Failure: key compromise (see F-01) cannot be answered inside an incident window; the only recovery artefact is an unencrypted ZIP whose permissions depend on the invoking shell; SelfTest proves restore on synthetic data only.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (NOT IMPLEMENTED notes AB-16.8; DIVERGENCE AB-16.9; G17 note AB-16.12; chain limitation stated in AB-14.9)

### F-08 — One append-only JSONL file is the only route back for federated identities, and the two sides' content hashes are not proven equal
- Topics: AB-17.4, AB-17.6, AB-17.10
- Evidence: "Where the VisionClaw kind is content-addressed (execution, kg) the local is a FRESH sha256-12 and the original urn:agentbox identity is recovered only from the durable UrnMapping store — lose the store, lose the crossing"; a missing row returns null — "the crossing is unrecoverable"; `roundTrips()` proves the algebra against a fresh in-memory store, "not a durability or recovery proof"; "No recovery receipt exists for the durable store". Additionally "JS sha12 hashes a UTF-8 string, VisionClaw content_address hashes bytes, byte-parity asserted nowhere".
- Failure: deletion or corruption of `/var/lib/agentbox/code-harness/bc20-urn-mappings.jsonl` permanently orphans every federated execution and kg identity; independently, an unproven hash-parity divergence lets agentbox and VisionClaw disagree about which content an address names — silent provenance corruption with no test to catch it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, PROTOCOL-registry rows open, AB-17.4/17.6/17.10)

### F-09 — Deleting memory in one store does not revoke it in the other, and there is no memory backup, RPO or RTO
- Topics: AB-20.7
- Evidence: "the VisionClaw Solid Pod deleteAgentMemory() has NO REVERSE TOMBSTONE into RuVector, so deleting the pod copy does not revoke the RuVector-held agent memory. No point-in-time RuVector backup exists (SQLite-only backup-sqlite.sh), so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO for memory today."
- Failure: an erasure request (or an operator delete of a leaked secret that was memorised) leaves the full content readable and searchable in Postgres indefinitely; after a store corruption there is no consistent point-in-time to restore either side to.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE D5, AB-20.7)

### F-10 — Spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile, money movements never reach the authority gate, and the new wallet ships permissive-only
- Topics: AB-15.6, AB-14.11, AB-15 (closing drift)
- Evidence: "spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile: the flock-guarded cost_cap ledger file …, the pod-held sats balance …, and the marketplace token_budget …. No surface sums them, so no single number answers what this container has spent." Also: "the authority classification table names payment_settlement zero-tolerance as an irreversible above-threshold sats spend (agentbox.toml:1007), but no production path passes that actionClass to the gate … routes/payments.js never reaches management-api/lib/authority.js:226." And: "sidestr-wallet consults a SpendPolicy before every signature and ships only a permissive implementation, leaving the authority gate … to the caller, which nothing in this topic yet supplies."
- Failure: a sats deposit, buy or withdraw executes with no approval gate and no reconcilable ledger; a double-spend bug, an over-withdraw or a drained sidechain treasury is undetectable from inside the box until the chain says so.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT ×2 in AB-15.6/AB-14.11; Drift at end of AB-15)

### F-11 — The running binaries predate the dream-engine verdict fixes and the strict Loom client: staged, not live
- Topics: AB-23 (audit qualification), AB-23.12, AB-23.13, AB-24 (audit qualification), AB-24.13
- Evidence: "ADR-2081 source corrections above remain staged for the supervised nightly loop. Its loaded Nix-store binary requires an image rebuild and process-identity receipt before these source fixes can be called live." Those fixes close a bug where "an aborting cargo build recorded outcome=PASSED exit=0 — a pipe-masked false positive that upheld an ACCEPT on 2026-09-06 (PR 4)" (AB-23.12) and the no-patch-ACCEPT misclassification (AB-23.13). Similarly the strict `loomGenerationVerifier` is staged while "the live façade … still reports lexical generation 2026-08-22 … without loaded identity/embedding fields", and the baked `vault` 0.1.0 "predates the portable-records file, so it cannot produce a promotable bundle" — the served corpus stays stale until a rebuild.
- Failure: tonight's engine still grades with the tail-masking bug: a candidate whose build actually aborts can record PASSED, reach accepted=true, and open a PR whose evaluators never passed — feeding a ledger a human reviewer is told to trust.
- Confidence: high (explicitly stated as staged)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (audit qualifications in AB-23 and AB-24; Debt AB-24.13)

### F-12 — Three failed posts silently unjournal the rest of an unattended night — and the journaling ADR was accepted on a night with the journal down
- Topics: AB-23.2, AB-23.19, AB-14.15
- Evidence: "every turn and side effect posts to POST /v1/exec/record … Fail-open: three consecutive failed posts open a breaker and the rest of the session runs unjournalled (journal.rs:30,227-231)". The record also states the night is "journalled, not policed". ADR-2071 is now "accepted, implementation complete, activation live" after clause (c) ran with `supervisorctl stop management-api` (AB-23.19) — with the API deliberately stopped, every journal post fails, so the accepted night ran entirely behind the breaker (inferred: posts go to the management API, which was down). C3 passes on `dream-last-night.json` plus a clean stop, not on journal contents (`adr-2087-check.sh:411-413`).
- Failure: an unattended night partitioned from the API — precisely the scenario ADR-2071 exists for — leaves zero journalled record of its side effects, and the acceptance regime certifies that outcome as passing.
- Confidence: high for the breaker behaviour; the acceptance-night framing is inferred from stated facts
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: the breaker is stated as fact in AB-23.2 (not flagged as a defect); the acceptance contradiction is unmarked

### F-13 — The identity-port plan contradicts itself, and flipping the hardening flag silently disables governance and identity features
- Topics: AB-23.20, AB-14.16, AB-16.14
- Evidence: the port's ACL loader refuses kinds 31400–31405 because "governance kinds record a human decision and must not carry a container key" (`acl.rs:65-73`), yet the dream engine publishes exactly those kinds signed with the JunkieJarvis key (`governance.rs:9-10`) and "ADR-2122 still plans to move dream-engine onto the port in W3b" — "As written, the port can never sign what AB-23.17 sends." Under the flag today: `load_key` "warns agent key unavailable and the round trip is SKIPPED, fail-open" — "the forum governance round trip stops without an error"; JunkieJarvis forum replies and governance-panel posts "stop until the W3b consumer cutover lands" (AB-14.16 OPEN, `ADR-2122:218-219`); the live mirror "falls back to a throwaway sender" (AB-16.14 Open).
- Failure: the boot rehearsal flips `role_isolation`; that night governance cases stop being published, unclear forum posts are held rather than asked, the mirror changes identity mid-stream, and nothing raises an alert. If W3b proceeds as written, the migration cannot sign its own events at all.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (high if the flag flips without the rehearsal catching the silent stops)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension AB-23.20; Open AB-14.16; Open AB-16.14)

### F-14 — Provenance attribution defaults to container or operator identity on three surfaces, making audit records unfalsifiable
- Topics: AB-17.2, AB-17.10, AB-26.6
- Evidence: with `AGENTBOX_AGENT_EVENT_AUTH='off'` explicitly selected, the publisher "still stamps the CONTAINER identity on an event any caller could have raised. Attribution is then plausible, not provable" (`agent-event-publisher.js:147-149` env fallback). Elevation: "routes/kg-elevation.js resolves ownerPubkey as auth.pubkey or AGENTBOX_X_ONLY_PUBKEY_HEX or AGENTBOX_PUBKEY (kg-elevation.js:162), so an elevation can be attributed to the operator identity when the request carries no verified pubkey". Unmarked: typed-spawn defaults ownership to "opts.owner or AGENTBOX_REFINE_OPERATOR or did:nostr:jjohare" (`typed-spawn.js:64`) — every unattributed spawn bead is recorded as owned by one named human.
- Failure: governance and audit records name the operator (or one specific person) for actions any co-resident process performed — exactly where AB-17.1 claims ADR-013 identity attribution "is never dropped at the federation boundary".
- Confidence: high for the behaviour; medium for incident impact
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes for the first two (DIVERGENCE AB-17.2, AB-17.10); the typed-spawn default is unmarked

### F-15 — A TUI save silently resets hand-edited model-routing config, including the cost ceiling, to fixed literals
- Topics: AB-28.10, AB-29.2
- Evidence: `tui_sections.rs:193-200` emits `[model_routing.neural]` with "every OTHER key a FIXED literal (provider "openrouter", quality_bar 0.50, cost_ceiling_usd_per_mtok 0, privacy_tier "public", trajectory true, assets_dir "/opt/agentbox/model-router")"; "a TUI save therefore RESETS provider/quality_bar/cost_ceiling/trajectory/assets_dir to these literals even if the operator hand-edited agentbox.toml to different values — … there is no read-back-and-preserve path" (unlike the ADR-2031 consultant-model rule). The literals are then projected as `AGENTBOX_MODEL_ROUTER_COST_CEILING_USD_PER_MTOK` etc. to the console (AB-29.2), which dispatches to a paid external API.
- Failure: an operator sets a real spend ceiling (or a different provider); the next TUI save of any unrelated key resets it to 0/openrouter without warning — the router console then runs against OpenRouter with a silently changed ceiling.
- Confidence: high that the reset occurs (stated); medium on the cost consequence, inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT AB-28.10), though only as a doc-drift note rather than a data-loss-of-config defect

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether break-glass is actually configured in the deployed environment, and with what expiry/scope values — F-04's severity hinges on deployment configuration the corpus does not show.
2. The current state of `role_isolation` on the running image and whether the staged dream-engine, Loom-client and `vault` rebuilds (F-11) have shipped — I would need the deployed image digest and the running binary versions.
3. The consumer semantics of `cost_ceiling_usd_per_mtok = 0` and an empty `loom_model` (does 0 mean "no ceiling"? does the facade guarantee answering an empty model name?) — needed to size F-15 and the loom-night fragility in AB-24.7.
4. Whether the relay actually hard-deletes an author's events on a kind-5 and whether the forum renders cases by subscription — AB-23.17 marks this "asserted, not verified"; the relay/forum sources are outside this corpus.
5. Actual network reachability: who can reach ports 9096/8080/8188 from the LAN and whether `visionclaw_network` containers are mutually trusted (sizes F-03, F-04, and the code-server `0.0.0.0` bind in AB-27.6), plus the cloud worker relay's retention policy for mirrored session content.

## agentbox#2 / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-loss incident (data / custody)

**Incident summary, as reconstructed from the estate diagrams:** client-confidential material left the estate through paths that were default-on and ungoverned at the verified revision, and the estate's signing keys were readable to the process that carried the disclosure. Every mechanism below was already documented in the corpus twelve months ago; none of it is new behaviour.

---

### R-1 — Nightly dream cycle ships the whole client corpus to a vendor by default, with no accepted egress record and no approval layer
- Topics: AB-23.2, AB-23.15, AB-24.14, AB-14.1, AB-14.15, AB-23.6
- Chain of events: a client repo is nominated for dreaming → the dream engine runs unattended in the 01:00–05:00 UTC window → the model call goes to the **default** provider Z.AI (`llm_provider = "zai"`), carrying repository content off the LAN → the egress register marks this path `no-accepted-record` — nothing accepts the corpus leaving → the engine applies any returned patch on an isolated worktree and opens a PR on the repo, governed only by in-engine evaluator gates and a human-merge gate that is a *process*, not a code control → journal posts go to `POST /v1/exec/record`, which "records, never approves or denies", and three consecutive failed posts open a breaker after which the rest of the night runs **unjournalled** → the client's corpus sits at a vendor with no record that anyone authorised it.
- Evidence: "the dream engine's DEFAULT reasoning provider is Z.AI (`llm_provider = "zai"`) … nightly repo content leaves the LAN unless `llm_provider = loom`" (AB-23.2, ADR-2053 note); "agentbox.toml:2191 keeps `llm_provider = "zai"` as the default, so nightly repository content leaves the LAN on every unattended night" (AB-23.15 Debt); "zai-night: … marker no-accepted-record, nothing accepts the corpus leaving" (`agentbox/config/egress-policy.json:254-268`, AB-24.14); "Records, never approves or denies (`exec-record.js:16-17`). Fail-open: three consecutive failed posts open a breaker and the rest of the session runs unjournalled (`journal.rs:30,227-231`)" (AB-23.2); "every OTHER surface … (direct tool call, code-mode, ACI shell, consultant, dream, beads, alt harness) still reaches EXEC with no interceptor" (AB-14.1 DIVERGENCE1); "the human-merge boundary is a PROCESS, not a code control" (AB-23.6).
- Warning signs visible today: the egress register itself flags the path as unaccepted; the governance pipeline is wired for exactly one surface (POST /v1/tasks) and the dream engine only records; the accepted ADR-2071 clause (c) test deliberately ran a night with the management API — the journal's only writer — stopped (AB-23.19), institutionalising an unjournalled unattended window.
- Confidence: high that the exposure exists exactly as described; the link to this specific incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Every routed prompt leaves the container to an external model, by default, with no per-project gate
- Topics: AB-22.2
- Chain of events: `[skills.routing].router = "jev"` is the default and the hook is registered → on UserPromptSubmit, any prompt ≥ `min_prompt_chars` 24 that is not a slash command is sent as "ONE Choice question over every routable skill description" to TypeSafe System One (model Jev), budget `timeout_ms` 4000 → the local BM25 cascade that could answer without egress exists but is **off by default** (`agentbox.toml:1038`) → the candidate rubrics sent alongside the prompt have grown past the manifest to include the operator's synced claude.ai account skills and enabled plugin skills (`lib/skill-route.cjs:276`, merged `:439-446`) → ADR-2090's acceptance covers "the routing prompt … for skill routing ONLY"; whether it covers those synced skill descriptions "is not recorded" (ADR-2091:25) → any session working on client material discloses its full prompts to a third party, and the per-project gate is explicitly deferred.
- Evidence: "DEBT — router jev is the default at this revision (`agentbox.toml:1022`), so a turn that routes at all and misses the cascade leaves the container. The per-project gate is deferred" (AB-22.2); "cascade = true (`agentbox.toml:1038`, off by default)"; "OPEN: ADR-2090 accepts egress of the routing PROMPT, but whether that acceptance also covers the descriptions of the operator's synced claude.ai skills … is not recorded" (AB-22.2, citing `docs/adr/ADR-2091-live-skill-router.md:25`); "Egress of the routing prompt is an accepted operator decision for skill routing ONLY" (AB-22.2, ADR-2090 invariant).
- Warning signs visible today: the acceptance is scoped "ONLY" to skill routing yet the shipped default egresses every qualifying turn; the scope already crept once (synced skills) without a recorded decision.
- Confidence: high that the default egress exists; inferred that it carried client-confidential prompts in this incident.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Total custody collapse: with `role_isolation` off (the shipped default), any agent process can read every signing key
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.7, AB-16.13, AB-16.14, AB-14.16, AB-16.8, AB-16.9, AB-16.5
- Chain of events: any supervised program, MCP server, skill or agent runs as `devuser` uid 1000 → it reads the bridge key (`/run/secrets/nostr.key`, written 0400 devuser while the flag is off, `config/entrypoint-unified.sh:1082-1091`), every per-agent DID key file, the AoE daemon token, and the 35 `DEVUSER_CLASS` provider credentials that sit in every agent process's environment by exception → it can sign as the bridge/sovereign identity, spend, and publish; under the flag-off default the gateway itself "holds and signs with the ROOT operator key" (identity `'operator'` is the default, `gateway.cjs:192-193`) → rotation and revocation are **not implemented** (ADR-2027 proposed/none/inactive) and publisher revocation requires a full nix rebuild — "the compromise window is one build-deploy cycle" (AB-16.8) → the hardening that exists (identity port, per-role uids) ships off, is only wired for the pods signer, and enabling it *silently stops* the forum governance round trip and degrades the live mirror, so there is operational pressure to leave it off.
- Evidence: "role_isolation OFF, the shipped default — a same-uid boundary. Every supervised program, MCP server, skill and agent runs as devuser, so each can read every other's 0600 key file, the AoE daemon token and the mirror key" (AB-16.1 N4); "a process running as the same devuser can still read the token file … Per-process isolation is future work" (GOVERNANCE-capabilities item 5, quoted in AB-16.7); "the 35 DEVUSER_CLASS credentials stay in every agent process by exception, owner disposition Q8 pending" (`agentbox/config/custody/env-classes.json:92`, AB-16.14); "sign_event, forum_event and nip42_auth have no client at this revision … they fail closed until the W3b cutover" (`docs/adr/ADR-2122-…md:218-219`, AB-14.16); "under the flag the forum governance round trip stops without an error" (`governance.rs:645-653`, AB-23.20 Open); every custody-register row: custodian UNCONFIRMED, cadence UNCONFIRMED (AB-16.9).
- Warning signs visible today: the custody register has zero confirmed custodians; the identity port's own plan cannot sign the governance kinds (see F-14); derived keys and the gateway root-key default were already flagged as divergences.
- Confidence: high that the exposure exists as stated; inferred that key material was actually taken in this incident.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Journal fail-open breaker silently ends the audit trail mid-night
- Topics: AB-23.2, AB-14.15, AB-23.19
- Evidence: "three consecutive failed posts open a breaker and the rest of the session runs unjournalled (`agentbox/services/dream-engine/src/journal.rs:30,227-231`)" (AB-23.2); the journal's single writer is the management API (`exec-record.js:63`), and ADR-2071 clause (c) ran an accepted night with that API deliberately stopped (AB-23.19).
- Failure: any API outage during the unattended window — including the sanctioned api-down night — leaves the entire back half of the night's side effects with no record at all; nothing detects the gap afterwards.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated as designed behaviour, not flagged as a defect)
- Severity: medium

### F-02 — Spend is triple-booked across three stores that never reconcile
- Topics: AB-15.6
- Evidence: "spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile: the flock-guarded cost_cap ledger file (`services/agentbox-ops/src/cost_cap/ledger.rs:75`), the pod-held sats balance behind GET /v1/pay/balance (`routes/payments.js:205`), and the marketplace token_budget (`lib/llm-marketplace.js:85`). No surface sums them" (AB-15.6 DEBT).
- Failure: an agent drains one store while the others report headroom; the operator cannot produce a defensible spend figure for a client dispute.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)
- Severity: medium

### F-03 — The `payment_settlement` zero-tolerance class is never wired to the authority gate
- Topics: AB-14.11, AB-15.6
- Evidence: "the classification table names payment_settlement zero-tolerance (`agentbox.toml:1074`) but no production path passes that actionClass. The only call sites are tests, and `management-api/lib/authority.js:226` never sees it" (AB-14.11 DEBT; repeated at AB-15.6 with `agentbox.toml:1007`).
- Failure: a real irreversible sats spend above threshold executes with no operator approval, because the one gate written for exactly that case is unreachable from the payments routes.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — Live mirror publishes unredacted session text to a non-LAN cloud worker, fail-open
- Topics: AB-16.11, AB-16.5
- Evidence: mirror "composes UNREDACTED selected text" then gift-wraps to the derived child and publishes to `wss://dreamlab-nostr-relay.solitary-paper-764d.workers.dev` — "the only agentbox egress to a NON-LAN destination in this domain"; off switch fails open on any publish error; "there is no single answer to 'is session content leaving this box, and redacted how'" (ADR-2026 divergence, AB-16.11).
- Failure: client session content is mirrored off-box unredacted (before wrapping); a publish-path regression silently continues, and the child secret that unwraps it is handed to devuser via the port (`identity-port-acl.json:42`, `port.rs:635-637`, AB-16.5 tension).
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — Break-glass bearer is a full identity bypass, unbounded by default, and equals BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4
- Topics: AB-16.6, AB-16.9
- Evidence: "A single shared secret bypasses NIP-98 entirely … a full identity bypass while enabled" on port 9096 and via `?access_token=` on WS upgrades (AB-16.6); "unset expiry permits unbounded lifetime", "unset scope is unrestricted", durable per-use receipt unproven; "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4" (AB-16.9 C3); ADR-2027 sets `verified_paths` empty and pins an older `verified_commit` (`docs/adr/ADR-2027-…md:193-198`).
- Failure: the bearer leaks once (it is a long-lived env/file value identical to another secret), giving permanent unscoped LAN identity as a sentinel pubkey whose downstream attribution is a placeholder.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OPEN)
- Severity: high

### F-06 — Gateway signs as the ROOT operator key out of the box
- Topics: AB-16.5
- Evidence: "the DEFAULT identity is 'operator', so out of the box the gateway holds and signs with the ROOT operator key. The derived-child mode exists but is opt-in via env" (`agentbox/config/nostr-gateway/gateway.cjs:192-193`, AB-16.5 DIVERGENCE).
- Failure: any gateway compromise or misconfiguration commands the estate as the operator identity — the one pubkey allowed to command (`gateway.cjs:189,195`).
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: high

### F-07 — Derived child keys are outside the custody register with no revocation on rotation
- Topics: AB-16.5
- Evidence: "neither derived key appears in the SECURITY-profiles provisional custody register … rotation is 'bump the env tag' with no revocation of the previously derived child at any relay" (AB-16.5 DIVERGENCE ADR-2027).
- Failure: a rotated mirror/gateway child keeps working at every relay that saw it; the register never knew it existed.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — Key-persistence failure is non-fatal: a whole session can run on a key that vanishes at restart
- Topics: AB-16.7
- Evidence: "persistence failure is NON-FATAL (`agent-identity.js:168-171`), so a container can run a whole session on an in-memory key that vanishes at restart. 'Key persistence failure can produce a valid but unstable identity.'" (AB-16.7 DIVERGENCE).
- Failure: events, receipts and forum posts signed under an identity that no longer exists — deniable or unattributable after the fact.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — Attribution fallbacks stamp the container/operator identity on events any caller raised
- Topics: AB-17.2, AB-17.10
- Evidence: with `AGENTBOX_AGENT_EVENT_AUTH='off'` explicitly selected, the env fallback "still stamps the CONTAINER identity on an event any caller could have raised. Attribution is then plausible, not provable" (`agent-event-publisher.js:147-149`, AB-17.2 DIVERGENCE); kg-elevation resolves `ownerPubkey` as `auth.pubkey` or `AGENTBOX_X_ONLY_PUBKEY_HEX` or `AGENTBOX_PUBKEY` (`routes/kg-elevation.js:162`, AB-17.10 DIVERGENCE).
- Failure: a governance-relevant event or elevation is attributed to the operator identity without any signature proving it.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — BC20: losing the JSONL mapping store loses the crossing identity; JS/Rust content-address byte parity is unasserted
- Topics: AB-17.4, AB-17.6, AB-17.10
- Evidence: for execution/kg the local URN is "a FRESH sha256-12 … recovered only from the durable UrnMapping store — lose the store, lose the crossing" (AB-17.4); `roundTrips()` "proves the algebra, not the deployment" and no recovery receipt exists (AB-17.4/17.6); "JS sha12 hashes a UTF-8 string, VisionClaw content_address hashes bytes, byte-parity asserted nowhere" (AB-17.10 DIVERGENCE).
- Failure: a lost or corrupt `bc20-urn-mappings.jsonl` (or a string-vs-bytes hash divergence) permanently severs provenance between agentbox and VisionClaw records for every content-addressed artefact.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: high

### F-11 — No verified backup or recovery for secrets or memory
- Topics: AB-16.12, AB-16.8, AB-20.7
- Evidence: the age-encrypted backup tool requires explicit operator invocation, "the active container has not been rebuilt with this source change", and "retention, off-host placement and the recovery-authority test remain the operator's responsibility — SelfTest proves restore works on synthetic data only" (AB-16.12 N2/N4); "No point-in-time RuVector backup exists (SQLite-only backup-sqlite.sh), so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO for memory today" and pod deletes have no reverse tombstone (AB-20.7 DIVERGENCE D5).
- Failure: volume loss destroys the sovereign identity material and the entire learned memory corpus with no tested recovery path — and a pod-side deletion is never revoked from RuVector.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE / acceptance gap)
- Severity: high

### F-12 — HNSW recall degrades silently after bulk deletes; the recall harness is the only detector
- Topics: AB-20.10, AB-20.7
- Evidence: "Healthy → Degraded : bulk deletion, e.g. memory_sweep_episodic"; "Recall drops with no error anywhere — nothing in the query path reports it, which is why the harness is the only detector" (AB-20.10); "a bulk delete degrades the HNSW graph silently. See AB-20.10" (AB-20.7).
- Failure: a routine episodic sweep quietly degrades semantic recall; agents keep retrieving with no signal until someone runs the harness, and decisions made on degraded recall are already shipped.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (documented index law with silent-degradation note)
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — Nothing can retire colloquy knowledge: Retired/Superseded have no write path
- Topics: AB-22.7, AB-28.11
- Evidence: "no code path in this crate or colloquy-mcp's six verbs currently WRITES UnitStatus::Retired or Superseded; only tests set them directly. The 'signed decision' retirement door … is not yet wired to an MCP verb" (`colloquy-core/src/graduation.rs:400` test only, AB-22.7 OPEN); disputed units "are still servable and are ranked down, never hidden" (`unit.rs:269-271`).
- Failure: known-wrong knowledge that has been flagged disputed keeps informing agents indefinitely; the lifecycle the ADRs describe cannot actually complete.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN, DEBT)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — The custody plan contradicts the governance plan: the identity port can never sign what the dream engine publishes, and the flag silently disables governance
- Topics: AB-23.20, AB-14.16, AB-16.13
- Evidence: "the port's loader refuses to grant kinds 31400-31405 … Yet the dream engine publishes the 31400 panel and 31402 cases signed with the JunkieJarvis key … As written, the port can never sign what AB-23.17 sends" (`identity_port/acl.rs:65-73`, `governance.rs:9-10`, ADR-2122:224-225, AB-23.20 TENSION); under the flag "the forum governance round trip stops without an error … `load_key` treats the refusal as fail-open and skips" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:452-454`, `governance.rs:645-653`); the mirror "falls back to a throwaway sender (`nostr-live-mirror.cjs:225-233`) until the W3b cutover" (AB-16.13 Open).
- Failure: an operator enables role_isolation as the hardening step and the forum decision inbox silently stops receiving cases while the nightly cycle keeps running — governance visibility lost with no error anywhere; the planned W3b fix is impossible as specified.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Open)
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — Unattended EXP-B8 tick performs outward side effects outside both journals
- Topics: AB-14.15, AB-23.19
- Evidence: "the EXP-B8 tick takes two outward side effects when its stopping rule fires, a signed forum post (`scripts/experiments/exp-b8-label-log.cjs:550-563`) and a pull request opened with `gh` (`:573-606`), and records neither through POST /v1/exec/record or the authority journal" (AB-14.15 Open); it runs every 30 minutes from the podcast-ingest crontab (`skills/podcast-knowledge-ingest/crontab:34-44`, AB-23.19 Debt).
- Failure: an unattended experiment posts to the public forum and opens PRs on repos with zero journal or authority record — the exact class of side effect ADR-2071 was meant to make visible.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, Debt)
- Severity: medium

## agentbox#3 / critical

### F-01 — Every secret in the container is readable by every process, today, in the shipped configuration
- Topics: AB-36 (intro), AB-36.6, AB-36.7, AB-34.6
- Evidence: `[security].role_isolation` ships **false** (`agentbox.toml:2227`); with it off the entrypoint execs `/etc/supervisord.conf` (`entrypoint-unified.sh:1207`) and every program runs `user=devuser` (`flake.nix:2947`, `flake.nix:2982`); "Until this change every secret in the container reached every process… every long-running program ran as devuser (uid 1000), so a 0600 key file protected nothing from a co-resident agent". Even with the flag **on**, 35 DEVUSER_CLASS credentials — "among them provider API keys, `BRIDGE_TOKEN` and `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`" — "remain in the agent environment by exception Q8" in both modes (`env-classes.json:90`, `env-classes.json:92`, `ADR-2122:158-159`). The only committed rehearsal receipt is STAGED with the flag false (`x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:4`, `:8`); the business text confirms "It is switched off today."
- Failure: any one compromised or mistaken agent tool, running as devuser, reads the sovereign Nostr identity key, the chain signer keys, the faucet treasury key and the ambient management-API bearer — impersonation of the organisation and movement of chain value in a single step, with no boundary in place.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open on activation and Debt on the 35 credentials, AB-36.6/AB-36.7; presented as a staged rollout rather than as a live exposure)

### F-02 — The host Docker socket bypass is open in the shipped mode, and the flag-on mitigation cannot fully close it anyway
- Topics: AB-36 (intro), AB-36.7
- Evidence: "A fourth bypass, the host Docker socket, only closes under the flag" — and the flag ships false (`agentbox.toml:2227`). "The host Docker socket inode stays world-accessible until the host chmods it. The socket is a bind of the host inode that earlier boots widened, and skipping the chmod under the flag does not narrow it (`docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`). The flag-on boot can only detect and record `degraded:docker-socket` (`entrypoint-unified.sh:605`)." Rehearsal check (f) treats "no docker socket" as a pass condition (`role-isolation-rehearsal.sh:25-26`).
- Failure: in the shipped flag-off boot, every devuser process — i.e. every agent — can write to the world-accessible host Docker socket, which is root-equivalent on the host: mount the host filesystem, spawn privileged containers, read every host secret including the container's own volumes. No container-side control in this material stops it.
- Confidence: high (inferred — the material states the bypass exists, closes only under the flag, and the flag ships off)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially (Open R2 covers only the flag-on residual; the flag-off exposure is implied, not marked as a finding)

### F-03 — The shipped image bakes a `NOPASSWD: ALL` sudoers drop-in for devuser, held off only by two removable guards
- Topics: AB-36.7
- Evidence: built with `role_isolation` off — "which is how it ships" — the image keeps devuser in `wheel` (`flake.nix:3864`) and bakes a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in (`bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`, called at `flake.nix:3878`), while "the same flake says the setuid wrapper is gone and there is no runtime escalation path (`flake.nix:3632-3636`)". The grant "is inert only because `no-new-privileges` is set (`docker-compose.yml:129`) and no setuid sudo ships"; an off-built/on-booted image "records `degraded:devuser-sudo` rather than repairing it (`entrypoint-unified.sh:642`)". "Reverting either guard on a flag-off build turns devuser back into root, past every role boundary in this topic."
- Failure: anyone debugging a blocked operation who removes the compose `no-new-privileges` flag or supplies a setuid sudo (a routine image change) converts devuser — the uid all agents run as — into passwordless root; the boot's own response is a log line, not a repair or a refusal.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, sudoers vs the no-runtime-sudo design)

### F-04 — Billing today debits a JSON document across three ledgers of which only one is atomic, with no reconciliation
- Topics: AB-31 (For the business), AB-31.6, AB-31.9
- Evidence: "the balance it debits is a JSON document rather than value anywhere (`sovereign-settlement.md:51-55`)"; the forum D1 adapter is "the only one of the three that debits atomically today" (ADR-2099:39-43, AB-31.6 V2); the host file payment store and its pay handler are still live (ADR-2099:43-44); three-ledger equality on a hundred random principals is only a **P2 exit criterion** (`sovereign-settlement.md:338`), i.e. not a property that holds now.
- Failure: a crash, rollback or two concurrent payments updates the three balances inconsistently — a payment credited in one ledger and not another, or a double debit — and with no chain and no reconciliation mechanism named anywhere in the material, the discrepancy is undiscoverable from the records the estate keeps.
- Confidence: medium (the state is stated; the frequency and mechanism of divergence are inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (presented as motivation for the settlement proposal, not as an operational risk to the running system)

### F-05 — The gates that keep real money off the chain are stubs: containment is not committed on-chain and the p21 receipt is checked for existence only
- Topics: AB-32.2, AB-32.3, AB-31.9
- Evidence: ADR-2103 D3 claims `parent`, `headerProfile`, `currencyPin`, `cashOut`, `pegConfirmations`, `refundBlocks` are committed as a `pin:` record in the genesis coinbase (`ADR-2103:73-74`); the seal established upstream commits only the chain id, pegs, genesisTime and signer witness — "every containment field" is NOT committed and the pin is UNBUILT (`ADR-2103:206-214`), with the upstream proposal "not been filed" (`:213`). The genesis gate's mainnet check is presence-only: "Resolving the receipt is NOT checked" (`sidechain-genesis.test.sh:86`), and the CI check that the receipt resolves, the check forbidding a level-1 chain on a mainnet parent, and a node that refuses to open one are all unbuilt (`ADR-2103:229-230`). A prior record called the same containment "architecturally enforced" while it was not built (`sovereign-settlement.md:60`).
- Failure: the guarantees `cashOut: false` and the mainnet receipt — the entire P21 story that "real money stays behind a legal gate" — exist only in a git file plus a field-existence test. A document with different containment but the same genesis commitments, or a mainnet-parent seal with a placeholder receipt, passes every gate that is built; nothing on the chain itself can contradict it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (latent until the chain carries value, but it is the custody control the whole design rests on)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension and Open in AB-32.2, Debt in AB-32.3)

### F-06 — The faucet treasury key stays readable by every process in both isolation modes, and no built mechanism ever removes it
- Topics: AB-34.3, AB-34.6
- Evidence: with the flag off, "which is how it ships", the faucet "still defaults to, and the live manifest still sets, the workspace path (`run-faucet.sh:27`, `agentbox.toml:1638`)"; "the migrate step copies that legacy file once and never deletes it, so a spend-capable key stays on the host-visible bind in both modes until a manual purge (`ADR-2122:212-215`)."
- Failure: any devuser process — every agent, before activation and after it — reads the treasury key and drains the faucet or mints grants at will; the copy that activation is supposed to protect survives the activation as a permanent second exposure requiring an unprompted manual purge.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (the key spends test assets only today, but it is a custody-class defect that outlives the mitigation)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension "narrowed by custody", AB-34.3)

### F-07 — The TLS bridge falls back to a hard-coded LAN IP and silently forwards decrypted traffic there
- Topics: AB-30.4
- Evidence: "detectGatewayIP() if HOST_IP unset — `ip route | grep default` (`https-proxy.js:21-28`), falls back to 192.168.0.51 **on any failure**"; the proxy then "forwards method/headers" to `http://HOST_IP:TARGET_PORT` (`https-proxy.js:193-203`).
- Failure: on any host where `ip route` fails or has no default route, the bridge terminates TLS and sends the request — including whatever Authorization or cookie headers the browser attached for the management API — to whatever machine happens to hold 192.168.0.51 on that network; responses from a stranger are relayed back to the operator as if they were the management API.
- Confidence: high (the fallback is stated; the credential-leak path is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — The role-isolation boundary fails open: a missing artefact or mount regression silently boots the flat-secrets mode
- Topics: AB-36.2, AB-36.3, AB-36.7
- Evidence: "An image that lacks either artefact boots as if the flag were off, and says so loudly" — `ROLE-ISOLATION-UNAVAILABLE` (`entrypoint-unified.sh:357`, `role-custody.sh:59`); `/run/secrets` not being a mount point is `ROLE-ISOLATION-DEGRADED` and boot continues (`role-custody.sh:99`); secret delivery "fails open with a counted, grep-able marker" (`role-custody.sh:11-14`); `degraded:docker-socket` and `degraded:devuser-sudo` are detect-and-record only (`entrypoint-unified.sh:605`, `:642`).
- Failure: a partially built image, a lost plan file or a mount regression puts the box back into the mode where every process reads every key, while `[security].role_isolation` still reads `true` in the config an operator or agent inspects; nothing refuses the boot, and discovery depends on someone grepping logs no watcher is described for.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as designed behaviour, not flagged as a risk)

### F-09 — After activation the identity port still grants devuser broad sovereign signing powers and hands it a secret, contradicting the business claim
- Topics: AB-36.4, AB-36.5
- Evidence: the ACL grants devuser (uid 1000) `sign_event` on the core key for kinds 38410-38413, 38415, 30840, 30841 (`identity-port-acl.json:39`), `forum_event` as JunkieJarvis for kinds 1, 42, 31923 (`:40`), and `mirror_key with secret true` (`:42`) — the last returns the live-mirror child key to the caller. The business text says the agents "just no longer hold the keys."
- Failure: a compromised agent running as devuser can still publish as the estate's forum persona and sign the sovereign key's event kinds through the port, and obtains the mirror child secret outright — the boundary narrows key *exfiltration*, it does not deliver the impersonation-resistant state the record advertises.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (the `mirror_key` secret return is deferred to a tension "recorded once, in AB-16"; the breadth of devuser's sign grants is not marked)

### F-10 — Two governing texts specify different key hierarchies for the same custody invariant
- Topics: AB-31.7, AB-34.4
- Evidence: PRD-024 "still derives k_spend and k_sign from k_id by derive_subkey (`sovereign-settlement.md:171-173`)"; the registry row, "since the 2026-10-02 ADR-2097 D3 amendment", says the binding key is "an INDEPENDENT key minted beside k_id… never derived (`PROTOCOL-registry.md:172`)".
- Failure: whichever text an implementer follows, the other's tests and records are wrong. Following the PRD makes "a compromised wallet is never a compromised identity" (`INGRESS-identity.md:456`) rest entirely on the domain-separation correctness of `derive_subkey` — precisely the property the registry amendment stopped trusting; following the registry leaves the PRD's wallet derivation unimplementable as written.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, AB-34.4 DR node)

### F-11 — The https-bridge's CORS headers are a combination browsers reject for credentialed requests
- Topics: AB-30.4
- Evidence: the proxy sets "Access-Control-Allow-Origin * AND Access-Control-Allow-Credentials true (`https-proxy.js:208-211`)"; the material's own note: this "is a combination browsers reject for credentialed requests per the Fetch spec — the two headers as written cannot both take effect for any request that actually carries credentials."
- Failure: the bridge exists so a browser can reach the management API cross-origin (`https-proxy.js:3-4`); every credentialed call through it fails, so the operator path is broken for exactly the requests that matter, and the pressure this creates pushes toward shipping credentials in a form CORS cannot reason about.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE note, AB-30.4)

### F-12 — The live level-2 co-signing runs the round the estate's own review rejected, on kinds the review said cannot carry it
- Topics: AB-35 (drift note), AB-35.3, AB-34.5
- Evidence: "Level 2 usable: sidestr-round 0.1.0's cosign co-signs with the JS signers live (`sovereign-settlement.md:336`, AB-34.5 N6)"; the drift note says sidestr-round (now 0.2.0) "shipped upstream's availability-tolerant round (not the reviewed BFT shape)"; the review found kinds 23510-23514 "are EPHEMERAL under NIP-01: relays are not expected to retain them" and `chain` "is not a portable indexed filter" (`REVIEW-kofn-consensus-gpt6-astra.md:217`); "the registry still allocates the ephemeral 23510-23514 band… without recording that the review rejected the shape those kinds were built for (`PROTOCOL-registry.md:167`)".
- Failure: the moment a second signer joins a root, the running round exhibits the review's documented failure mode — a safety violation that loses money rather than stalling — and its certificates can be silently dropped by relays, losing finality evidence; the registry continues to present the band as fit for the purpose.
- Confidence: high on the facts; the impact is latent while roots are single-signer
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (Debt and Open in AB-35.3 mark the registry gap; that the rejected shape co-signs live is not marked)

### F-13 — The upstream-pins file violates its own bump rule, and the violation is baked into the image
- Topics: AB-34.1
- Evidence: the file's rule is "to bump a line only after the chain has been restarted and a block produced on that commit (`upstream-pins:5-7`)"; "the `blaketestnode` line was bumped on 2026-10-01 as an 'operator-accepted active development head', a reason that is acceptance rather than a produced block (`upstream-pins:10`, commit `47e187934`). The bake now carries that same commit (`lib/sidestr-upstream.nix:57-60`)."
- Failure: consensus-relevant code — the BLAKE2b parent node that validates `sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4` — ships at a development head that never produced the evidence the file's own rule demands; the precedent means every future bump can bypass the rule by citing acceptance, and the untested head is immutable in baked images until a rebuild.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, AB-34.1)

### F-14 — `SIDESTR_ALLOW_UNPINNED=1` reopens agent-writable consensus code with no bound on how long it stays set
- Topics: AB-34.1
- Evidence: "while the override is set, the exposure the bake closed is back, a dirty checkout runs, and nothing bounds how long it stays set (`run-producer.sh:87-93`, DEBT node UN2)"; the unpinned mode "logs SIDESTR-UNPINNED every start… warns, never refuses (`run-producer.sh:86-93`)".
- Failure: an operator sets the override to test an upstream fix and forgets it; from then on the producer executes whatever is in the workspace checkout — writable by devuser, i.e. by every agent in the shipped mode — so block production and validation code is agent-modifiable, with only a start-time log line as a trace.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, AB-34.1 UN2)

### F-15 — Each chain is a single signer, unanchored, supervised FATAL-after-5, with stale-serving dependents and no described notification path
- Topics: AB-32 (intro), AB-32.4, AB-34.1, AB-34.3, AB-34.5
- Evidence: one signer per chain (`sidechain/README.md:10` area, AB-32 intro); `checkpoint_every = 0`, "NOT ANCHORED (`agentbox.toml:1666`, AB-34.5 X3)"; "startretries 5 then FATAL: a failed start means a pin to fix, not a restart loop (`flake.nix:2939-2942`, `:2952`)"; on producer failure the mirror "keep[s] the previous copy, do not fail the loop" (`mirror-sync.sh`, AB-34.3) and the faucet "wait[s] on GET tip every 15 s" indefinitely (`run-faucet.sh:65-68`); the parent is one LAN Bitcoin node (AB-32.4); the business text records that a container restart on 25 September "stopped it for four days with nothing to bring it back."
- Failure: one unreadable key, one failed preflight or one down parent node puts the producer in FATAL with nothing to recover it and no alerting described anywhere; readers continue to be served the stale mirror as if current and the faucet silently never pays; loss of the single signer key ends the chain permanently — no second signer, no anchor to recover from.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (the outage history and the unanchored status are stated; the composite availability and key-loss risk is not marked as such)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. The secret-backup service: `services/secret-backup/src/main.rs` is an AB-30 source but has no section — whether the chain signer keys, identity keys and block file are backed up, encrypted, and restore-tested is invisible, and custody sign-off depends on it.
2. The shipped compose topology: actual port publications (the https bridge's "loopback-only via compose" is only a code comment, `https-proxy.js:31-33`), the docker-socket bind's container-side path and mode in the flag-off boot, the contents of `docker-compose.override.yml`, and the host's uid/gid allocation across the 960-979 range the roles bake into.
3. The management API's blast radius under `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` — what a bearer holder can command (supervision, rebuilds, chain actions) — needed to size the ambient-credential exposure in F-01.
4. Whether anything watches the failure markers the material produces (`ROLE-ISOLATION-UNAVAILABLE`, `degraded:*`, "push failed and will retry", supervisord FATAL) — no monitoring or alerting path appears in any topic.
5. AB-16 (the pods-signer cutover and the live-mirror child key handling, explicitly deferred), and whether the JSON payment store carries real obligations today — both change the severity of F-04 and F-09.

## agentbox#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — custody and boundary failure in the agentbox container

Basis: this report is reconstructed solely from the distilled topic files at `verified_commit 6466e39313c3eb4ba0cadfc2efd4e7ffa3ccc296` and the citations they carry. Nothing else was available. Where a step goes beyond a stated fact it is labelled inferred. Every item should be verified against the code and its tests before anyone acts on it.

The incident, as best the material supports it: an agent-side compromise inside the container converted into estate-wide key disclosure and settlement-ledger tampering, because the container's only boundaries were the ones the shipped configuration leaves switched off.

---

### R-1 — With `role_isolation` off, one compromised agent tool read every sovereign key in the container
- Topics: AB-36, AB-36.3, AB-36.6, AB-34.6
- Chain of events:
  1. The container is built and run as shipped: `[security].role_isolation = false` (`agentbox.toml:2227`).
  2. The entrypoint picks `/etc/supervisord.conf`; every long-running program runs as devuser, uid 1000 (`flake.nix:2947`, `flake.nix:2982`), and supervisord passes PID 1's environment to every child (AB-36 intro).
  3. `/run/secrets` is chowned `1000:1000`, mode 0700, and devuser's `nostr.key` is written "as today" (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:388`) — the sovereign identity key is a devuser-owned file.
  4. The chain producers and faucets also run as devuser and read the signer key, parent RPC credential and treasury key "straight from their at-rest paths" (`config/sidechain/run-producer.sh:70-71`, `config/sidechain/run-faucet.sh:27`, AB-34.6 off-mode).
  5. Thirty-five DEVUSER_CLASS credentials, including `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` and `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, remain ambient in every devuser process by the Q8 exception (`config/custody/env-classes.json:90`, `:92`, ADR-2122:158-159).
  6. An agent tool — or a compromised transitive dependency of anything running as devuser — reads the sovereign key, the management Bearer token, provider keys and the faucet treasury key.
  7. It signs as the estate (the forum house kinds 1/42/31923 path exists precisely for that key, `config/custody/identity-port-acl.json:40`), calls the management API with a valid Bearer, exfiltrates pod and client data through the same API, and spends provider credits.
  8. The client discovers impersonation and data exposure.
- Evidence: `agentbox.toml:2227` (flag false); `config/entrypoint-unified.sh:388` (flag-off `/run/secrets` handling); `flake.nix:2947`, `flake.nix:2982` (devuser programs); `config/custody/env-classes.json:90-92` and ADR-2122:158-159 (35 ambient credentials); AB-36 intro: "every secret in the container reached every process… a 0600 key file protected nothing from a co-resident agent"; AB-36 business: "One mistaken or compromised tool could therefore impersonate the organisation or move value."
- Warning signs visible today: ADR-2122 is `proposed`, `activation_status: inactive`, and may move only on a flag-on PASS receipt (ADR-2122:135-143); the only committed receipt is STAGED with the flag false (`docs/estate-closeout/x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:4`, `:8`); `ab_custody_revert` hands any migrated at-rest copy back to devuser whenever the flag is off (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:725`).
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high.

### R-2 — The host Docker socket stayed world-accessible; a container process reached the daemon and became host root
- Topics: AB-36.7, AB-36.1, AB-36 (intro)
- Chain of events:
  1. Earlier boots widened the host Docker socket's inode, and the host never narrowed it (`docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`, AB-36.7 Open R2).
  2. The socket is bind-mounted into the container — the material calls it "a fourth bypass" that "only closes under the flag" (AB-36 intro) — and the inode is world-accessible, so uid 1000 can write it.
  3. The same devuser foothold as R-1 speaks to the Docker daemon, which is root on the host (`config/role-accounts.json:6`).
  4. It starts a privileged container mounting the host filesystem, or execs into the agentbox container as root.
  5. From host root it reads the `agentbox-secrets` named volume (every identity, signer and treasury key at rest), the workspace bind (client pods, the payment store JSON, the block files), and can delete volumes outright.
  6. Every boundary AB-36 drew was container-internal; none of it survives this step.
- Evidence: `docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`; `config/entrypoint-unified.sh:605` (the flag-on boot can only record `degraded:docker-socket`, not repair it); `scripts/activation/role-isolation-rehearsal.host.sh:18-19` (the host half records the socket mode without gating on it) while ADR-2122:142 counts `degraded:docker-socket` as a failure; `config/role-accounts.json:6` (uid 965 reserved precisely because a role with that gid would sit in the host docker group).
- Warning signs visible today: the open R2 itself — the exposure is named, unowned by anything the container can do, and the rehearsal treats it as a recorded observation rather than a gate; the unconditional devuser-out-of-root group change (`flake.nix:3863`) shows the authors closing adjacent bypasses while this one stayed open.
- Confidence: high that the exposure exists and is reachable from the container; the specific exploitation path is inferred from the world-accessible inode plus the daemon running as host root.
- Severity: high.

### R-3 — Chain signer, treasury key and block state all sat devuser-writable; the public ledger was rewritten from inside the box
- Topics: AB-34.1, AB-34.3, AB-34.5, AB-34.6, AB-32.1, AB-32.4
- Chain of events:
  1. The signer key lives at `/var/lib/agentbox/secrets/sidestr-NAME.key`; with the flag off the producer reads it as devuser (`config/sidechain/run-producer.sh:70`, `flake.nix:2947`). Even with the flag on, the legacy workspace twins are never deleted and stay devuser-readable "until a manual purge" (ADR-2122:212-215).
  2. The faucet treasury key defaults to, and the live manifest still sets, the workspace path (`config/sidechain/run-faucet.sh:27`, `agentbox.toml:1638`).
  3. The block file and index live under WORKSPACE, a host bind (`config/sidechain/README.md:26`, `run-producer.sh:62`) — writable by the same uid.
  4. A devuser-resident process edits `blocks.dat`/`blocks.json`, or takes the signer key and announces its own kind-33333 tips to the five public relays (`run-producer.sh:79`).
  5. The mirror loop commits whatever the block directory holds to the public Pages repository within 120 s as "mirror tip N" (`mirror-sync.sh:72`, `:77-79`).
  6. Neither chain is anchored — `checkpoint_every = 0` on `dreamlab-txbt4` (`agentbox.toml:1666`), no producer writes checkpoints at all (`config/sidechain/README.md:13-18`) — and each root has a single signer (`config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:17`), so nothing on a parent chain contradicts a rewritten tip.
  7. Clients — the forum wallet reads the chain through the Pages mirror (`mirror-sync.sh:5-6`) — follow the tampered tip; DREAM balances and the receipts the seal existed to make third-party-verifiable are no longer trustworthy; the faucet is drained to attacker wallets within its own limits (`agentbox.toml:1639-1640`).
- Evidence: as cited per step above; additionally the producer override `SIDESTR_ALLOW_UNPINNED=1` runs a devuser-writable workspace checkout as consensus code with only a log line and no bound (`run-producer.sh:84-93`, AB-34.1 UN2), and `upstream-pins` already broke its own bump rule once on "acceptance" (`config/sidechain/upstream-pins:10`, commit `47e187934`).
- Warning signs visible today: every component above is already marked Tension or Debt in the material except the writable-block-file fact, which is stated plainly; the four-day producer outage after 25 September (AB-34 business) shows how young the chain's operational history is.
- Confidence: high for each component; medium for this exact composition — inferred.
- Severity: high.

---

### F-01 — https-bridge sends `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` together with `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true`
- Topics: AB-30.4
- Evidence: `https-proxy.js:208-211` sets both headers on every proxied response; the material's own note states the Fetch spec forbids the combination taking effect for credentialed requests.
- Failure: today it silently masks an intent to allow credentialed cross-origin use of the management API through the bridge (published on loopback, reachable from the operator's browser); any client or future fix that honours both — or a fix that pins an origin while keeping credentials — turns any website the operator visits into a driver of the management API. Non-credentialed cross-origin reads of unauthenticated responses are already permitted by `*`.
- Confidence: high that the headers are as stated; the exploit path is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE note).
- Severity: medium.

### F-02 — The setup server's `/api/proxy/*` attaches the management Bearer key with no caller authentication shown
- Topics: AB-30.2, AB-30.3
- Evidence: the axum router exposes `/api/config`, `/api/shutdown`, `/api/proxy/{*path}` (`setup/server/src/main.rs:222-228`); the proxy loads the mgmt key from `/var/lib/agentbox/secrets/mgmt-key`, `~/.agentbox/mgmt-key` or `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` (`main.rs:166-184`) and forwards with `Authorization: Bearer <key>`; the sequence depicts no token, origin or Host check on any route, and `POST /api/shutdown` likewise (`main.rs:81`).
- Failure: while setup runs, a webpage the operator visits — or any local process, since everything is devuser — issues requests to `127.0.0.1` candidate ports; one hit gets full management-API access with the key attached by the proxy, or kills the server. The ephemeral port narrows this but does not close it (the URL is handed to the browser at `main.rs:246`).
- Confidence: low, inferred — the absence of an auth check is from a sequence diagram that may simply not depict one; an engineer must confirm.
- Marked by authors: no.
- Severity: high.

### F-03 — Fresh installs are seeded from `agentbox.default.toml` with all learning/hygiene gates off
- Topics: AB-30.3
- Evidence: when the config file is missing, `get_config` seeds from the shipped default (`setup/server/src/main.rs:44`), which carries all learning/hygiene gates OFF (AB-30.3).
- Failure: a new client deployment starts from the most permissive baseline; the operator saves the seeded TOML largely unchanged and agents run ungated from day one, so the exfiltration-class behaviours the gates exist for are not blocked by default.
- Confidence: high that the seed is permissive; the operator-saving-unchanged step is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no.
- Severity: medium.

### F-04 — `upstream-pins` was bumped on "acceptance" rather than a produced block, and that commit is baked into the image
- Topics: AB-34.1, AB-33 (tension)
- Evidence: the file's own rule is to bump a line only after the chain has restarted and produced a block on that commit (`config/sidechain/upstream-pins:5-7`); the `blaketestnode` line was bumped 2026-10-01 as an "operator-accepted active development head" (`upstream-pins:10`, commit `47e187934`); the bake carries the same commit (`lib/sidestr-upstream.nix:57-60`).
- Failure: consensus-relevant upstream code changed on trust alone; a behaviour change or defect in that head reaches both sealed chains without either ever having produced a block on it — blocks diverge from what existing clients validate, or production fails preflight and goes FATAL after five retries.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension).
- Severity: high.

### F-05 — `SIDESTR_ALLOW_UNPINNED=1` reopens the writable-checkout exposure with no time bound
- Topics: AB-34.1
- Evidence: the override runs the workspace checkout at any commit (`run-producer.sh:85`); it logs `SIDESTR-UNPINNED` and warns but never refuses (`run-producer.sh:86-93`); the material's own DEBT: the exposure the bake closed is back and "nothing bounds how long it stays set" (AB-34.1 UN2).
- Failure: the override is left set after debugging; from then on the chain's consensus code is whatever sits in a devuser-writable directory, and a co-resident agent edits it before the next restart.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt).
- Severity: high.

### F-06 — Legacy workspace twins of spend-capable keys are copied once and never deleted
- Topics: AB-34.3, AB-34.6, AB-36.3
- Evidence: the migrate step copies the legacy file once and never deletes it, so a spend-capable key stays on the host-visible bind in both modes until a manual purge (ADR-2122:212-215); with the flag off the faucet defaults to and the live manifest still sets the workspace path (`run-faucet.sh:27`, `agentbox.toml:1638`); under the flag the at-rest copies are role-only "for every copy except the legacy workspace twins" (AB-34.6 invariant).
- Failure: role isolation is switched on and validated by the rehearsal; the treasury and signer keys remain readable by every devuser process through the twins, so the boundary the receipt proves does not cover the copies that can spend.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension).
- Severity: high.

### F-07 — The producer goes FATAL after five start retries, and its preflight depends on a LAN parent node
- Topics: AB-34.1, AB-32.4
- Evidence: `startretries` 5 then FATAL, "a failed start means a pin to fix, not a restart loop" (`flake.nix:2939-2942`, `:2952`); preflight exits 1 unless the signer key, parent RPC credential, sealed document and tree are all readable (`run-producer.sh:109-110`); the parent RPC is the LAN testnet4 node with a cookie credential (`run-producer.sh:72-74`); precedent: a container restart on 25 September stopped the chain for four days (AB-34 business).
- Failure: the LAN Bitcoin node goes down or its cookie rotates; the producer exhausts retries and stays FATAL indefinitely; the chain stalls, the mirror freezes, and clients transact against a stale tip.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism; medium, inferred, that clients act on the stale tip.
- Marked by authors: no (the FATAL policy is stated as design, not flagged).
- Severity: medium.

### F-08 — The p21/mainnet gate is a field-presence check; nothing resolves a receipt or refuses a level-1 chain on a mainnet parent
- Topics: AB-32.3, AB-31.9
- Evidence: case 3 refuses only unless a `p21Receipt` field is present, and "Resolving the receipt is NOT checked" (`tests/config/sidechain-genesis.test.sh:86`, AB-32.3); the CI resolution check, the level-1-on-mainnet prohibition and the refusing node are all unbuilt (ADR-2103:229-230); the mainnet phase may not begin until the gate exists as code (`sovereign-settlement.md:329-330`) — it does not.
- Failure: under commercial pressure a mainnet-parent chain is sealed with a placeholder receipt string; CI passes; real value moves on a chain with no regulatory gate anywhere in the enforcement path.
- Confidence: high that the gap exists; the future sealing is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt/Open).
- Severity: high.

### F-09 — Genesis commits neither parent nor containment; the on-seal binding ADR-2103 D3 claims does not exist
- Topics: AB-32.2, AB-31.1
- Evidence: upstream's `buildGenesis` commits only the id, pegs, genesisTime and signer witness (ADR-2103:206-208); the `pin:` record is unbuilt and must be an upstream proposal (ADR-2103:210-214); D3 asserts the fields are committed on-seal (ADR-2103:73-74) and the amendment concedes they are not (ADR-2103:208).
- Failure: a validator that obtains the chain document from anywhere but this git repository cannot bind the chain to its parent from chain data; a mis-set or tampered `parent` in a served document is contradicted by nothing on-chain, and peg validation follows the wrong parent.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension/Open).
- Severity: medium.

### F-10 — Level 2 would run on ephemeral kinds with an availability-tolerant round; the corrected wire profile has no kind allocation
- Topics: AB-35.2, AB-35.3, AB-31.5
- Evidence: kinds 23510-23514 are ephemeral under NIP-01, so relays need not retain them (`REVIEW-kofn-consensus-gpt6-astra.md:217`); the review requires stored kinds, single-letter routing tags, dual signatures and digest-fetched persisted certificates (ADR-2101:162-168); the registry allocates none of it (`docs/PROTOCOL-registry.md:163-173`); a single-decision-per-block design is "unsound however large its quorum" (ADR-2101:156-158); `sidestr-round` shipped "upstream's availability-tolerant round (not the reviewed BFT shape)" (AB-35 drift note, SR-01.6).
- Failure: k-of-n activation proceeds on what exists; safety-relevant certificates vanish from relays and the round tolerates the partitions the amendments were written to close — two conflicting final blocks at one height, both spent from.
- Confidence: medium — the components are high-confidence; activation is a premise.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt/Open/Drift).
- Severity: high.

### F-11 — The shipped image bakes devuser into `wheel` with a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in
- Topics: AB-36.7, AB-36.1
- Evidence: built flag-off, as shipped, devuser stays in `wheel` (`flake.nix:3864`) and a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in is baked (`config/bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`, `flake.nix:3878`); it is inert only because no setuid sudo ships (`flake.nix:3632-3636`) and `no-new-privileges` is set (`docker-compose.yml:129`); "Reverting either guard on a flag-off build turns devuser back into root" (AB-36.7 Tension).
- Failure: any later image change — packaging a setuid sudo, relaxing `no-new-privileges` to fix a tool — silently completes a pre-baked path from uid 1000 to uid 0, past every role boundary in AB-36.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension).
- Severity: high.

### F-12 — When `/run/secrets` is not its own mount, the boot records `ROLE-ISOLATION-DEGRADED` and continues
- Topics: AB-36.3
- Evidence: not a mount point → `ROLE-ISOLATION-DEGRADED secrets-mount` (`config/lib/role-custody.sh:99`); delivery "fails open with a counted, grep-able marker" (`config/lib/role-custody.sh:11-14`); the mount is declared in compose (`docker-compose.yml:110`) and the whole point of a separate mount is that devuser owns `/run` and can rename root's entries under it (AB-36.3 rationale, ADR-2122:328-335).
- Failure: the image is run via plain `docker` or a scheduler that drops the tmpfs declaration; the boot proceeds flag-on with the secrets root inside a devuser-owned `/run`, where entries can be renamed — the exact attack the mount was drawn to prevent, live, with only a log line to say so.
- Confidence: medium — degrade-and-continue is stated; the rename consequence is the reason the mount exists.
- Marked by authors: no (the fail-open is presented as rationale, not debt).
- Severity: high.

### F-13 — An image missing the roles artefact boots the flag-off shape while the TOML says the flag is on
- Topics: AB-36.2
- Evidence: `AGENTBOX_ROLE_ISOLATION` is read from `[security].role_isolation` (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:346`); when the roles config or plan is absent, `ROLE-ISOLATION-UNAVAILABLE: boot as flag off` (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:357`).
- Failure: the owner flips the flag on an old image, or an artefact drops out of the bake; the boot reverts to the all-devuser shape while the configuration asserts isolation — the R-1 posture with a dashboard claiming otherwise.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism; medium, inferred, that operators would read the flag rather than the boot marker as the truth.
- Marked by authors: no.
- Severity: high.

### F-14 — The committed activation receipt predates the rule it would now fail, and its uid numbering differs from the baked table
- Topics: AB-36.7
- Evidence: the receipt's uid table is "derived from /etc/agentbox.toml (design §2.2 numbering)" from a run at an earlier commit (`docs/estate-closeout/x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:16`, `:26`); the rehearsal at this revision requires the baked table and plan with no derived numbering (`scripts/activation/role-isolation-rehearsal.sh:41-43`); "The receipt predates the rule it would now fail."
- Failure: activation is argued from a receipt whose uids came from a different numbering than the baked accounts; the identity-port ACL is keyed by numeric uid (`config/custody/identity-port-acl.json:12`), so a mismatch either refuses every caller or grants to the wrong uid at cutover — a signing outage or an unintended grant.
- Confidence: medium.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift).
- Severity: medium.

### F-15 — Single-signer roots, zero anchoring, and a devuser-writable block file make history rewrite undetectable off-chain
- Topics: AB-34.5, AB-34.6, AB-32.1, AB-32.4
- Evidence: each root has one signer (`config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:17`); neither chain is anchored, no producer writes checkpoints (`config/sidechain/README.md:13-18`; `agentbox.toml:1666` `checkpoint_every = 0`); with the flag off, state and block file live under WORKSPACE (`run-producer.sh:62`, `config/sidechain/README.md:26`) writable by devuser; the mirror publishes the block directory every 120 s (`mirror-sync.sh:72`, `:77-79`).
