- Failure: a devuser-resident process rewrites `blocks.dat`/`blocks.json`; within two minutes the public Pages mirror commits the tampered history as "mirror tip N"; with no parent checkpoint and one signer, nothing outside the box contradicts it; clients that do not validate every header from genesis accept the new tip.
- Confidence: high for the writable-state and no-anchor facts; the client-acceptance step is inferred and depends on their validation depth.
- Marked by authors: no (each fact is stated; the combination is not drawn).
- Severity: high.

## dreamlab-ai-website / critical

### F-01 — One external server's key is simultaneously primary forum admin and governance publisher, and the split to fix it is staged, not executed
- Topics: DW-01.8, DW-03.4, DW-03.5
- Evidence: DW-01.8: "visionclaw-server is a governance publisher pubkey shared with the forum's primary admin pubkey"; DW-03.4 state diagram: "visionclaw-server (11ed6422...663c) is BOTH primary admin AND [governance].agent_pubkeys publisher". Rotation requires a four-location atomic change (dreamlab.toml:39-72 comment) — five once `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY` is included — and "the auth-worker ADMIN_PUBKEYS CF secret cannot be rotated from this repo or CI".
- Failure: compromise or leakage of that single key, held by an external system this repo does not control, yields full forum admin (moderation kinds 30910-30914 via DW-06.5, `/api/whitelist/set-admin`) plus Agent Control Surface governance publishing (kinds 31400-31405). Recovery is a slow, manual, multi-location change including a Cloudflare dashboard secret — a long attacker-retention window during incident response.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DW-03.4 "Open, staged"; legacy ADR-040 D3)

### F-02 — A plaintext secp256k1 private key is hardcoded in three committed scripts and lives in git history
- Topics: DW-07.2, DW-06.9 (D13)
- Evidence: `ADMIN_PRIVKEY_HEX` hardcoded at `seed-forum.mjs:20`, `seed-semantic.mjs:17`, `assign-cohorts.mjs:17`, directly violating the repo's own rule (`CLAUDE.md` Security Rules: "NEVER hardcode API keys, secrets, or credentials"). The material states "a plaintext private key checked into git history is a credential-hygiene defect regardless of scope" and that whether the key is still live "cannot be determined from this repo alone"; it is not among the enumerated admin/agent pubkeys (DW-03.3).
- Failure: anyone with repo read or any historical clone holds a signing identity that, if ever provisioned (whitelisted, cohort-assigned — `assign-cohorts.mjs` implies it was used to assign cohorts), still works against the live relay/workers. History retention means removal from HEAD does not remediate.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (INVARIANT VIOLATION, D13)

### F-03 — Anonymous public ingress has no anti-abuse control beyond a per-IP rate limit, and bypasses content moderation
- Topics: DW-04.5, DW-02.7, DW-04.3
- Evidence: `SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:282`: "10 events/s/IP relay limit only — no PoW/CAPTCHA/per-recipient throttle; kind 1059 bypasses content moderation". Both public surfaces (contact form DW-02.7, Talk-to-AI DW-04.3) ride this pipe; the LLM-cost mitigation is a client-side throttle plus a manual ops kill-switch `JUNKIEJARVIS_ENABLED=0` (`:284`).
- Failure: an attacker with rotating IPs floods whitelisted recipients (admins, junkiejarvis) with unmoderated kind-1059 DMs — harassment/phishing delivered as DMs — and burns LLM spend until an operator manually flips the switch. The unmoderated stored content then becomes the operator's custody problem (see F-05).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (threat register T1/T3 — acknowledged, unresolved)

### F-04 — The only backup path has never been exercised (no restore script) and omits ADMIN_KV — bans and mutes are unrecoverable
- Topics: DW-07.5, DW-03.8
- Evidence: DW-07.5: "there is no corresponding restore script, only the raw D1/R2/KV export". The KV backup coverage enumerates only SESSIONS/POD_META/SEARCH_CONFIG (`forum-backup.sh:31-35`), while ADMIN_KV is where bans/mutes live (DW-03.8: a namespace split would "silently drop bans/mutes") — ADMIN_KV appears nowhere in the coverage map. The `provision-kv` pre-job "resolves **or creates**" the namespace (`workers-deploy.yml:61`); a created one is empty.
- Failure: (a) at a real DR event, the exports turn out unrestorable or partial because restore was never rehearsed; (b) if ADMIN_KV is deleted or a future deploy's title lookup fails and silently binds a fresh empty namespace, every ban and mute evaporates with no backup copy — banned abusers return and moderators cannot reconstruct the moderation state.
- Confidence: high on the textual facts; the ADMIN_KV omission is inferred from the backup script's own coverage map (coverage outside `forum-backup.sh` cannot be ruled out from this material)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — the no-restore fact and the split-namespace failure mode are stated (as design rationale), but neither the missing restore path nor the ADMIN_KV backup gap is flagged as a risk anywhere

### F-05 — User-submitted DM content cannot be erased by its author, and erasure does not reach NAS backups for 14 nights
- Topics: DW-04.5 (T2), DW-07.5
- Evidence: `SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:283`: "erasure is operator-side D1 purge only — self-service NIP-09 deletion is cryptographically impossible (author key is a discarded throwaway)". Separately, nightly backups retain 14 nights (`KEEP_NIGHTS=14`, `forum-backup.sh`) and "kind-40/42/0 events are signed plaintext … the NAS copy is readable content" (`forum-backup.sh:16-18`).
- Failure: a data-subject erasure request over contact-form or Talk-to-AI content requires manual D1 surgery; even after a successful purge, plaintext channel content persists on NAS backups up to 14 days, and no purge-of-backups procedure is documented. This is a compliance/custody incident waiting for the first request.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes for the deletion impossibility (threat register T2); the backup-retention interaction is not marked

### F-06 — Zone encryption was shipped citing an ADR that does not exist, while the governing doc still describes the old posture
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-06.6 (I7), DW-06.9 (D15)
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml:123,137,148` sets `encrypted=true` on zone2/3/4, `:149` sets zone4 `agent_keys=true`, `:33-37` adds the `[encryption].enabled` master gate — all citing 'ADR-2016' (`dreamlab.toml:34,149`) — yet "no ADR-2016-*.md exists in docs/adr/ at HEAD — the record was never written". The governing `IDENTITY-zones.md:166-167` still asserts "only zone3 is encrypted".
- Failure: operators and auditors working from IDENTITY-zones mis-model the encrypted surface (e.g., believe zone2/minimoonoir and zone4 content is plaintext, or that the master gate affects only zone3), and a security-posture change is in production with no decision record to distinguish intended from accidental — or to reverse it from.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("BREACHED at HEAD", D15, DOC-DRIFT)

### F-07 — Seven-plus config values survive only as hand-synced mirrors, and the most security-critical mirror site is invisible to CI
- Topics: DW-03.5, DW-03.6, DW-03.7
- Evidence: seven enumerated mirror groups (2-4 sites each) per ADR-2005's accepted deferral; admin-pubkeys is 3 readable sites plus the auth-worker CF secret marked "verifiable: false" (M1). Before 2026-09-05 CI compared only one group — "a rotation that updated the TOML and missed a mirror shipped stale keys or zones silently" (`config-mirrors.mjs:1-18`); the secret is pushed only by a manual `workflow_dispatch` (`set-worker-secrets.yml`).
- Failure: an admin-key rotation updates the TOML, relay and search `[vars]`, but the operator forgets the manual auth-worker secret push — the auth-worker keeps authorizing the old key indefinitely, and no CI check can see the divergence. A demoted or compromised old key retains auth-worker admin rights.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (ADR-2005 deferred-tension framing in `config-mirrors.mjs`)

### F-08 — Search-worker admin authorization diverges from the rest of the system: D1 promotions don't reach it, D1 demotions don't either
- Topics: DW-03.10, DW-04.6
- Evidence: "the search-worker honours only its own ADMIN_PUBKEYS [vars] value — D1-promoted admins are not visible to it" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:272`), its own copy at `search-worker.wrangler.toml:38`; everywhere else admin = static ∪ D1 (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259-270`).
- Failure: a moderator promoted via `/api/whitelist/set-admin` gains relay/auth admin powers but not search-admin — inconsistent enforcement; worse in the other direction: an admin demoted in D1 (or removed after a rotation) retains full search-worker admin rights until someone remembers the static copy and redeploys.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("Known gap", SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:272)

### F-09 — Poker table economics are hand-mirrored into the deploy env and are invisible to the completeness sweep
- Topics: DW-03.6, DW-03.1
- Evidence: "`[poker]` is mirrored BY HAND into `deploy.yml` `POKER_CONFIG_JSON` (dreamlab.toml:305-308; deploy.yml:83-89) … Neither site appears in `config-mirrors.mjs`'s `MIRROR_BEARING_KEYS` (`config-mirrors.mjs:41-47`), so the DW-03.7 completeness sweep cannot see it: a `[poker]` edit that misses the deploy mirror ships stale table parameters silently."
- Failure: the operator changes DREAM house-seat table parameters in the authored TOML; the deploy ships the old JSON; the live house-seat table — a game with a named currency and agent house seats (`nostr-bbs-poker-citizen`, DW-01.2) — runs different economics than authored, with zero CI signal.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("Drift (an eighth, unenumerated mirror)")

### F-10 — Three documents cite three different stale kit pins, and the parity gate checks only the SHA, not the version
- Topics: DW-01.5, DW-06.7 (D9), DW-06.9 (D12)
- Evidence: `BASELINE-architecture.md:113` cites `a7544687…`/beta.9; `README.md:298` — "the repo's most-read file" — cites `2d693ed2…` (beta.6, "re-pinned 2026-07-21"); `kit-compatibility-record.md:347` still marks beta.10 as "Current (canonical)". Meanwhile the workflow comments say `v1.0.0-beta.14` while the crates.io pins and `CANONICAL_KIT_VERSION` say beta.15 — "pin-parity compares the SHA, so the stale version annotation does not fail the gate."
- Failure: an operator re-pins guided by the README or BASELINE and selects a beta.6/beta.9-era kit or crate version. Precedent shows pin mistakes are catastrophic: client/worker skew "wiped the forum on 2026-06-15" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:117-120`). The SHA-only gate flags cross-site mismatch, never wrong-version selection.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, self-reported at each site)

### F-11 — The agent roster in dreamlab.toml is never parsed by the deployed software — editing it does nothing
- Topics: DW-03.3
- Evidence: "`ForumConfig` does not parse the `[[agents]]` table at all" — the kit renders authorization from server-side D1 `agent_registry.registered_by` (`IDENTITY-zones.md:154-157`); `authorised_by` is authored but never rendered; IDENTITY-zones.md:89 still says "Seven agents" against ten in the TOML (`dreamlab.toml:386-405`).
- Failure: an operator revokes or edits an agent (a poker house seat, junkiejarvis) in `dreamlab.toml` believing the roster is authoritative — the deployed forum never reads it, so a "revoked" agent keeps acting while every doc in the repo claims the roster governs it. Symmetrically, a newly added agent silently lacks whatever the TOML implies.
- Confidence: high (the non-parsing is stated; the failure follows directly)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT on `authorised_by` not rendered) — though the marked framing understates it: the marked issue is display drift, the real issue is an inert authorization source

### F-12 — Talk-to-AI has no request/reply correlation and an unverified identity hint
- Topics: DW-04.3
- Evidence: "the DM transport carries no protocol-level correlation … a late answer to a timed-out question can otherwise read as the answer to whatever was asked most recently" (`AIChatFab.tsx:9-19` `lateForQuestion`; estate closeout `IDENTITY-zones.md:189`); `identityHint` is "EXPLICITLY UNVERIFIED — no proof of possession" (`AIChatFab.tsx:429-430`).
- Failure: a user asks Q1, it times out, they ask Q2; Q1's answer arrives and is presented as answering Q2 — confidently wrong guidance under the site's AI-assistant branding. Separately, any caller can assert any pubkey as `identityHint`, so the agent's "who is asking" context is spoofable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (closeout comment flags both explicitly)

### F-13 — "End-to-end encrypted" zones carry `agent_keys=true` and get their keys granted server-side at allocation
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-01.5, DW-06.8 (F6)
- Evidence: zone2/3/4 are `encrypted=true` (`dreamlab.toml:123,137,148`) and zone4 additionally sets `agent_keys=true` (`:149`); DW-01.5 records beta.15 behavior as "zone keys granted on allocation/admin sign-in"; the README markets "End-to-end encrypted DMs, NIP-59/NIP-44" (`README.md:204`).
- Failure: members of family/personal zones post under an E2EE expectation, but zone keys are distributed through server-side allocation and zone4 explicitly provisions agent keys — the operator's agents (and whoever controls allocation) sit inside the trust boundary the marketing draws around cohort peers. A privacy incident if agent-readable content is later acted on contrary to user expectation.
- Confidence: medium — the flags and grant mechanism are stated; that agents can actually decrypt zone content is inferred from `agent_keys=true` plus server-granted keys, not from a stated key-custody flow
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Zone cohort access depends on a dual-accept invariant whose violation already caused a mass silent lockout once
- Topics: DW-03.1
- Evidence: "`required_cohorts` must stay dual-accept (zone id + legacy slug) until every legacy slug grant is migrated, or locked-zone members lose access — dropping either arm collapsed legacy members to welcome-only in a 2026-07-20 regression" (`IDENTITY-zones.md:164-165`; `dreamlab.toml:116-119` comment).
- Failure: a future zones refactor "cleans up" cohorts to ids-only (or drops the legacy arm before D1 grants are migrated) and every legacy member of zone2/3/4 silently falls back to welcome-only — a mass lockout of the entire private community with no error anywhere, exactly as it happened in July.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (INVARIANT)

### F-15 — The documented Workers end-state (branded domains) is not in DNS, and the one verification is three months stale
- Topics: DW-01.3, DW-06.7 (D4)
- Evidence: the branded domains (`relay./api./pods./search./preview.dreamlab-ai.com`) "are the documented end-state but are **not provisioned in DNS** — the client instead talks to raw `*.workers.dev` hosts baked into the build env (`deploy.yml:50-54`; verified 2026-06-09 `ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED`, not re-checked as of 2026-08-31) — shipping the branded domains baked in previously severed every client API call" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:91-97`).
- Failure: anyone acting on the documented end-state — cutting DNS, or "fixing" the build env to the branded hostnames per the docs — reproduces the prior total outage of every client API call (signup, contact, forum). Meanwhile nobody actually knows the current DNS state; the only hard check predates HEAD by months.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (D4, Invariant 4 context)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Whether the hardcoded `ADMIN_PRIVKEY_HEX` (F-02) is a live identity** — needs live relay whitelist/cohort checks against the derived pubkey; the material says this cannot be determined from the repo.
2. **Whether the auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` Cloudflare secret actually equals `dreamlab.toml [admin].static_pubkeys` today** — marked `verifiable: false`; only a dashboard read or live worker probe settles it.
3. **Zone E2EE key custody** — whether "zone keys granted on allocation/admin sign-in" means the server or an admin can decrypt zone2/3/4 content; required to confirm F-13's trust-boundary claim beyond inference.
4. **Whether `/api/setup-status` `needsSetup` consults the static env admin set or only D1 `is_admin` rows** — determines whether a D1 wipe or partial restore re-opens the first-user-is-admin bootstrap in production.
5. **Whether ADMIN_KV is captured by any backup mechanism outside `forum-backup.sh`, and whether any restore drill has ever been run** — the coverage map lists only three KV namespaces and the repo contains no restore artifact; both need operational evidence.

## dreamlab-ai-website / premortem

# Post-mortem — dreamlab-ai-website / forum estate

**Incident class:** loss and suspected unauthorized administrative access to a client's forum data (locked-zone content and/or pods), unrecoverable within any acceptable window. Root cause is not yet confirmed against live systems; this document ranks the three candidate root causes the architecture material supports, then lists latent defects found during the investigation. Everything below is drawn only from the diagrams pack at `verified_commit: ebaf16c0462407ba4eb09dcc3220a1846b0d5c80`; every citation is the pack's own path:line reference and must be re-checked against the code before action.

---

### R-1 — An admin key the operator believed retired stayed live: the auth-worker's admin set is a hand-mirrored Cloudflare secret CI cannot verify, and D1-promoted admins survive any static rotation
- Topics: DW-03.4, DW-03.5, DW-03.6, DW-03.8, DW-04.6, DW-05.1
- Chain of events:
  1. The operator executes the long-staged admin/governance key split (runbook `docs/deployment/admin-key-split-runbook.md`, referenced `dreamlab.toml:54-55`).
  2. The split is a four-location atomic change — `[admin].static_pubkeys` in `dreamlab.toml`, relay `[vars].ADMIN_PUBKEYS`, search `[vars].ADMIN_PUBKEYS`, and the auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` Cloudflare secret — extended to five by the `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY` client re-point (`deploy.yml:61-66`).
  3. The first three sites are CI-comparable; the secret is not — it is `verifiable: false` (DW-03.5, A4) and is pushed only by `set-worker-secrets.yml`, a `workflow_dispatch`-only workflow (`set-worker-secrets.yml:12-13`) that is not part of any deploy pipeline. The deploy-time check validates the secret is *set*, not that it is *current* (`workers-deploy.yml:288`).
  4. The secret push is missed or run with the old value. The auth-worker keeps honouring the old admin set — including the shared `visionclaw-server` key the split was meant to remove.
  5. The holder of the old key authenticates to the auth-worker HTTP API (NIP-98 per `docs/security/AUTHENTICATION.md:200`) and calls `/api/whitelist/set-admin` to promote pubkeys into D1 (`whitelist.is_admin`).
  6. Admin resolution is `static ∪ D1` (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259`) — so the promoted principal holds admin on relay/auth **even after a fully successful static rotation**, because rotating the static set clears no D1 rows. The last-admin demotion block (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:270`) prevents trivially evicting them.
  7. With relay+auth admin, the principal exercises moderation powers (kinds 30910-30914, `hidden_events`, `moderation_actions` — DW-03.11, DW-06.5) against the client's zone content: hiding, deleting, reconfiguring. E2EE may keep DM *content* unreadable, but destruction of zone data is fully available.
- Evidence:
  - "the auth-worker ADMIN_PUBKEYS CF secret cannot be rotated from this repo or CI" (DW-03.4 stateDiagram note; `dreamlab.toml:39-72` comment).
  - A4: "auth-worker CF secret ADMIN_PUBKEYS, verifiable: false, pushed by set-worker-secrets.yml" (DW-03.5).
  - "a rotation that updated the TOML and missed a mirror shipped stale keys or zones silently"; before 2026-09-05 CI compared only one of the mirror groups (`config-mirrors.mjs:1-18`).
  - Admin = static ∪ D1, promotion endpoint, last-admin block, bootstrap (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259-270`, DW-04.6).
  - The mirror set has a *known live hole today*: the poker config mirror is unenumerated and invisible to the completeness sweep (DW-03.6) — the sweep's guarantee holds only for its six enumerated key names (`config-mirrors.mjs:41-47`).
- Warning signs visible today: the split sits open as "staged not applied" (drift register D7); `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY` is explicitly INTERIM (`deploy.yml:61-66`); the 2026-07-20 cohort regression (DW-03.1) already demonstrated that a hand-edit to identity config silently broke access; the eighth-mirror drift shows new governed values keep escaping the checker.
- Confidence: high — every mechanism above is stated in the material; only the claim that this specific chain fired in the incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Compromise of the unsplit `visionclaw-server` key: one external-server key is simultaneously the forum's primary admin and the agent-governance publisher, and eviction is manual and multi-site
- Topics: DW-01.8, DW-03.3, DW-03.4, DW-06.8
- Chain of events:
  1. `visionclaw-server` (`11ed6422...663c`) holds **both** roles: forum primary admin and `[governance].agent_pubkeys` publisher — "SAME key, two roles" (DW-03.3, DW-03.4).
  2. The key's private half is operated by/on an external server (VisionClaw, EXTERNAL — DW-01.8); forum primary-admin key custody is therefore outsourced outside this repo's controls. This repo only authors the roster it publishes (`forum-config/dreamlab.toml:264,275-277`).
  3. That server (or key handling on it) is compromised — mechanism outside this material.
  4. The attacker is primary admin (all powers of R-1 step 7) **and** can publish governance events kinds 31400-31405, the Agent Control Surface that drives the ten agents' authorized behavior (`README.md:205`, DW-06.8 F7) — including agents with zone keys (`zone4 agent_keys=true`, `dreamlab.toml:149`) and house-seat poker agents.
  5. Eviction requires executing the staged split across four/five locations, one of which (the CF secret) is manual, `workflow_dispatch`-only and unverifiable (R-1) — the attacker retains admin throughout the remediation window.
- Evidence: DW-03.4 diagram and note ("Held deliberately"); DW-01.8 ("governance publisher pubkey shared with the forum's primary admin pubkey"); DW-03.3 VC trust-domain node; DW-06.8 F7 ("this repo authors agent_pubkeys", kit-implemented surface).
- Warning signs visible today: drift register D7 ("Admin/governance key unsplit, staged not applied") has been an open item since before this pack's stamp; `dreamlab.toml:39-72` documents the deliberate hold; no expiry or canary on the shared key is described anywhere.
- Confidence: medium — the dual-role key defect is stated with certainty; that the VisionClaw side was the entry point is inferred (the material says nothing about that server's exposure).
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Data loss made permanent by recovery: the estate's only backup path has no restore procedure, its enumerated scope omits Durable Object state, and an auth-D1 restored empty hands admin to the first signer-up
- Topics: DW-03.10, DW-03.11, DW-04.6, DW-07.5
- Chain of events:
  1. A data-loss event hits the Cloudflare estate (relay/auth D1, R2, KV, or the relay Durable Object) — cause outside this material.
  2. The operator reaches for the only backup path that exists: the nightly export (`forum-backup.sh`, 03:10 UTC, `KEEP_NIGHTS=14`) — but "there is no corresponding restore script, only the raw D1/R2/KV export" (DW-07.5).
  3. The backup's enumerated scope is D1 (`dreamlab-relay`, `dreamlab-auth`), R2 (`pods`, `vectors`), KV (`SESSIONS/POD_META/SEARCH_CONFIG`) — Durable Objects appear nowhere in it, while `NostrRelayDO` "manages its own SQLite schema at runtime" (DW-03.11) and owns the relay's RELAY DO binding (DW-03.10). Any event state resident in the DO is unbacked and unrecoverable (this exclusion is inferred — the material names D1 `dreamlab-relay` "canonical" but never states what the DO holds or that it is exported).
  4. The improvised restore brings `dreamlab-auth` D1 back empty or partial: `GET /api/setup-status` reports `needsSetup` and **the first user to sign up becomes admin** (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259`) — a bootstrap takeover by whoever registers first on a live domain.
  5. Even a "successful" D1 restore loses DO-resident state and mismatches KV session/replay tables; the client's zone content and/or pods are gone, and the NAS copies (`/mnt/dell/shared/backups/dreamlab-forum`) have no tested path back into Cloudflare.
- Evidence: DW-07.5 ("no corresponding restore script, only the raw D1/R2/KV export"; scope `forum-backup.sh:26-35`; retention `KEEP_NIGHTS=14`); DW-04.6 bootstrap; DW-03.10 `R_DO`; DW-03.11 DO runtime schema; DW-07.5's own note that the NAS copy holds readable plaintext events (`forum-backup.sh:16-18`).
- Warning signs visible today: the estate already suffered a total forum wipe once from a deploy-side mistake ("wiped the forum on 2026-06-15", `BASELINE-architecture.md:117-120`); the backup design documents retention but no restore test, RTO, or DO coverage anywhere in the pack.
- Confidence: medium — the missing restore script and the bootstrap mechanism are stated; the DO-scope gap and the empty-restore path are inferred.
- Severity: high

---

## Latent defects found during investigation

### F-01 — Kit re-pin can still race client against workers: the 2026-06-15-wipe mitigation is trigger-based, with no stated ordering between the two deploy pipelines
- Topics: DW-01.5, DW-01.6, DW-05.3
- Evidence: the wipe precedent (`BASELINE-architecture.md:117-120`); the fix is that `workers-deploy.yml` *also* fires on `forum-config/Cargo.lock` and `KIT_REF` changes (DW-01.5). No dependency, ordering, or cross-check between `deploy.yml` and `workers-deploy.yml` is asserted anywhere; `workers-deploy.yml` additionally gates on KV provisioning and secret validation (DW-03.8) that can fail independently of the site pipeline.
- Failure: a re-pin lands; the site publishes the new client while the workers deploy is still running (or has failed on secret/KV validation with nothing in the site pipeline noticing); the new client runs against old workers — the exact skew class that wiped the forum.
- Confidence: medium — mitigation stated; the residual race is inferred (concurrency between the workflows is unstated, so not asserted either way).
- Marked by authors: no (the original incident and its trigger fix are documented; the residual hole is not flagged).
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Pin annotations and History rot freely because pin-parity compares only the SHA, and the canonical record is self-authored
- Topics: DW-01.5, DW-05.4, DW-05.8, DW-06.9 (D9, D12)
- Evidence: pin-site comments say `v1.0.0-beta.14` (`deploy.yml:113`, `rust-ci.yml:19`, `workers-deploy.yml:40`) while crates and the record say `beta.15` (`forum-config/Cargo.toml:49-52`, `kit-compatibility-record.md:31`); `README.md:298` says beta.6; `BASELINE-architecture.md:110,113` says beta.9; the History table still marks beta.10 "Current (canonical)" (`kit-compatibility-record.md:347`); "pin-parity compares the SHA, so the stale version annotation does not fail the gate."
- Failure: an operator re-pins guided by README or the History table instead of `CANONICAL_KIT_SHA`; because the record is the machine-readable source of truth and is itself edited by hand, "restoring parity" can mean editing the record to match the wrong pin rather than reverting it.
- Confidence: high (all facts stated).
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift / DOC-DRIFT, D9 and D12).
- Severity: low

### F-03 — search-worker's admin set is its own static copy; D1-promoted admins are invisible to it, so the five workers enforce divergent admin truths
- Topics: DW-03.10, DW-03.5 (M1), DW-04.6
- Evidence: "D1-promoted admins are not visible to it" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:272`); `search-worker.wrangler.toml:38` carries its own `[vars].ADMIN_PUBKEYS`, not read from `ADMIN_KV`/`ADMIN_KV_RO` (DW-03.10); other workers resolve `static ∪ D1` (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259`).
- Failure: a legitimately promoted moderator's actions are denied on search while succeeding on relay/auth; conversely, mid-rotation, a key dropped from static but live via D1 remains admin on relay/auth while search already dropped it — inconsistent enforcement across the moderation surface.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (flagged as "Known gap").
- Severity: medium

### F-04 — Client PII (contact form, Talk-to-AI) cannot be deleted by its subject, and is retained in D1 plus 14 nights of plaintext-bearing NAS backup
- Topics: DW-02.7, DW-04.5 (T2), DW-07.5
- Evidence: "self-service NIP-09 deletion is cryptographically impossible (author key is a discarded throwaway)"; erasure is "operator-side D1 purge only" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:283`); the contact form ships validated PII into gift-wrap DMs (`src/pages/Contact.tsx:395-409`); nightly NAS export retains `KEEP_NIGHTS=14` and holds readable content for plaintext kinds (`forum-backup.sh:16-18`); with no restore script there is correspondingly no selective-delete-from-backup procedure.
- Failure: a data-subject deletion request arrives; D1 is purged but the NAS copies retain the data for up to 14 more nights with no documented removal path; the ephemeral author keys are long discarded, so the ciphertext cannot even be re-identified for targeted deletion. Regulatory/client-loss exposure.
- Confidence: high (mechanisms stated; the compliance failure is inferred from them).
- Marked by authors: yes (Threat register T2; backup content-sensitivity note).
- Severity: high

### F-05 — A plaintext secp256k1 private key is hardcoded in three root-level seeders and is permanent git history
- Topics: DW-07.2, DW-06.9 (D13)
- Evidence: `ADMIN_PRIVKEY_HEX` hardcoded at `scripts/seed-forum.mjs:20`, `scripts/seed-semantic.mjs:17`, `scripts/assign-cohorts.mjs:17`, violating `CLAUDE.md`'s own Security Rules ("NEVER hardcode…"); the material states the key is not among `[admin]`/`[[agents]]` pubkeys and that its liveness cannot be determined from this repo; the same files import from an unresolvable absolute path (`/home/devuser/workspace/project2/...`).
- Failure: anyone with repo read holds a signing identity whose relay/D1 privileges, if ever granted, persist independent of any static-key rotation (see R-1 step 6); the key can NIP-42-AUTH as itself and read its own inbox wherever it is whitelisted.
- Confidence: high (the key's presence is stated; its privileges are unknown).
- Marked by authors: yes (INVARIANT VIOLATION; D13).
- Severity: medium

### F-06 — Interim identity reuse: the public site's contact-DM recipient is the operator's full admin key, and M5 checks set membership, not key liveness
- Topics: DW-03.4, DW-03.5 (M5), DW-03.6, DW-02.7, DW-06.9 (D7)
- Evidence: `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY` is marked INTERIM — "the operator's current working admin key, already relay-whitelisted so signup DMs deliver" (`deploy.yml:61-66`); whoever reads contact enquiries must therefore hold the primary admin private key; the mirror check M5 verifies only that `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY` is a *member* of `[admin].static_pubkeys` (DW-03.6), never that the baked key is currently held or is the intended recipient.
- Failure: (a) any device/process that reads the marketing contact inbox possesses the forum's primary admin key — inbox compromise escalates directly to the R-1/R-2 admin powers; (b) if the split re-points the client mirror to a key nobody controls while keeping it a set member, every enquiry is gift-wrapped to an unreadable key while the relay returns OK-true and the UI toasts success (`src/pages/Contact.tsx:389-391, 411-417`).
- Confidence: medium — inferred (the interim sharing is stated; both failure consequences are reasoned beyond the material).
- Marked by authors: yes (Open; D7; INTERIM note).
- Severity: medium

### F-07 — Talk-to-AI replies depend on two third-party open relays, and request/reply correlation does not exist
- Topics: DW-04.3, DW-06.6 (I8)
- Evidence: the primary relay's whitelist gate rejects gift wraps addressed to the ephemeral session key, so replies are "readable only from open relays" (`relay.damus.io`, `relay.primal.net`); the invariant requires the reply set to remain a subset of the agent's publish fan-out or "replies are never seen" (`IDENTITY-zones.md:168-169`); "the DM transport carries no protocol-level correlation" — a late answer can render as the answer to a different, later question (`ChatMessage.lateForQuestion`, `AIChatFab.tsx:9-19`; `IDENTITY-zones.md:189`); `identityHint` is explicitly unverified (`AIChatFab.tsx:429-430`).
- Failure: the agent's fan-out changes (or the open relays prune history) and all replies silently vanish; independently, a client acts on a misattributed answer — a correctness failure on a client-facing AI surface.
- Confidence: high (all stated).
- Marked by authors: yes (Invariant I8 + explicit closeout note).
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — Anonymous ingress: kind-1059 bypasses content moderation and throttling is IP-rate plus client-side only
- Topics: DW-04.5 (T1, T3)
- Evidence: "10 events/s/IP relay limit only — no PoW/CAPTCHA/per-recipient throttle; kind 1059 bypasses content moderation" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:282`); LLM-cost controls are serialised sends, a client-side throttle, and an ops kill-switch `JUNKIEJARVIS_ENABLED=0` (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:284`).
- Failure: a scripted poster rotating IPs floods encrypted gift wraps into relay D1 — storage growth, backup bloat, and agent spend — while moderation cannot see the content and the client-side throttle is bypassable by construction.
- Confidence: high (limits stated); exploitation path inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Threat register T1/T3).
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — Poker parameters are an eighth, unenumerated hand-synced mirror the completeness sweep cannot see
- Topics: DW-03.6, DW-03.7, DW-01.6
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml [poker]` is "mirrored BY HAND into deploy.yml POKER_CONFIG_JSON" (`dreamlab.toml:305-308` comment; `deploy.yml:83-89`, injected at `:274,346`); neither site appears in `MIRROR_BEARING_KEYS` (`config-mirrors.mjs:41-47`), so the sweep's guarantee "holds only for the six keys it enumerates"; the authors state outright that a missed mirror "ships stale table parameters silently."
- Failure: the DREAM table (house seat `nostr-bbs-poker-citizen`) runs with client-side parameters that diverge from the server-authored toml — stakes, chains (`SIDESTR_CHAINS_JSON`, `deploy.yml:82`), or table behavior wrong for players. Today the wallet surface is gated to testnet4 (`deploy.yml:77`), which bounds the blast radius; if that gate flips, this becomes a value-handling defect.
- Confidence: high (the drift and the sweep blindness are both stated).
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift — "an eighth, unenumerated mirror").
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — Runtime config reaches forum/BBS clients via sed-injected inline JSON, and no validation of the injected payload is stated
- Topics: DW-01.2, DW-01.6, DW-06.6 (I3)
- Evidence: `window.__ENV__` is injected by `sed` at deploy time (`BASELINE-architecture.md:66-70`; `deploy.yml` "Inject runtime env config"); payloads now include `ZONE_CONFIG_JSON`, `SIDESTR_CHAINS_JSON`, `POKER_CONFIG_JSON` (`deploy.yml:77-94`); the stated validation invariant covers endpoints only ("validate every endpoint", I3, `check-effective-endpoints.mjs`) — nothing in the material says the injected JSON is parsed or verified after injection.
- Failure: a value containing characters that break the sed replacement or the inline script yields a truncated or absent `window.__ENV__` on one client; zone rendering, the encryption master gate, or poker config silently default. The 2026-06-09 `ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED` endpoint incident (`deploy.yml:50-54`) shows this env-baking class has already fired once.
- Confidence: inferred (the sed mechanism is stated; the fragility and the absence of payload validation are reasoned, not documented).
- Marked by authors: no.
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — Clients bake raw `*.workers.dev` endpoints while the branded domains remain undeployed DNS fiction
- Topics: DW-01.3, DW-06.7 (D4), DW-06.6 (I4)
- Evidence: branded worker domains "are the documented end-state but are **not provisioned in DNS**"; clients talk to raw `*.workers.dev` hosts baked into the build env (`deploy.yml:50-54`; `ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED` verified 2026-06-09); shipping the branded domains baked in previously "severed every client API call" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:91-97`); Invariant 4 keeps GitHub Pages as origin until a DNS re-cut (`BASELINE-architecture.md:169-171`).
- Failure: someone "completes" the documented end-state by flipping the baked endpoints to branded domains without the DNS cut (the docs invite exactly this), or a workers.dev subdomain is retired/renamed — every client API call fails again. `check-effective-endpoints.mjs` validates the repo's configured endpoints, not live DNS resolution.
- Confidence: high (precedent stated); recurrence inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (D4).
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — The shared ADMIN_KV namespace is enforced only by a deploy-time title lookup; a provisioning change silently drops bans and mutes on the pod worker
- Topics: DW-03.8, DW-03.9
- Evidence: `provision-kv` "resolves or creates a SINGLE shared dreamlab-admin-kv namespace" so the auth-worker's read/write and pod-worker's read-only bindings share it — "a per-worker title would split it and silently drop bans/mutes" (`workers-deploy.yml:61` context, DW-03.8); both bindings ship the `REPLACE_WITH_NEW_ADMIN_KV_ID` placeholder resolved only at deploy time.
- Failure: a namespace rename, deletion, or lookup miss causes "creates" to mint a fresh empty namespace that the pod-worker binds read-only — banned members regain pod/R2 access with no error raised anywhere.
- Confidence: medium (the hazard is stated; the trigger requires a provisioning change).
- Marked by authors: yes (called out explicitly in DW-03.8).
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — Zone encryption shipped citing ADR-2016, which was never written; governing invariant I7 is breached at HEAD and zone4 agent-key custody has no written procedure
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-06.6 (I7), DW-06.9 (D15)
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml:34,149` cite 'ADR-2016' but "no ADR-2016-*.md exists in docs/adr/ at HEAD — the record was never written"; zone2/3/4 all carry `encrypted = true` (`dreamlab.toml:123,137,148`) against `IDENTITY-zones.md:166-167`'s "only zone3 is encrypted"; the new `[encryption].enabled` master gate (`dreamlab.toml:33-37`) is itself a three-site hand-mirrored flag (M7).
- Failure: key management for the encrypted zones — including `zone4`'s `agent_keys = true`, i.e. automated key custody — has no authoritative procedure; on key loss, member onboarding failure, or a gate flip, there is no documented recovery path, and the change-process discipline ADRs exist to enforce was bypassed for the estate's most custody-sensitive feature.
- Confidence: high (all facts stated).
- Marked by authors: yes (BREACHED; D15).
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Several controls are advisory by construction: npm audit continues on error, kit clippy never blocks, and the only CORS smoke test is manual
- Topics: DW-05.1, DW-05.2
- Evidence: `node-audit` runs with `continue-on-error` (ci.yml); kit clippy is "advisory: warnings reported, not enforced" (`rust-ci.yml:40-63`) on a manual-only dispatch (`rust-ci.yml:8-9`); Playwright is "deliberately NOT wired into" any gate (`CLAUDE.md:63-64`), and `tests/forum-smoke.spec.ts:404-410` is "the only spec asserting workers return scoped CORS."
- Failure: a dependency advisory or a CORS-widening regression in worker code ships green — the one assertion that would catch the CORS regression never runs in CI, and cross-origin reads of authenticated endpoints become possible.
- Confidence: high (facts stated); the exploit is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no (each is documented as a deliberate choice, not flagged as a risk).
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — Content generators fail soft: a moved directory or malformed YAML silently blanks live site sections
- Topics: DW-06.1, DW-02.3
- Evidence: a missing `public/data/workshops/` writes an **empty** `workshop-list.json` instead of failing the build (`generate-workshop-list.mjs:97-99`); a YAML parse error writes an empty `testimonials.json` (`generate-testimonials.mjs:22-25`).
- Failure: a content refactor of the class already documented in this estate (the `friends`→`minimoonoir` rename that orphaned channels, DW-07.3) breaks an input path; the build stays green and production ships empty workshop/testimonial indexes with no signal to anyone.
- Confidence: high (stated behavior); occurrence inferred.
- Marked by authors: no (described as design, not flagged as a defect).
- Severity: low

---

**Verification note for the reviewing engineer:** R-1's steps 3–6, R-3's step 4, and F-01/F-06/F-10/F-11's failure paths are the items most dependent on reading the actual workflow files (`set-worker-secrets.yml`, `workers-deploy.yml`, `deploy.yml` injection steps) and the workers' `admin.rs` — the pack asserts the mechanisms but not the pipeline concurrency or the secret-check semantics, and those are where this post-mortem's ranking would first be falsified.

## estate / critical

### F-01 — Shipped compose defaults produce demo-open (anonymous API reads) and a standalone Cloudflare tunnel can front it publicly with no local gate
- Topics: ES-10.3, ES-10.3 (CFT, LANGAP)
- Evidence: Code defaults are fail-closed (`RBAC_PUBLIC_READS` `unwrap_or(false)` at `rbac_gate.rs:126-132`; `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS` absent refuses boot at `project/src/main.rs:795-804`). The shipped `docker-compose.unified.yml` inverts both: `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS: ${RBAC_PUBLIC_READS:-1}` (line 106) and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS: ${RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS:-1}` (line 107). The standalone `docker-compose.cloudflared.yml` fronts "WHATEVER profile the backend booted" (`docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:3-8`) and the route is "a Cloudflare dashboard Public Hostname — no local config.yml, so the exposed mapping is NOT reviewable from this repo" (`docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:5-6`). ADR-2119 "leaves this standalone file unaffected" (`ADR-2119:64-66`), so `VISIONCLAW_INGRESS=lan` does not stop a hand-started standalone tunnel.
- Failure: Operator launches with shipped defaults (demo-open) and separately starts the standalone cloudflared for remote access. Anonymous `/api` reads now reach the public internet through a tunnel whose route mapping exists only in a Cloudflare dashboard, invisible to the repo's port-audit gate (which only sweeps published ports).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially (DRIFT1, LANGAP noted separately, but the *combination* of demo-open default + unreviewable standalone tunnel + gap in the port audit is not assembled as one risk)

### F-02 — Agent-event authentication defaults to OFF in production
- Topics: ES-02.1
- Evidence: The first `alt` branch is `AGENTBOX_AGENT_EVENT_AUTH=off (default)` returning `{ok:true, did:null}` (`agentbox/management-api/lib/agent-event-auth.js:108`). No marker, no DIVERGENCE, no TENSION. The NIP-98 gate that the rest of the identity mesh relies on is opt-in, not opt-out.
- Failure: Any process on the network that can reach the management-api can emit forged agent events (actions, beams, provenance records) attributed to any `source_agent_id` and `target_node_id` without presenting any credential. The downstream identity-attribution reconciliation (`reconcileSourceUrn`) is skipped because `did` is null.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — deleteAgentMemory never reaches RuVector — the embedding row persists and stays semantically searchable after pod deletion
- Topics: ES-07.9 (D5), ES-08.9
- Evidence: `deleteAgentMemory` in `agentMemory.ts:196-204` has "no reverse-tombstone dispatch to mcp claude-flow memory_delete or equivalent." `docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md` calls this "the largest erasure hole." No point-in-time RuVector backup exists, "no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO."
- Failure: A data subject requests erasure. The pod resource is deleted (HTTP 204) but the 384-dim embedding vector and its associated text remain in `ruvector-postgres`, indexed by HNSW, and return in every semantic search on that namespace indefinitely. There is no mechanism to find which row corresponds to a deleted pod resource, and no backup to restore from.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in ES-07.9 D5 and ES-08.9)

### F-04 — Raw email access "capability gate" checks a PUBLIC key passed as a tool argument — any agent with the bearer token can bypass the privacy filter
- Topics: ES-06.3
- Evidence: "The nostr_pubkey ARGUMENT is checked against the server allow-list" (`agentbox/skills/email-search/SKILL.md`). The material then states: "A Nostr PUBLIC key is the publishable half, an identity/capability token, not a secret. Passing it as a tool argument is the design, not a leak." The operator's did:nostr identity is used throughout the estate and is by definition public. The only real gate is the bearer token (tier 1), which every agent in the container needs to reach the gateway at all.
- Failure: Any agent session with the gateway bearer token reads the operator's did:nostr pubkey from the environment (`AGENTBOX_X_ONLY_PUBKEY_HEX`), passes it as the `nostr_pubkey` argument to `fetch_email_raw`, and receives raw mail with full headers, sender, subject and text — the egress filter is bypassed. The "tier 2 capability" is knowledge of a public string.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (presented as INVARIANT / correct design)

### F-05 — Three independent sats ledgers, none synced, one deposit path returns 501
- Topics: ES-03.12, ES-01.8
- Evidence: "the estate runs THREE independent did:nostr-keyed sats ledgers and NONE of them is synced with the others" (`agentbox/docs/proposals/sovereign-settlement.md:61`). The host `FsPaymentStore` deposit handler "returns 501 so no value ever entered it" (`ADR-2111:22-25`). Version pins skew: the forum record reads solid-pod-rs `0.4.0-alpha.15` while the forum resolves `0.5.0-alpha.7`, and "no gate compares them" (`ADR-2012:28-30`).
- Failure: A user is credited on one ledger and debited on another; the three balances disagree. The host ledger has never received a deposit but its API implies it is a store of record. An audit cannot reconcile because there is no shared transaction log.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in ES-03.12)

### F-06 — No point-in-time backup for the pod store; no cross-store consistent restore; no declared RPO or RTO
- Topics: ES-08.8
- Evidence: "no point-in-time backup for the pod store, scripts backup-sqlite.sh covers SQLite only" (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md` Known divergences). "no cross-store consistent restore, no declared RPO or RTO."
- Failure: The pod filesystem store (FsBackend) is corrupted or accidentally deleted. There is no backup to restore from. All user preferences, ontology contributions, proposals, annotations and inbox data are lost. The SQLite backup does not cover this store.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in ES-08.8)

### F-07 — Replay cache full under load refuses ALL new authentication — a production DoS vector presented as an invariant
- Topics: ES-04.3
- Evidence: "on a FULL cache the code REFUSES new auth (ReplayCacheFull) and must NOT evict the oldest live entry" (`nip98.rs:189-197`). The prune threshold is 4096 entries (`nip98.rs:183`), TTL is 120 seconds (`nip98.rs:178`). The authors frame this as "Bounded memory beats availability" — an INVARIANT, not a defect.
- Failure: Under sustained legitimate load (approximately 35 NIP-98 authentications per second across the estate — multiple agents, multiple MCP clients), the 4096-entry cache fills within its 120-second TTL window and every new authentication is rejected with `ReplayCacheFull`. All NIP-98-gated surfaces (agent events, MCP relays, pod writes, governance) become unavailable simultaneously. Recovery requires load to drop below the fill rate for 120 seconds.
- Confidence: medium (the fill rate required is inferred from the stated constants; whether production traffic reaches it is not shown)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (presented as a deliberate INVARIANT)

### F-08 — No rationale gate fires on any VisionClaw governance case — human decisions bypass rationale enforcement entirely
- Topics: ES-05.12, ES-05.13
- Evidence: "VisionClaw's 31400 carries only a d tag and a PanelDefinition with no tier or task-property field" (`events.rs:211-217`, `events.rs:91-101`). "the relay folds every VisionClaw case to the advertised default, medium, and check_rationale only bites at high or critical: NEITHER rationale gate fires on a VisionClaw case" (`nostr-bbs-core/src/governance.rs:525`, `wrangler.toml:48`, `governance.rs:769`). The host gate sees `None` (`enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:391-392`).
- Failure: A human operator approves a destructive or irreversible action on a VisionClaw panel with zero reasoning text. Neither the host's declared-tier gate nor the relay's effective-tier gate fires, because the case folds to medium. The decision is stored and dispatched without any rationale, violating the augmentation condition that "no surface may fabricate a human's rationale" and "absence renders as absence."
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN in ES-05.12)

### F-09 — Agent events are silently lost during WS disconnection — no replay or catch-up mechanism
- Topics: ES-02.1, ES-02.2
- Evidence: The publisher pushes events to subscribers synchronously via callback (`agent-event-publisher.js:146-149`). The WS subscriber forwards via `ws.send(JSON.stringify(event))` (`agent-event-ws-subscriber.js:144-151`). On disconnect, reconnect backoff goes up to 30 seconds (`agent-event-ws-subscriber.js:27-28,133-136`). The `eventBuffer` holds max 1000 events but the material describes no mechanism for a reconnected subscriber to replay from it. The subscriber's callback fires regardless of connection state.
- Failure: The WS connection between agentbox and VisionClaw drops for 10 seconds (network blip, container restart). During that window every agent action emitted is lost — no beam renders, no provenance recorded, no CTC canary. There is no log of the loss and no way to recover the events.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the material shows no replay mechanism but does not explicitly state events are dropped)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — MCP hub is a boot-order single point of failure for eight MCP servers
- Topics: ES-01.7
- Evidence: "Eight servers ride the hub" (`agentbox.toml:1329-1332`). The hub waits up to 120 seconds for the config projection (`hub/mod.rs:297`), then either starts or parks FATAL (`flake.nix:3140-3142`). "this one program is the first check when several MCP servers refuse connections at once."
- Failure: The bootstrap program fails to project the config (disk full, race condition, misconfiguration). The hub waits 120 seconds, then parks FATAL. All eight MCP servers — including memory, task management and orchestration — become unavailable simultaneously. Every agent session in the estate loses these capabilities. The FATAL park requires manual intervention (`autorestart=unexpected`).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (the SPOF is noted in passing but not marked as a TENSION or DEBT)

### F-11 — Black-hole hang: retired model host address silently stalls every reasoning call while health stays green
- Topics: ES-06.7
- Evidence: "the old model host is dead… never target — black-holes every synthesis while GET /health still answers" (`ADR-2023-loom-facade.md:42`, `SKILL.md:99,217`). "FINGERPRINT is exactly that split — health GREEN, all reasoning calls stall." The 180-second timeout is "the signature of a stale reasoning route, NOT a dead container."
- Failure: `REASONER_BASE_URL` is configured with the retired address. The gateway container starts, `/health` returns 200, the operator sees a healthy system. Every `ask_email` and `refresh_inbox` call stalls for 180 seconds then times out. No error surfaces until the operator correlates the timeout pattern. The fix requires changing the route, not restarting the container — but the diagnosis is non-obvious because health is green.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TRAP / DIVERGENCE in ES-06.7)

### F-12 — solid-pod-rs version pins skew across three consumers with no gate comparing them
- Topics: ES-01.1, ES-03.12
- Evidence: VisionClaw compiles crates.io `=0.5.0-alpha.12` (`Cargo.toml:224`). Agentbox Nix pins `v0.5.0-alpha.12` from `rev d64131b3` (`solid-pod-rs.nix:59,62`). The forum record reads the host at `0.4.0-alpha.15` while the forum resolves `0.5.0-alpha.7` (`ADR-2012:28-30`). "no gate compares them."
- Failure: A wire-format or behavioural change between `alpha.7`, `alpha.12` and `alpha.15` causes the forum's pod-worker to write data that the host's embedded pod cannot read, or vice versa. No CI check catches this because no gate compares the three pins. The skew is invisible until a cross-repo pod operation fails at runtime.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT in ES-01.1, DIVERGENCE in ES-03.12 SKEW)

### F-13 — Dream engine governance panel has no signing path under role_isolation — the feature that enables the flag disables the panel
- Topics: ES-05.15
- Evidence: The identity port "can never be granted [kinds 31400-31405] to any caller, and an ACL that tries does not load" (`identity_port/acl.rs:71-73`). The dream engine "is a named W3b consumer of the port" (`ADR-2122:224-225`) but "the kinds its governance panel publishes are exactly the ones the port refuses, so under the flag the dream panel has no signing path at this revision."
- Failure: Operator enables `role_isolation` for security hardening. The dream engine's governance panel (31400 panel and 31402 cases, signed as JunkieJarvis) can no longer publish. The nightly dream cycle's governance outputs silently stop appearing on the relay. No error is raised at flag-enable time; the failure is discovered when governance records are missing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION in ES-05.15)

### F-14 — Pod writes from LAN clients may be silently refused because nginx drops the port from the Host header on /solid/ and /pods/
- Topics: ES-09.9 (XFHOPEN)
- Evidence: The pod NIP-98 check "rebuilds its URL from X-Forwarded-Host" (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:207-222`), but "the /solid/ and /pods/ blocks still forward host without the port" (`nginx.dev.conf:174,200`, `nginx.production.conf:177,222`). "Whether a LAN client signing host:3001 for a pod write is refused is untested."
- Failure: A LAN client (e.g., a browser at `http://host:3001/solid/...`) signs a NIP-98 event with the URL including port 3001. The nginx `/solid/` block forwards `Host: host` (no port). The backend rebuilds the URL without the port. The signed URL does not match the rebuilt URL, `UrlMismatch` is returned, and the pod write is refused with 401. Every pod operation from LAN clients fails with a confusing auth error.
- Confidence: medium (the material says "untested" — this is a plausible failure inferred from the stated forwarding behaviour)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN in ES-09.9)

### F-15 — Adapter contract versions frozen at 1.0.0 despite live churn — consumers cannot detect breaking changes
- Topics: ES-03.9
- Evidence: "pods, memory, events and orchestrator all still declare 1.0.0 despite live churn, so a breaking change would need a MAJOR bump that has NOT happened. A consumer cannot tell from the version whether the contract it compiled against still holds" (`agentbox/docs/BASELINE-container.md`).
- Failure: A adapter implementation changes its wire format or error semantics. The contract version stays 1.0.0. A consumer (e.g., a management-api route or an external integration) compiled against the old contract continues calling with old assumptions. The mismatch manifests as runtime data corruption or silent misinterpretation, not as a version-detection error at startup.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in ES-03.9)

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual production traffic rates for the replay cache**: whether the 4096-entry / 120-second window is exceeded in practice requires load data (requests per second across all NIP-98 surfaces) that no diagram states.

2. **Whether the standalone cloudflared tunnel is actually running in the deployment**: the material describes the file and the gap, but not whether any operator has started it alongside a demo-open backend. This determines whether F-01 is a live vulnerability or a latent one.

3. **Actual key custody locations and rotation procedures**: ES-10.10 states "every custodian, deployed location, rotation cadence and incident-response window is UNCONFIRMED." Without knowing where keys actually live, I cannot assess whether a compromised key can be rotated or whether a key holder can be identified.

4. **Whether the WS event-loss path (F-09) drops events in practice**: the `ws` library may buffer sends when in a connecting state, or the subscriber may queue internally. The material describes no queue but also does not state the library's behaviour on send-during-disconnect.

5. **The real multi-tenant posture**: multi-user pods are "a design scaffold, defaults off," but the material does not state how many users the production deployment actually serves. If it is single-operator, several multi-tenant risks (pod provisioning, namespace isolation, RBAC defaults) may be latent rather than active.

## estate / premortem

# Post-mortem — reconstructed from the estate record

The brief fixes the incident class: twelve months out, a data, custody or security failure that cost a client. What follows is the set of root causes the estate's own documentation makes most probable, and the latent defects found while tracing them. Every claim carries the citation the material gives; where I reason past the material I say so. Nothing here is verified against running code — the engineers checking these findings should treat the citations, not my framing, as the claim.

---

### R-1 — Deleted agent memory stays semantically searchable in RuVector forever: the erasure path never reaches the vector store
- Topics: ES-08.9, ES-07.9, ES-07.3, ES-08.11
- Chain of events:
  1. A client (or regulator) requests erasure of agent-held memory about them. The operator deletes it through the sanctioned pod surface: `deleteAgentMemory(podPath, agentId, key)`.
  2. The pod-side call deletes the `.jsonld` resource — `DELETE`, 204 or 404-as-success (`ldpClient.ts:206-216`, `agentMemory.ts:196-204`).
  3. The call graph never reaches RuVector. `agentMemory.ts:196-204` has no reverse-tombstone dispatch to `memory_delete` or equivalent — "this call path does not even attempt the secondary write, let alone log its absence," in violation of Invariant 6 of `docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md` which requires a dropped secondary write to be logged, not swallowed.
  4. The RuVector row persists intact — not just the 384-dim embedding but the whole stored value (ES-07.2: "Retrieve-by-key still returns the WHOLE value") — and remains live to HNSW semantic search, including via the global `namespace "*"` cross-namespace search (ES-07.3).
  5. Months later, another session's `memory_search` surfaces the deleted client-identifying content in results. The client learns their erasure request was never honoured.
- Evidence: ES-08.9 states the gap directly and the historical governing document calls it an erasure hole (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md` Known divergences and open items). ES-07.9 D5 names it "Largest erasure hole" and adds: "No point-in-time RuVector backup exists, so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO." ES-08.11 confirms the durable reconciliation journal "has no production destructive adapters: subject/authority mappings and live erasure acceptance remain outstanding." The 2026-09-07 audit qualification explicitly performed "No database mutation or reindex."
- Warning signs visible today: the material's own audits flagged and qualified this twice without remediation; the deletion path is client-side with no estate-level erasure entry point; the embedding cap (~512 tokens, ES-07.2) means the searchable residue is the front-loaded, fact-densest prefix of the value.
- Confidence: high — the defect is stated, not inferred; only the discovery vector is assumed.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The standalone cloudflared tunnel fronts the demo-open dev posture, putting anonymous reads of the private graph on the public internet through a route no repo can review
- Topics: ES-10.3, ES-09.8, ES-10.1
- Chain of events:
  1. The shipped dev compose defaults invert the fail-closed code defaults: `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS:-1` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:106`) and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS:-1` (`:107`) yield the demo-open posture — "anonymous /api reads ON, owner-less boot permitted" (ES-10.3 NET). `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE` now also defaults to 1 on that service (line 90).
  2. The standalone `docker-compose.cloudflared.yml` joins `visionclaw_network` with alias `visionclaw-server:3001` and fronts "WHATEVER profile the backend booted." Its route is "configured as a Cloudflare dashboard Public Hostname — no local config.yml, so the exposed mapping is NOT reviewable from this repo" (`docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:5-6`).
  3. The tunnel is outbound-initiated and publishes no port, so it is "invisible to the ADR-2013 compose port-audit" that only sweeps published ports (`:6-8`).
  4. ADR-2119 made the *unified* file's cloudflared opt-in under its own profile but "leaves this standalone file unaffected" (`ADR-2119-prod-ingress-is-declared-lan-or-tunnel.md:64-66`): `VISIONCLAW_INGRESS=lan` does not stop a hand-started standalone tunnel fronting a LAN-declared host.
  5. Compounding: "default dev and production both publish host port 3001, so concurrent activation collides. Compose profiles do not enforce mutual exclusion" (ES-09.8 GATE) — the tunnel can be fronting the demo-open dev container while the operator believes the hardened prod service is serving.
  6. An external party enumerates `/api` anonymously through the Cloudflare hostname. Client-confidential graph content is disclosed. The operator cannot even reconstruct what was mapped, because the mapping lives in a dashboard.
- Evidence: ES-10.3 CFT states the outcome outright: "with this demo-open posture, anonymous /api reads reach the public internet via the Cloudflare tunnel, not just the LAN." The code's fail-closed default (`rbac_gate.rs:126-132`, `unwrap_or(false)`) versus the compose override is documented as two separate facts (DRIFT2). The anonymous-reads-enable log line exists at `rbac_gate.rs:194`.
- Warning signs visible today: ES-10.1's own invariant — "Any combination outside these three rows is UNSUPPORTED, a defect not a variant"; the unreviewable dashboard-configured hostname; the port-collision non-exclusion; the drift note that release now requires explicit `VISIONCLAW_SECURITY_PROFILE` intent while the dev service's defaults remain demo-open.
- Confidence: high — the exposure path is asserted by the material end to end; only the external reader and timing are assumed.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Agent-executable container holds the raw host Docker socket behind evadable deny rules: one injected instruction escalates to host-level access to every sibling's data
- Topics: ES-10.12, ES-01.5, ES-10.10
- Chain of events:
  1. `role_isolation = false` is the shipped default. With the flag off, "the entrypoint widens the host Docker socket o+rw for devuser" (`agentbox/config/entrypoint-unified.sh:589-593`) and "PID 1's environment, .env included, reaches all of them" (ES-10.12 O2).
  2. Independently, the override grants `group_add 965` — "the container drives docker WITHOUT sudo" (ES-01.5 N2). The socket is the host inode, already widened by earlier boots; "only a host-side chmod" narrows it — the boot merely records `degraded:docker-socket` (ES-10.12, `entrypoint-unified.sh:596-612`, `docker-compose.override.yml:115-117`).
  3. Claude Code sessions run with `[claude_code] bypassPermissions` default plus deny rules (`agentbox.toml:642-657`), but "Deny rules (`agentbox.toml:657`) are pattern matches a `sh -c` wrapper evades, so it is access control, not a vault entry" (ES-10.10 D6).
  4. The container's agents routinely process untrusted-adjacent content: the email corpus through the email-search skill (ES-06), web pages through the browsercontainer CDP door, nostr events. A prompt-injected instruction that reaches a shell evades the deny list.
  5. With the Docker socket reachable, the instruction mounts the host root or launches a privileged sibling: the pods at 8484, ruvector-postgres, `/run/secrets`, the VisionClaw graph, the email index — mass cross-tenant read and tamper from a single agent session.
- Evidence: every standing condition above is stated with citations in ES-10.12, ES-01.5 and ES-10.10. What is *not* shown is the injection itself or a privilege-escalation event; the material documents capability, not occurrence.
- Warning signs visible today: the boot is designed to *record* `degraded:docker-socket` rather than fail; the role-isolation rehearsal (agentbox AB-36) is pending; the custody register itself is "proposed governing surface … UNCONFIRMED" (ES-10.10 ST); the egress register catalogues network egress but the socket is a host-control channel, not a route in that register.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the conditions are stated with high confidence; the exploitation chain is my composition of them.
- Severity: high

### F-01 — A build launched inside the container silently bakes stale code, so "shipped" fixes never land
- Topics: ES-09.1, ES-09.2, ES-01.5
- Evidence: ES-09.1 (operator-verified ENV-FACT): the container has the HOST Docker socket mounted; builds issued from inside "resolve bind-mount paths against ITS OWN (host-side) cwd/view"; the failure mode is "container starts, health check passes, edits never take effect." ES-01.5 D2 labels it a TRAP: "a build launched from INSIDE this container resolves bind paths against the HOST filesystem and silently bakes stale code."
- Failure: an operator patches a security defect from the Claude Code container and rebuilds; health stays green; the vulnerable image-build-time code keeps serving. The incident recurs with everyone believing the fix was live, and forensics waste the response window on "already fixed" code.
- Confidence: high — mechanism stated; the incident linkage is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Trap)

### F-02 — Nothing asserts the shipped release binary omits dev-auth; the only gate is a text check of committed Dockerfiles
- Topics: ES-09.5, ES-09.11
- Evidence: ES-09.5: "ADR-2037 (proposed, implementation_status none): no CI or image-build assertion yet verifies a shipped release binary omits dev-auth — a mis-targeted pipeline could promote the stubbed-hygiene binary to production undetected." The `dev-auth-release-gate` job is a "hermetic text assertion over the committed Dockerfiles/entrypoints" (`ci.yml:146-155`, "no --release cargo line may name dev-auth" `:163-168`) — it checks files, not artefacts. Dev-auth "compiles in the Bearer dev-session-token bypass and stubs `enforce_release_env_hygiene` to a no-op" (`src/main.rs:169` stub).
- Failure: a release built through any path other than the committed Dockerfiles carries dev-auth; the well-known dev bearer token authenticates callers and the boot-time env hygiene never fires, so the release's own guard is also stubbed.
- Confidence: high — the assertion gap is stated; the mis-targeted build is the conditional trigger.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (the gap is the ADR's own status)

### F-03 — The TXO stand-in deposit is a reachable free-money oracle over non-atomic ledger state
- Topics: ES-08.12, ES-03.12
- Evidence: ES-08.12 D6: the deposit "credits sats for any parseable TXO URI guarded only by a replay key. A chain-settled estate must not keep a reachable free-money oracle" — deletion is only PROPOSED. PRE: "non-atomic payment state is fixed FIRST. The crate's own README reproduces a critical finding saying not to carry value through the payment routes until it is fixed." ES-03.12: three unsynced did:nostr-keyed ledgers; the host deposit handler returns 501 (`ADR-2111:22-25`); the forum store "settles atomically and still reconciles with nothing."
- Failure: once any balance is trusted, fabricated TXO URIs mint sats limited only by a replay key, and concurrent credit/debit on non-atomic state corrupts the WebLedger; the three ledgers then disagree with no reconciliation path.
- Confidence: medium — the mechanism is described as present; whether value is carried on these routes today is not stated (the host's own deposit is a 501 stub).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence; the free-money-oracle wording is theirs)

### F-04 — The default pod write path is unsigned, and a degraded boot silently mints a `did:nostr:local` placeholder identity
- Topics: ES-08.2, ES-04.4
- Evidence: ES-08.2: `nip98` is null unless `sign_requests=true`, and the "default keeps prior unsigned behaviour byte-identical (`pod-signer.js:34-35`)." ES-04.4: the signer can fall back UNSIGNED, and "the did:nostr:local placeholder fallback that `agent-identity.js:41-43` says the entrypoint still runs unconditionally" means "a degraded boot can silently produce a NON-SOVEREIGN identity." A signer load failure is cached and never retried (`pod-signer.js:106-116`). ADR-2064 closed the fail-open hole only with the flag on; with it off, "unsigned is the declared baseline."
- Failure: production runs the default flag-off path; a key-load failure at first write caches permanently; pod data is thereafter written unsigned under a placeholder identity — unattributable, indistinguishable from tampering, and non-sovereign in exactly the sense the estate claims as its differentiator.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-05 — Neither rationale gate can fire on a VisionClaw case, because the two gates read different tiers and nothing declares one
- Topics: ES-05.12, ES-05.13
- Evidence: ES-05.13: the host gate reads the DECLARED tier — `None` for VisionClaw bodies (`enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:391-392`, gate no-ops at `:411-413`); the relay reads the EFFECTIVE tier, which folds an undeclared case to `medium` (`governance.rs:525`, `wrangler.toml:48`), and `check_rationale` "only bites at high or critical." Conclusion stated: "NEITHER rationale gate fires on a VisionClaw case." ES-05.12 OPEN, restated 2 October.
- Failure: a high-stakes human decision on a VisionClaw-originated case is published with empty reasoning and passes both gates; when the client contests the destructive change, no rationale exists anywhere in the estate to produce.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open + Divergence)

---

Two cross-cutting observations the checkers should carry: first, several of these compose — the demo-open posture (R-2) is amplified by the 3001 collision (ES-09.8) and unreviewable dashboard routing; the socket chain (R-3) is amplified by F-01, because even a correct fix can silently fail to deploy. Second, the estate's audits repeatedly *qualify* these defects without remediating them (ES-07 audit, ES-08.11 closeout, ES-10 custody qualification), so absence of an open ticket should not be read as absence of the defect.

## knowledgegraph / critical

### F-01 — Retired pipeline took the enforcement of at least six baseline invariants with it; no live equivalent is stated anywhere
- Topics: KG-07.1, KG-07.2, KG-05.3, KG-01.1
- Evidence: KG-07.1 lists invariants whose only stated enforcement mechanisms are archived files: I6 references "EXPECTED_CLASSES in build.yml" — the very workflow KG-01.1 and KG-05.3 show moved to `archive/github-workflows/build.yml` and inert; I8 cites `census.py:61`, I9 `visibility.py:133`, I11 `manifest.py:137` — all under `archive/logseq-era-2026-09-22/pipeline/`. KG-05.3 states the sole live producer is visionGraph's `vault build` (publish.yml:157-190) and names only one gate: "space and Earth domain contract" (publish.yml:296-297). No visibility scan, census, identity check, or manifest step is stated for the vault output. The governing doc `build-and-gates.md:1-3` still claims the six-gate workflow is the active "reproducible half" (marked as drift, but the invariant register still presents all 11 as live baseline).
- Failure: A private identifier in the vault-era corpus (Obsidian vaults routinely hold private notes) reaches `narrativegoldmine.com` because nothing runs the I9 private-IRI rescan; or a corpus change drops classes with no I6/I10 count-identity check, and consumers reason over a silently truncated graph. There is also no stated manifest (I11) to audit any gh-pages generation against.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — from the absence of any stated equivalent gate upstream; verify the vault build before acting)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the `build-and-gates.md` staleness is marked as DIVERGENCE in KG-05.3, but the loss of invariant enforcement is presented as historical record in KG-07.2, not as an open defect)

GitHub's detector compounds this by labelling the whole repo AGPL (LICENSING.md:28-29).

### F-10 — TITLE-vs-SLUG mirror mismatch makes raw.githubusercontent.com a runtime dependency for live page prose

- Confidence: medium (the mismatch and fallback are stated; the population of affected pages is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in KG-04.4 — the dead mirror; the runtime external dependency is the unmarked half)
