## Not judgeable from this material

1. **visionGraph's publish.yml hardening and deploy secret scope** — workflow/job permissions, action pinning, and the exact credential used for the `external_repository` gh-pages write; needed before assessing F-07 honestly.
2. **What GitHub Pages actually serves** — the Pages source configuration: KG-01.1 claims root `static/ns/v2.jsonld` is "still served" and that root CNAME is "read by Pages", while KG-05.3 says gh-pages (built from the vault output) is the served branch; whether the vault build/peaceiris deploy preserves CNAME, custom 404s, and root static assets on gh-pages is unstated.
3. **The vault build's gate inventory** — whether `vault build` implements equivalents of the retired census, private-identifier scan, identity check, and manifest emission; this decides whether F-01 is a live exposure or already mitigated upstream.
4. **The 183-byte NGG1 golden fixture's home and executor** — which repo holds it and which CI (if any) runs it against the vault writer and both vowl-wasm reader versions; decides F-05.
5. **Upstream tier-size bounding and markdown sanitisation** — whether any vault/publish step bounds T0/T1/T2 sizes against the explorer caps (F-03), and whether the renderer sanitises markdown fetched from the raw.githubusercontent fallback (XSS exposure of KG-04.4 is untestable from here).

## knowledgegraph / premortem

**Post-mortem — narrativegoldmine.com publication chain (knowledgeGraph / visionGraph / VisionClaw). Incident class: data/custody.**

Scope note: every claim below is taken from the audited topic files at verified commit `4ed9ac1` (knowledgeGraph) and `015ca2c` (visionGraph). Nothing was read from source. Where the material is silent, the finding is marked inferred. Each finding must be re-verified against code and tests before anyone acts on it.

---

### R-1 — Every privacy and accounting gate retired with the Logseq pipeline; the live publisher has no evidenced equivalent
- Topics: KG-01.1, KG-05.3, KG-05.5, KG-06.4, KG-07.1, KG-07.2, KG-07.3
- Chain of events: the corpus grows upstream in the visionGraph vault, beyond this repo's review (the production surface already diverged — six domains here vs eight shipping, KG-04.6) → vault build + Quartz render it → publish.yml runs `vault validate` and gates the bundle on "the space and Earth domain contract" (publish.yml:296-297) → peaceiris deploys to this repo's gh-pages → Pages serves it at narrativegoldmine.com → corpus-sync pushes the same bundle into VisionClaw (publish.yml:459-465), where Whelk reasons over it and agentbox's Loom/ontology-bridge tools serve it. A page or identifier the old pipeline would have held back — non-boolean/absent `vc:public`, a private IRI — ships and is reasoned over downstream, because invariant 1 (boolean gate re-checked per output stage), invariant 8 (zero-rejected census), invariant 9 (visibility.py at every output, release-gate rescan) and invariant 11 (per-artifact manifest) all lived in files now under `archive/logseq-era-2026-09-22/pipeline/` and in the retired build.yml.
- Evidence: KG-07's source list is entirely `archive/logseq-era-2026-09-22/pipeline/{census,visibility,manifest,release_gate}.py` and `archive/github-workflows/build.yml` — all archived; KG-01.1 states the archived workflow "cannot run again even if triggered." KG-05.3 enumerates the live run's steps — validate (publish.yml:157-170), build (182-190), domain-contract gate (296-297), deploy, corpus-sync (459-465) — no visibility or privacy step appears anywhere. KG-07.2 records that even when live, "equal counts cannot prove equal identities or intended visibility — release_gate.py check_identity (line 127) was the mitigation, never a closure." KG-07.3: 4,383+ referenced-but-undeclared targets ship as stubs "deliberately NOT redacted," and a person-named class (`ethan-mollick`) sits in the public TBox. KG-06.4: the `review/` promotion outlet for pending (`public:: false`) candidates was retired with the archive; README.md:15-16 says archived content is "not accepted into."
- Warning signs visible today: all eleven baseline invariants' enforcement points sit in archived code; the only gate the live pipeline demonstrably has is a domain-scope contract, not a content-visibility contract; the corpus evolves upstream of the repository that owns the published artefact.
- Confidence: medium — inferred. The archival of every privacy/accounting gate is stated; the absence of a replacement upstream is an argument from the material's exhaustive step list, not from reading publish.yml.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Production runs a diverged tree; the governed explorer copy never ships, so fixes and format pins land in the wrong place
- Topics: KG-04.1, KG-04.3, KG-04.6, KG-07.1
- Chain of events: a client-visible defect appears on narrativegoldmine.com → the engineer works against `knowledgeGraph/explorer` — the tree BASELINE, architecture docs and the invariants register all govern → the fix lands, the golden fixture passes → deploy runs visionGraph publish.yml:376, which builds `publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/modern`, a different tree → the fix never reaches the site. In parallel, the shipped tree pins `@dreamlab-ai/vowl-wasm` npm `0.1.2` while this copy pins the v0.1.1 GitHub-release tarball (explorer/modern/package.json:20 vs WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:20). Invariant 2 claims "writer + both readers pinned to a 183-byte golden fixture" — two different reader versions cannot both be that pin, so writer↔reader NGG1 drift is unwatched. A tier that overruns MAX_NODES 1500 / MAX_EDGES 4000 / FOCUS_MAX 300 hits `assertScope`, which by contract throws RangeError rather than truncating (scope.ts:144-155) — the `/graph` route hard-fails for the client.
- Evidence: KG-04.6 states outright "this copy is never built or deployed by anything in this repository"; build.yml "never builds explorer/" (archive/github-workflows/build.yml:1); domain slugs diverged 2026-10-01, six vs eight (explorer/modern/src/types/scope.ts:73-80 vs WasmVOWL/modern/src/types/scope.ts:75-84); KG-04.1 note1: BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:14 and architecture/explorer.md:10 cite an in-tree `explorer/rust-wasm/src/ngg1.rs` that does not exist at this commit; KG-04.3 note: caps are CONTRACT — "rejected, never silently truncated."
- Warning signs visible today: the only tree where the 11 invariants, the golden fixture and the caps are testable demonstrably does not ship; the two trees already disagree on the one dependency both pin.
- Confidence: high — the divergence and non-shipping status are stated outright; which failure fires first (lost fix, format misparse, cap overflow) is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Sole write custody of the production branch sits in another repository's workflow, with no gate, no review path and no stated CNAME handling in the target repo
- Topics: KG-01.1, KG-05.3, KG-05.5, KG-06.2, KG-07.1
- Chain of events: a run of visionGraph publish.yml (owner push or manual, per KG-05.5 note2) builds an empty, partial or bad bundle → peaceiris/actions-gh-pages with `external_repository=knowledgeGraph` replaces this repo's gh-pages — the only production surface — wholesale → nothing in this repo can block, review or roll it back: its own workflow is archived with `permissions: contents: read` at workflow and job level ("could not write even if a step tried," build.yml:6-8) and "nothing here computes it any more" (KG-05.5) → the bundle is non-deterministic by design (invariant 4: manifest UUID/time and blank-node ids vary) and its verification instrument, manifest.py:137, is archived, so there is no SHA-256 chain to select or verify a rollback against → the stated post-deploy step is not verification but further propagation: corpus-sync pushes the bad bundle into VisionClaw with the source sha (publish.yml:459-465), where it is reasoned and served through agentbox before human eyes. Separately, the domain binding depends on a CNAME that "Pages reads" but that "no workflow here writes" (KG-05.3); what the external deploy does to the CNAME on the served branch is unstated anywhere in the material.
- Evidence: KG-05.3's sequence states the order (validate → build → deploy → corpus-sync after deploy) and the CNAME parenthetical; KG-01.1 invariant: "this repo no longer authors or builds the corpus — it is now the deploy TARGET"; KG-07.1 I4/I11 with manifest.py archived; KG-06.2 for the VisionClaw/agentbox grounding path.
- Warning signs visible today: production content, production domain binding and production provenance are all controlled outside the repository that owns the domain; the only stated post-deploy action is onward propagation, never verification.
- Confidence: medium — inferred. The custody structure and step order are stated; the deploy's effect on CNAME and the absence of any rollback path are inferred from silence.
- Severity: high

## nostr-rust-forum / critical

Findings, ranked by the damage each would do in production.

### F-01 — Admin authority is broken across the whole deployment surface, and one template ships a real stranger's key as default admin
- Topics: NF-07.2, NF-08.4, NF-08.7, NF-10.6 (DD3), NF-10.7 (S2)
- Evidence: ADMIN_PUBKEYS is read by the auth worker (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/src/admin.rs:71`) and the relay (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/auth.rs:183`) but declared in exactly one template — the search worker — which ships "a REAL 64-hex pubkey" (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:33`), the only non-placeholder value in any template (NF-07.2 N3). SETUP.md:122-126 never lists the variable, and `nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:10-13` asserts there is no ADMIN_PUBKEYS reader in src/ — contradicted by auth.rs:183. Separately, the relay's members branch queries a table no migration or bootstrap creates (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/auth.rs:192`; lib.rs:686 creates 16 tables, none `members`), so effective relay authority is static ∪ whitelist only (NF-08.7 N3); and the auth-worker's RELAY_DB database_id is a zero placeholder (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/wrangler.toml:20`).
- Failure: A by-the-book deployment has no static admin on auth or relay; first-admin bootstrap requires manual D1 surgery. Any deployment keeping the search-worker default has its ingest admin controlled by a key the operator never chose. Admins added via the auth members table silently hold zero relay powers — moderation, 31403 decisions, admin kinds all refuse with no explanation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT DD3; notes NF-07.2 N2-N3, NF-08.7 N4-N5, NF-08.4 N3; anomaly O4 for the dead members branch)

### F-02 — Governance guards compare pubkeys case-sensitively while the material itself proves the same key verifies in two casings
- Topics: NF-06.6, NF-06.15, NF-06.18, NF-10.11 (KF-6)
- Evidence: KF-6: "the self-review and original-signer guards compare pubkeys case-SENSITIVELY while NIP-98 returns the pubkey verbatim" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:29`), enforced at `nostr-bbs-core/src/governance.rs:1738` and `:1835`. The bypass precondition is established by the material: "NIP-98 accepts case-insensitive hex and returns event.pubkey verbatim, and the event id is recomputed over that string, so the SAME key can legitimately present in two casings and both verify" (NF-06.15 N1, `governance_api.rs:98-100`). The registry fixed exactly this class (`canonical_agent_pubkey`, governance_api.rs:108); core did not — "the same class the registry closed in NF-06.15, left open in the core aggregate" (NF-10.11 N6). NF-06.18 N1 adds that the live requesting agent holds the admin flag, i.e. an agent that can open cases can also publish 31403.
- Failure: An admin/reviewer agent re-cases its own pubkey hex in a 31403; the self-review equality (`CaseError::SelfReview`) misses and it decides its own case — the exact FR2.2 invariant the relay claims to enforce before save (`nip_handlers.rs:1099-1105`). The supersession direction fails closed (false denials) rather than open.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-6)

### F-03 — The public unfurl endpoint ships with a denylist-only SSRF guard and no wall-clock timeout
- Topics: NF-07.5, NF-07.6, NF-10.7 (S3)
- Evidence: "without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS the guard is denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable" is stated in the code (`nostr-bbs-preview-worker/src/ssrf.rs:13`); the template declares only ALLOWED_ORIGIN (`nostr-bbs-preview-worker/wrangler.toml:13`), so the real mitigation is unset by default (NF-07.6 N4). "There is NO wall-clock timeout - only redirect-count and body-size limits bound a fetch" (NF-07.6 N3; MAX_REDIRECTS 3 at ssrf.rs:33; caps 2 MiB ssrf.rs:38, 10 MiB ssrf.rs:44).
- Failure: /preview fetches attacker-supplied URLs. A DNS name resolving public at check time and internal at connect time passes the denylist because the Workers runtime has no resolve-then-pin primitive (ssrf.rs:13). Separately, slow-dripping bodies within the size caps hold Worker invocations with no time bound.
- Confidence: high (vulnerability stated in code; exploit path inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE acknowledged in code; S3)

### F-04 — Governance read APIs treat "authenticated" as "any nostr key", and the admin-only reviewer aggregate is reconstructible from them
- Topics: NF-02.6, NF-06.9, NF-10.11 (KF-2, KF-2a)
- Evidence: KF-2: "the governance READ endpoints verify a NIP-98 signature with no membership check, so authenticated is effectively anonymous" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:23`); KF-2a: the admin-gated reviewer aggregate "can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read" (`known-findings.md:24`). The route table confirms cases and decisions are require_authed only (`governance_api.rs:561`, `:626`), while reviewer telemetry is admin-gated (`governance_api.rs:1407`).
- Failure: Anyone holding any valid key — not a member, never invited — enumerates broker cases, decision outcomes, rationales and receipt timelines, including moderation-adjacent content; the admin-only telemetry gate protects nothing because its inputs are world-readable to keyholders.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-2, KF-2a — recorded as an operator decision "taken once for both")

### F-05 — Encrypted zones silently become plaintext-and-searchable when ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is unset, and no template declares it
- Topics: NF-03.12, NF-05.10, NF-08.2, NF-08.8
- Evidence: `is_encrypted` = gate AND zone.encrypted AND NOT public (`zone_config.rs:127`), gate read from env at `zone_config.rs:86`; "ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is read from env at zone_config.rs:86 but declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker …

## nostr-rust-forum / premortem

# Post-mortem — forum kit production incident (client loss)

Scope note: everything below is drawn from the diagrams-as-code record and its `path:line` citations. Where I reason past what the record states, the confidence line says "inferred". Nothing here rests on an ordering or concurrency the diagrams do not assert.

---

## Ranked root causes

### R-1 — Preview-worker SSRF: denylist-only guard, allowlist unset in every template, no fetch timeout
- Topics: NF-07.5, NF-07.6, NF-10.7 (S3)
- Chain of events: 1) A deployment installs preview-worker from the template, which declares only `ALLOWED_ORIGIN` (`nostr-bbs-preview-worker/wrangler.toml:13`) — `PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS` is never set. 2) The guard therefore runs denylist-only; the crate's own module doc states that the Workers runtime exposes no resolve-then-pin primitive, so without the allowlist it is DNS-rebinding-vulnerable (`nostr-bbs-preview-worker/src/ssrf.rs:13`). 3) A member's client renders a link under an attacker-controlled hostname and calls `GET /preview`. 4) The SSRF policy runs against the parsed host (`ssrf.rs:294` onward); the name resolves to a public IP at check time and passes every denylist class (`ssrf.rs:352-466`). 5) The subsequent fetch re-resolves the same name to a blocked target; the manual redirect re-check (`ssrf.rs:174`, `ssrf.rs:192`, cap 3 at `ssrf.rs:33`) does not close this because the first hop is the rebound one. 6) Whatever the internal endpoint returns is parsed as OpenGraph data and handed back to the requesting client. Separately, there is no wall-clock timeout at all (NF-07.6 N3) — only redirect-count and body-size caps bound a fetch — so held-open origins also tie up worker capacity.
- Evidence: the in-code acknowledgment `ssrf.rs:13`; the allowlist exists (`ssrf.rs:100`, `ssrf.rs:114`, installed at `lib.rs:511`/`ssrf.rs:153`) but the template never sets it; the no-timeout divergence NF-07.6 N3; NF-10.7 S3 repeats all three as a live deployment-shaped divergence.
- Warning signs visible today: the code documents its own vulnerability; the only preview template var is `ALLOWED_ORIGIN`; the acknowledged absence of a resolve-then-pin primitive means the allowlist is the sole real mitigation.
- Confidence: high that the configuration gap and guard limitation are exactly as stated; the exploitation step (post-check re-resolution reaching an internal target inside the Cloudflare runtime) is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Silent encryption-off: `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` is declared in no worker template, so a redeploy leaves "encrypted" zones storing plaintext
- Topics: NF-08.2, NF-08.8, NF-08.9, NF-03.12, NF-05.12, NF-05.16
- Chain of events: 1) The operator marks a zone `encrypted` in `forum.toml`; `ZONE_CONFIG` projects to the relay env and to clients. 2) `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` is read from env at `zone_config.rs:86` but "declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker" (NF-08.2 N4, NF-08.8 N7) — there is no template line to copy and no SETUP step names it. 3) A redeploy or new environment omits the var; `is_encrypted = gate AND zone.encrypted AND NOT is_public_read` (`zone_config.rs:127`) evaluates false. 4) The encrypted-zone ciphertext gate at `nip_handlers.rs:1157` never fires; the write is treated as an ordinary zone write. 5) The retro BBS client gates its composer on the `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` config flag (`config.rs:68`, `config.rs:162`, `screens.rs:906-918`) — with the flag absent it believes the board is not encrypted and composes plaintext into the encrypted-flagged board; the relay accepts it because its own gate is also off. 6) The material's own invariant says the shape check is the only thing guaranteeing "no plaintext … lands in an encrypted zone by ANY author, admins included" (`zone_config.rs:198-201`) — with the master gate off, that guarantee is void, and the confidential posts sit in clear in D1. 7) Client-side, the same flag being off also stops grant sync ("no gift wrap is re-opened … until encryption_enabled() is true", NF-05.16 N3), so new members never receive keys — the zone silently degrades in both directions.
- Evidence: NF-08.2 N4 ("a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF"); NF-08.8 N7; the write-gate chain NF-08.9 (`nip_handlers.rs:1150-1163`, `zone_config.rs:205-217`); the BBS composer gate NF-05.12 N4; the relay-side invariant NF-03.12 N5 / `zone_config.rs:193-201`.
- Warning signs visible today: the drift is already documented; this estate's deployments demonstrably diverge from template vars — the pod template ships `PAY_ENABLED "false"` while production runs it true (NF-04.9 note), the same class of omission.
- Confidence: medium — every link in the chain is stated; only the future act of omitting the var is not.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Client key custody: the root nsec embedded in a magic-link fragment, in a QR, and persisted in localStorage
- Topics: NF-05.8, NF-05.3, NF-05.4, NF-05.9, NF-12.10
- Chain of events: 1) Signup renders the recovery sheet; the nsec is generated in-WASM into a QR (`recovery_sheet.rs:66`, `:294`) and into a `/connect` magic link with the nsec in the URL fragment `#k=` (`recovery_sheet.rs:290`). 2) The member uses, bookmarks or shares that link, or keeps the sheet; URL fragments persist in browser history and browser-account history sync, and any extension or URL-capturing tool on the machine reads the root key off the link or the paper. 3) Independently, a remember-me session persists the session key in localStorage (`auth/session.rs:156`), readable by any script running on the origin. 4) An attacker holding the root key signs events as the member everywhere — NIP-98 to every REST surface, NIP-42 to the relay. 5) Because every DM is sent as a gift-wrap *pair* including a copy sealed to the sender's own key (NF-05.9, NF-12.10), the attacker also recovers the member's entire **sent** DM history. 6) The pod is owned by `did:nostr:{pubkey}` with owner Control (NF-04.5), so the key grants full pod read/write. Nothing on the relay distinguishes the attacker: the signature *is* the identity (README.md:213-217).
- Evidence: fragment and QR citations above; the material's safety claim covers only "browsers do not send the fragment to the server" (NF-05.8 note) — no claim about history, sync or extensions; localStorage/sessionStorage persistence (NF-05.4 note, `auth/session.rs:156`, `:163`); "The private key never leaves the device" (NF-05.3 note) sits in direct tension with a key printed on paper and embedded in a URL; the root is the single point of failure because every subkey is root-recoverable (`keys.rs:240`, NF-10.2 K6).
- Warning signs visible today: full private-key material in a URL, in a QR, and in web storage, all stated as shipped behavior.
- Confidence: medium (inferred) — the artifacts are stated; the exfiltration vector is reasoning beyond the material.
- Severity: high

---

## Latent defects found while investigating

### F-01 — zone-migrate purges the only readable copy of channel history after sealing
- Topics: NF-01.1, NF-12.11
- Evidence: the CLI "seals plaintext channel history into sealed-original envelopes, verifies, then purges the plaintext" (`crates/nostr-bbs-zone-migrate/Cargo.toml:5`, NF-01.1 N4); `open_sealed` imposes six checks and "any single failing check makes the envelope undecryptable — callers must never partly trust it" (`sealed.rs:50-51`, checks at `sealed.rs:379-413`). Nothing in the material states the migrator's "verifies" step is a full round-trip decrypt under the zone key members actually hold.
- Failure: the migration runs, the purge completes, and later one envelope fails one of the six checks — or the zone key for that epoch is absent from a restored key store — and that history is permanently unrecoverable; the plaintext no longer exists anywhere.
- Confidence: medium (inferred parity gap; the purge itself is stated).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — Admin event deletion is silent, so deletion never propagates to clients
- Topics: NF-01.3, NF-05.6
- Evidence: `POST /api/admin/events/delete` — "silent kind-42 delete, no kind-5" (`admin_events.rs:5`), deliberate because "a kind-5 would tombstone the id that a sealed-original envelope restores" (`lib.rs:343-345`); client tombstones are built only from kind-5 events (`channels.rs:93`, `channels.rs:98`).
- Failure: an erasure or takedown honored by row deletion never reaches clients — every cached copy keeps rendering the content indefinitely, and any already-delivered copy is unaffected.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (presented as deliberate ADR-2017 design)

### F-03 — Ban evasion: moderation gates key on the raw signing pubkey; gift wraps are excluded from ban gating
- Topics: NF-03.4, NF-03.5, NF-03.6, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-4/KF-9 — the write gate resolves a device key to its owner but "the moderation gates that follow key on the RAW signing pubkey, and a gift wrap has no author to resolve at all" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:27`, `:44`); gift wraps are deliberately excluded from the ban gate (`nip_handlers.rs:56-65`) and bounded only by the recipient whitelist (`nip_handlers.rs:859`).
- Failure: a banned member keeps publishing DMs and reactions via throwaway-authored gift wraps, or — with `DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED` on — ordinary kinds via a device key whose raw pubkey is not the banned owner.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, KF-4/KF-9)

### F-04 — Case-sensitive self-review and original-signer guards allow self-decision via hex-casing variants
- Topics: NF-06.6, NF-06.15, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-6 — "the self-review and original-signer guards compare pubkeys case-SENSITIVELY while NIP-98 returns the pubkey verbatim" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:29`), guards enforced at `governance.rs:1738` and `:1835`; meanwhile "the SAME key can legitimately present in two casings and both verify" (`governance_api.rs:98-100`). The registry closed this class for lookups (NF-06.15); the core aggregate keeps it open.
- Failure: a delegated reviewer or admin holding the case-owner's key in the opposite casing decides their own case — the SelfReview invariant (`governance.rs:1286`) does not fire and an unauthorized self-approval is recorded as valid.
- Confidence: high (guard property and casing fact both stated; the assembly is direct).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, KF-6)

### F-05 — Governance read endpoints: NIP-98 without membership check is effectively anonymous
- Topics: NF-02.2, NF-02.6, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-2/KF-2a — the governance READ endpoints verify a signature with no membership check, "so authenticated is effectively anonymous, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:23-24`); an operator decision "taken once for both".
- Failure: any keyholder — on an open instance, any registrant — reads cases, decisions and moderation context, and reconstructs the admin-only reviewer telemetry.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, KF-2/KF-2a)

### F-06 — The relay's members-table admin branch is structurally dead, so revocation through the auth surface has no effect
- Topics: NF-08.7, NF-10.4
- Evidence: the relay queries members in its OWN D1 (`auth.rs:192`) where "no migration and no bootstrap creates a members table there (relay lib.rs:686 creates 16 tables, none of them members). That branch is structurally dead" (NF-08.7 N3); effective relay authority is `ADMIN_PUBKEYS ∪ whitelist.is_admin` only.
- Failure: an operator who revokes adminship through the auth-worker members table — the surface that exists for it — sees no effect on the relay; the ex-admin retains full relay admin (suspend, silence, silent deletion, sealed-original writes).
- Confidence: high that the branch is dead (stated); medium (inferred) for the revocation scenario.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, NF-08.7 N3 / O4)

### F-07 — The relay `events` table exists only as a SETUP.md copy-paste
- Topics: NF-08.6, NF-09.5
- Evidence: "No CREATE TABLE for events exists in relay migrations 0001-0008 or in ensure_schema" (`relay lib.rs:686`); the sole source is `SETUP.md:69`; the topic carries an explicit "**Debt:**" line.
- Failure: a DR restore, new staging environment or rebuilt D1 assembled from the repo rather than the runbook boots and then fails every event query; an operator fixing it under pressure by recreating the database destroys stored events.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, NF-08.6)

### F-08 — Client env fallbacks diverge, so a missing `__ENV__` silently points clients at the wrong relay
- Topics: NF-05.7, NF-05.2
- Evidence: O8 live — `relay.rs` reads `window.__ENV__` inline (`relay.rs:974`) instead of the shared helper (`utils/relay_url.rs:151`), and "the two resolvers' hardcoded fallbacks DIVERGE" (`relay.rs:22` vs `utils/relay_url.rs:9`).
- Failure: a deploy that fails to inject `__ENV__` connects members' WebSocket traffic to one hardcoded default relay while REST surfaces resolve elsewhere — content and presence metadata are published to an operator that is not the forum's, with no error surfaced.
- Confidence: high (divergence stated; the wrong-endpoint consequence is direct).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (anomaly register O8, re-verified live)

### F-09 — `KV` and `SESSIONS` are the same physical namespace
- Topics: NF-08.4, NF-10.3
- Evidence: O11 — both bindings share one namespace id (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/wrangler.toml:24` and `:39`); "key-collision risk across the two logical uses" (NF-08.4 N2).
- Failure: a key written by one logical use overwrites the other's entry — rate-limit state or session data corrupted under colliding keys; the material gives no key schemas, so the concrete impact is unbounded.
- Confidence: high that the shared id is real; the failure shape is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (anomaly register O11, live)

### F-10 — Payment debit/delivery atomicity and the cached balance: no recovery path stated for spends
- Topics: NF-04.9, NF-04.11
- Evidence: debit is "the surviving spend-side primitive" (`pay_ledger.rs:434`); `recover_orphaned_jobs` exists "because a chain confirmation can outlive a Worker invocation" (`pay_ledger.rs:1273`) — for deposits — and no equivalent is stated for a debit committed before the paid effect; `read_balance` returns the cached D1 number (`pay_ledger.rs:412`) while the chain view with a staleness bound "is an error — not yet built" (ADR-2012 `...md:48-52`); production runs `PAY_ENABLED` true (NF-04.9 note).
- Failure: a Worker death landing between a committed debit and the service it purchases leaves the member charged and unserved; the served balance is the journal's with no error bound against the chain.
- Confidence: low (inferred) — argued from what is and is not stated about debit ordering.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — ADR-2012 implementation_status partial)

### F-11 — The welcome bot's full nsec is sealed at rest under a single master key
- Topics: NF-02.2
- Evidence: `set-bot-key` seals the nsec "with ChaCha20-Poly1305 under WELCOME_MASTER_KEY" (`crypto.rs:77`, `:97`), stored in D1 alongside the welcome tables (`schema.rs:164`).
- Failure: a D1 exfiltration combined with a weak, shared or leaked `WELCOME_MASTER_KEY` yields a trusted first-contact identity's private key; the attacker signs and DMs as the welcome bot.
- Confidence: medium (inferred leak path).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — The trust-sweep record contradicts itself on whether the audit INSERT is conditional
- Topics: NF-03.9, NF-03.10, NF-10.10, NF-11.12
- Evidence: NF-03.9's note says the sweep's remaining defect is that "its unconditional audit INSERT can commit when the optimistic UPDATE changes zero rows"; NF-10.10 asserts the audit is conditional — "INSERT audit SELECT WHERE changes equals 1" with both counts required to equal one; NF-11.12 N1 says both closeout defects are fixed.
- Failure: whichever account misdescribes the code: either `admin_log` records demotions that never committed (false moderation facts driving operator decisions), or the maintainers' own ledger of what is fixed is wrong. The two cannot both be true.
- Confidence: medium (documented self-contradiction; code must adjudicate).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift — NF-03.9 flags it remaining; NF-10.8 N1 flags the governing doc stale)

### F-13 — The search index is two stores that "must be written together" with nothing making that atomic
- Topics: NF-07.4
- Evidence: "the deserialiser parses ONLY the Vec segment (`store.rs:185`) — the mapping the Meta segment documents is actually read back from KV, so the two stores must be written together" (NF-07.4 N1; mapping at `lib.rs:339`).
- Failure: an ingest or retract that updates the R2 vectors but not the KV mapping (or vice versa) leaves vectors labeled with stale ids — search returns the wrong event or resurrectes retracted content under its old label.
- Confidence: high (coupling constraint stated; the partial-write scenario is inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — `ensure_schema` runs DDL on every request, before CORS and authentication
- Topics: NF-02.1, NF-08.5, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-3 — "ensure_schema runs its DDL on EVERY request, before the CORS short-circuit and any authentication" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:26`); both workers bootstrap idempotently on every cold start (`schema.rs:19`, relay `lib.rs:686`), and the auth fetch path runs it per request (`lib.rs:162`).
- Failure: unauthenticated request flooding multiplies D1 write load across auth and relay; quota exhaustion or throttling becomes a pre-auth availability failure for the whole forum.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, KF-3)

### F-15 — A real third-party admin pubkey ships as the template default, while `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` is undeclared where it is read
- Topics: NF-07.2, NF-08.7, NF-10.3, NF-10.6, NF-10.7
- Evidence: only the search-worker template declares `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` and it ships "a REAL 64-hex pubkey" (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:33`) — "the only non-generic value in any template" (NF-07.2 N3); the relay template asserts "there is no ADMIN_PUBKEYS reader in src/" (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:10-13`) while `relay auth.rs:183` and `auth admin.rs:71` both read it; SETUP never lists it (NF-08.7 N4/N5, S2, DD3).
- Failure: deployments built from the templates carry an unknown keyholder as search admin (arbitrary `/ingest` and `allowPrivate` retraction of the semantic index); an operator who discovers the missing bootstrap and copies that visible value into the relay/auth vars grants that keyholder full relay admin — silent event deletion (NF-01.3), sealed-original publication (NF-11.16), suspend/silence, and admin-set pubkey aliases that reroute DM delivery (`profile_stats.rs:6`, `user_admin.rs:525`).
- Confidence: high that every stated fact holds; the copy-into-relay step is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift DD3; Divergence S2)

## sidestr-rs / critical

### F-01 — The running estate is an upstream JavaScript engine; every Rust behaviour these documents describe is undeployed
- Topics: SR-01.4, SR-02.4, SR-03.4, SR-04.5
- Evidence: The producer "runs the upstream JavaScript engine baked at the upstream pins" (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:117`); the Rust libraries appear only as a dashed edge "validated components; native producer not deployed" (SR-01.4). Yet SR-02.4's admission/eviction behaviour, SR-03's EVM overlay, and SR-04's Hitch kernel are all presented as the system's behaviour. Opens in SR-01.4, SR-02.4, SR-03.4 and SR-04.5 each state a separate non-deployment.
- Failure: A consensus or funds incident on the live chain originates in third-party pinned JS. The remediation path an operator would assume exists — switch to the Rust stack — is unavailable: no producer is deployed, `sidestr-evm` and the reserve crates are unpublished, and the sealed chain documents (`chain.json:28`) cannot activate the rules that would make the Rust code relevant.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, SR-01.4; reinforced by Opens in SR-02.4, SR-03.4, SR-04.5)

### F-02 — A single snapshot file concentrates the channel signing key, all revocation secrets and preimages
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: The snapshot "contains the signing key, the revocation basepoint and per-state secrets, and preimages" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:22`) and "must be protected as wallet key material" (`snapshot.rs:24`); the host persists it atomically before sending anything (`protocol/mod.rs:30`). No backup, replication or encryption requirement is stated anywhere.
- Failure: Disk loss or corruption of one channel's snapshot after a counterparty broadcasts an old commitment means no revocation secrets exist to build the penalty (`protocol/chain.rs:182`) — the old state confirms uncontested and the balance is stolen. Exfiltration of the same file hands an attacker the signing key plus remedies. Only version 2 restores (`snapshot.rs:182`), so any version bump strands channels the same way 0.1 was stranded.
- Confidence: high (contents stated; the missing backup story is inferred from silence)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — Theft-window protection (penalties, claims) depends entirely on host-side chain watching
- Topics: SR-04.4, SR-04.5
- Evidence: The host supplies "chain lookups for on_spend" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`); the kernel reacts only to a spend it is told about (`protocol/chain.rs:176`), claims `to_local` "after delay" (`chain.rs:364`), republishes claims "after six blocks" (`chain.rs:578`) and takes HTLCs to chain at `delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` before expiry (`protocol/machine.rs:1306`). Detection reliability, and the values of the delays and margins, are not in the material.
- Failure: The watcher host is down, gap-fed, or reorg-blind while a counterparty unilaterally closes with a revoked state. The penalty transaction is never constructed or never confirms inside the `to_local` delay; the counterparty sweeps everything. Missed HTLC expiry loses offered amounts outright.
- Confidence: medium (duty split stated; loss windows inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Money-safety timers (funding, unfunded, proposal, retries) moved out of the protocol into an unspecified host scheduler
- Topics: SR-04.5, SR-02.4
- Evidence: "Hitch keeps failed broadcasts, retry timers and the proposal, funding and unfunded timeouts on its channel document; here they are the host's scheduler, with the constants exported" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:70`, `mod.rs:121`); the router "expects the host to retry a retryable action" (`route.rs:13`). In the port these timers are therefore not durable channel state, unlike the reference. Separately, immature transactions "can be submitted again once their height arrives" (`sidestr-core/src/state.rs:631`) — resubmission is also somebody else's job.
- Failure: A host restart that loses scheduler state never fires the unfunded timeout: the operator's wallet outpoint stays committed to a channel believed dead, which the peer can later fund into a channel nobody watches. A missed re-broadcast of a time-critical claim runs into expiry and loses the HTLC amount.
- Confidence: medium (delegation is explicit; host durability is unstated — the risk is exactly the unstated part)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Published 0.1 contains a known theft-class flaw and its channels are stranded on the flawed version
- Topics: SR-04.1, SR-04.3
- Evidence: "the 0.1 key let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf, which is the flaw this release fixes" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:45`, `:52`); 0.2 refuses version-1 snapshots (`protocol/snapshot.rs:29`, `:182`); "Any channel opened with the earlier version has to be closed with that version" (SR-04 business). `sidestr-hitch` is one of the published crates (SR-01.1) and its releases are logged on the estate board (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:114`).
- Failure: Any third party that adopted published 0.1 holds channels in which a counterparty can cheat via its own penalty path, with no migration: 0.2 will not restore their snapshots and closing still runs the flawed construction. An estate operator opening channels to such peers inherits exposure. Today the estate runs none ("Nobody in the estate runs one"), so this is conditional.
- Confidence: high (all facts stated; external exposure unknown)
- Severity: medium (high the moment any 0.1 channel holds value)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — ADR-0001 declares the reference the compatibility contract while `sidestr-evm` deliberately diverges in durable-state semantics
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-03.2, SR-03.3
- Evidence: "The reference engine is the compatibility contract. A green Rust-only test suite cannot prove wire or consensus parity" (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-...md:27`); but "The crate deliberately commits only an applied block, unlike the reference's `prepare`" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:84`). The oracle suite replays accepted/refused block fixtures (`tests/oracle.rs:1`, `ci.yml:186`), which cannot exercise prepare-time behaviour, so this divergence sits outside the tested contract.
- Failure: If a Rust follower or producer is ever run beside the JS engine, the two engines hold different durable state after a crash mid-prepare; resumed roots and withdrawal timing diverge, and restore refuses on root/config/time mismatch (`sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:305`), producing split-brain or a bricked resume rather than a detected mismatch.
- Confidence: medium (divergence stated; consequence inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Wire-level departure: malformed points are dropped without the reference's reject
- Topics: SR-04.5, SR-01.2
- Evidence: "a point off the curve is refused at deserialisation and dropped without a `reject`, where Hitch replies with a reject at the proof-of-possession check" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:63`). The wire oracle validates what Rust sends (`tests/wire_oracle.rs:185`), not what it declines to send.
- Failure: A reference peer that sends a malformed point waits indefinitely for a reject that never comes — the channel silently stalls until timeouts or a force-close. This is also a standing precedent that the "reference is the contract" rule has tested exceptions.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, SR-04.5)

### F-08 — A peer who withholds the final revoke freezes the whole channel
- Topics: SR-04.2, SR-04.3
- Evidence: Until the last secret reveal, "nothing is forwarded, settled or announced and no further update is accepted or proposed" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:4`, `:27`); set-aside alternatives keep HTLCs "bound until that state number is revoked or the funding is spent" (`protocol/machine.rs:561`).
- Failure: A counterparty proposes an update, receives the ack, then goes silent (malice or crash). The channel is fully frozen — no payments in either direction, in-flight HTLCs unresolvable — and the only exit is an on-chain force-close while HTLC expiries run. No protocol-level timeout for a stalled handshake is described; that is host scheduler territory (F-04).
- Confidence: high (behaviour stated); whether it is a defect rather than a design trade-off is a judgement
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — Router tick can force-close a healthy channel on misread state
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: On tick, the router "settles, fails, or force-closes forwards whose downstream state is lost or held only by a set-aside alternative" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:570`, `:578`); the host is permitted to "restore the previous snapshot if the save fails" (`protocol/mod.rs:30`), i.e. rolled-back host state is an anticipated condition.
- Failure: A host restore to an older snapshot makes a still-live downstream binding look like a set-aside-only alternative; the tick unilaterally closes a channel that was fine. Funds lock for the close delay, on-chain costs are paid, and on a hub this cascades across every channel sharing the misread.
- Confidence: low (mechanism stated; misfire scenario inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Sealed chain documents cannot activate any of the advertised rule surfaces
- Topics: SR-02.4, SR-03.4, SR-01.3
- Evidence: `sidestr:dreamlab`'s document "reaches `containmentDigest` without a `rules` field" (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:5`, `:28`) and without an `evm` rule (`chain.json:1`); SR-02's business section concedes the capabilities are "source capabilities only"; SR-01.3's invariant bars source evidence from promoting activation.
- Failure: Production operation of the existing chains is limited to a plain UTXO ledger. Offering assets, pools, markets or EVM requires sealing a new chain; no migration path for existing UTXOs or users is described anywhere in the material, so any production commitment to these features rests on an activation process that does not yet exist.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, SR-02.4 and SR-03.4)

### F-11 — Crash window between snapshot persist and message send has no specified recovery
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: The host "persists the full recovery boundary atomically before sending anything the call returned, and restores the previous snapshot if the save fails" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:30`). Save failure is covered; a crash after persist but before send, and the resend/idempotence semantics on restart, are not addressed.
- Failure: The host dies between committing a state advance and emitting the peer message. After restart the machine has advanced while the peer has not — the handshake deadlocks (compounding F-08), or a naive resend exercises duplicate-handling paths the JS oracles never ran.
- Confidence: low (inferred from the stated obligation and its boundaries)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — Channel transport rides third-party Nostr relays with no stated redundancy or privacy story
- Topics: SR-04.5
- Evidence: "a host carries each peer message as a kind-23600 Nostr event" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`, `:105`). Nothing is stated about relay redundancy, delivery guarantees, or the visibility of channel traffic to relay operators.
- Failure: A relay outage or censorship between peers stalls the update handshake and the close reactions; channels freeze and hosts must force-close (cost and delay, not direct loss). Relay operators also observe message timing and volume per channel — a business-metadata leak.
- Confidence: low (transport stated; reliance and consequences inferred)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — The compatibility-contract ADR misstates its own oracle set, and the set mutates without an ADR record
- Topics: SR-01.2
- Evidence: ADR-0001 says CI pins "all four reference repositories" (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-...md:27`) while the workflow pins six (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`); Reef was repinned from `648487a` to `2bd3cb8` (`ci.yml:25`) and the teller added at `7c00cea` (`ci.yml:30`) with no corresponding ADR change recorded.
- Failure: An engineer auditing parity from the ADR checks four repos and skips Reef and the teller — exactly the oracles for BIP 21 payment requests and the RFC 8785 bytes that reserve attestations are signed over. A parity regression in the two unlisted oracles ships unnoticed, and the JCS-signed attestation bytes silently change meaning on the next silent repin.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, SR-01.2)

### F-14 — The five files pin their citations to different estate commits, so the evidence base is not one snapshot
- Topics: SR-01 (header), SR-02 (header), SR-03 (header), SR-05 (header)
- Evidence: SR-01, SR-03 and SR-05 verify `agentbox` at `9fd49a9` while SR-02 verifies `6466e39` and `visionflow` at `e49fe42` where SR-01/SR-03/SR-04 use `62d16e0`; SR-02 cites `../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:5`, `:28` against a different agentbox commit than SR-01/SR-03 cite the same lines.
- Failure: Re-verification is ambiguous: an engineer checking SR-02's chain-document claims at the SR-01 commit (or at HEAD) may find different line content, and the custody-boundary claims — "no `rules` field", "no `evm` rule" — cannot be reproduced against a single stated estate state. Any finding that fails re-check taints trust in the rest of the distillation.
- Confidence: high (the hashes differ on the face of the headers)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Stale published-crate count, in two places, one of them unmarked
- Topics: SR-01.1, SR-01.3
- Evidence: The README's crate table lists seven published crates with no teller row (`../sidestr-rs/README.md:41`) versus eleven members and a publishable `webledgers-teller` (`../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:3`, `:42`, `:43`) — marked Drift. Separately and unmarked, the SR-01.3 state diagram itself says "Published: seven reusable crates released" while SR-01.1 says eight.
- Failure: An integrator reading the README (or the activation state machine) builds against a stale crate map — e.g. assumes the teller is private and internally forks a JCS implementation instead of consuming the oracled crate, diverging from the bytes reserve attestations are signed over.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: partially — the README instance is marked (Drift, SR-01.1); the SR-01.3 diagram instance is not

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Sat↔gwei conversion and coinbase funding** (SR-03.1): whether wei amounts not divisible by 1 gwei round, strand as dust, or break conservation, and what funds the coinbase outputs that pay withdrawals. Need `sidestr-evm/src/rule.rs` coinbase-allowance logic.
2. **Mempool durability and replacement policy** (SR-02.4): whether the mempool survives restart and how conflicting/conflicting-witness transactions are replaced. Need `sidestr-core/src/state.rs` persistence code.
3. **The signer set of the live chains** (SR-03 business mentions "the chain's signers"; `chain.json:5` says level 1): who signs blocks, the quorum, and key custody — the actual consolidation-of-trust question for production.
4. **Host and supervisor topology**: whether any canonical host implementation exists, whether snapshot backup is required anywhere, and whether the single agentbox supervisor of producer/mirror/faucet (SR-01.4) is a single point of failure or has HA.
5. **Reserve attestation signer** (SR-05.2, `sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:611` exposes a "BIP-340 signer hook"): who holds that key, its rotation policy, and what freshness rule a future consumer applies to an attestation whose reserve can move after the cited tip.

## sidestr-rs / premortem

Post-mortem, written twelve months after the SR-01–SR-05 distillation. The operator lost a client to a custody-class failure. Everything below is drawn from defects and tensions the distillation itself records at the cited verified commits; where the incident chain is my reconstruction rather than a stated fact, the confidence line says so. The three root causes are ranked by likelihood given the estate's documented trajectory (host unbuilt, crates released and awaiting adoption, live chains still produced by the upstream JavaScript engine).

### R-1 — Legacy Hitch channels can only be closed by the code that carries the revocation flaw
- Topics: SR-04.1, SR-04.3
- Chain of events: A host service was built and channels were opened while `sidestr-hitch` 0.1 was the current kernel. 0.2.0 (1 Oct) replaced the commitment's revocation construction with a two-party key and refuses to restore version-1 snapshots, so the operator kept a 0.1 deployment alive solely to close legacy channels, exactly as the snapshot code demands. In 0.1 the commitment's owner could spend its own revocation leaf; the counterparty broadcast an old commitment and immediately self-spent the penalty path, moving the old balance before any remedy could engage. The client's share was gone before reconciliation, and no 0.2 remedy exists for a 0.1 channel.
- Evidence: the flaw is stated by the authors — "the 0.1 key let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:52`, with `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:45`); "a party could cheat with its own penalty path" (SR-04 business text); v1 snapshots are refused and "such a channel must be closed with 0.1" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:29`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:182`); the corrected construction (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/lib.rs:740`) and its tested invariant (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/tests/oracle.rs:465`) exist only in 0.2.
- Warning signs visible today: a security-motivated breaking release whose only migration path is "keep running the vulnerable version for every channel it opened", in an estate that at documentation time had no host managing any fleet (`../project/docs/adr/ADR-2111-re-sequence-rgb-for-bridged-assets-and-delete-the-host-payment-store.md:95`).
- Confidence: high that the flaw and forced-0.1 closure are real (stated); inferred that a live 0.1 channel existed and was exploited.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The host boundary is where the money sits, and nothing existed to verify a host against it
- Topics: SR-04.3, SR-04.4, SR-04.5
- Chain of events: A host was built to the 0.2 boundary. The kernel performs no IO and returns actions; the host must persist the channel snapshot atomically *before* sending anything the call returned and restore the previous snapshot if the save fails (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:30`). The host broadcast the returned message before the snapshot save completed, then crashed. On restart it restored a snapshot missing the counterparty's latest per-state secret — the secret needed to punish their just-revoked commitment. The counterparty broadcast that old commitment; the host had no remedy; the balance differential was taken. The same window applies to received preimages needed to settle upstream.
- Evidence: snapshot contents include the signing key, revocation basepoint, per-state secrets and preimages (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:22`); storage "must be protected as wallet key material" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:24`); the no-IO contract and host duties (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:105`); time-critical host duties — HTLC to chain at `delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`), claim republication after six blocks (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:578`), scheduler timeouts the host must run (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:70`), funding broadcast and watch (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/opening.rs:178`).
- Warning signs visible today: the entire boundary is unimplemented and unowned — ADR-2111 is "proposed, unimplemented and inactive" (`../project/docs/adr/ADR-2111-...md:95`) and adoption sits under the open board row (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:101`) — while 0.2.0 deliberately widened the host's money-bearing duties.
- Confidence: inferred — every duty named is stated in the material; which duty failed is my reconstruction.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Live chains are produced by the JavaScript engine while clients consume Rust crates; parity is bounded by the fixture corpus
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-01.4
- Chain of events: A client adopted released crates (the Published→Adopted path the board tracks) — e.g. `sidestr-wallet` 0.5.1 for payment handling or `sidestr-core` for validation — against `sidestr:dreamlab`, whose blocks continued to come from the upstream JavaScript producer. A block or payment request arrived whose behaviour falls outside the regenerated fixture corpus — the only drift check CI performs. The Rust consumer diverged from the producer: it either accepted a block the JS engine would refuse or rejected/misparsed one it accepted. The client acted on wrong state or a wrong payment and lost value on reconciliation.
- Evidence: the estate's own position — "a green Rust-only test suite cannot prove wire or consensus parity on its own" (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27`); the live producer is the JS engine at upstream pins (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111`, `../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:117`); drift checking is fixture regeneration and comparison (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:186`); the reference pins themselves move — Reef was repinned `648487a`→`2bd3cb8` (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:25`) — and releases were cut against them (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:114`).
- Warning signs visible today: two engines run side by side with the JS one authoritative and the Rust one released for adoption; the compatibility ladder exists precisely because parity outside the corpus is unproven.
- Confidence: inferred — the mechanism and adoption path are stated; the specific divergent behaviour is not identified in the material.
- Severity: high

### F-01 — Set-aside alternative states bind HTLCs with no exit short of force-close
- Topics: SR-04.2, SR-04.3
- Evidence: a colliding or rejected signed state is "kept as an alternative whose HTLCs stay bound until that state number is revoked or the funding is spent by something else" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:20`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:561`); the only kernelside remedy is the router tick's protective force-close when "only a set-aside state binds" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:570`).
- Failure: a peer sends a signed update, then goes silent; HTLCs remain bound under the set-aside alternative; value is released only by on-chain teardown, or stuck entirely if the host's tick is not running.
- Confidence: medium (inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — The forward record has the same crash window the snapshot contract closes explicitly
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: the router records a ForwardRecord "before the add, host persists it" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:108`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:173`), but only for snapshots does the material state the atomic before-send ordering and restore-on-failure requirement (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:30`).
- Failure: the host broadcasts the downstream `add` before persisting the record, then crashes; on restart the forward is unknown, the downstream preimage is never settled upstream, and the upstream HTLC fails back while the downstream payment stood.
- Confidence: medium (inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — Snapshots carry the signing key and per-state secrets with nothing enforcing their protection
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: the snapshot contains "the signing key, the revocation basepoint and per-state secrets, and preimages" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:22`); "snapshot storage must be protected as wallet key material" is a documentation demand on a host that did not exist (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:24`, `../project/docs/adr/ADR-2111-...md:95`).
- Failure: a host persists version-2 snapshots as ordinary service state, in logs or backups; whoever reads them holds the channel signing key and can spend the channel directly.
- Confidence: medium (inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Off-curve points are dropped without a reject, diverging from the reference's wire behaviour
- Topics: SR-04.5, SR-01.2
- Evidence: "a point off the curve is refused at deserialisation and dropped without a `reject`, where Hitch replies with a reject at the proof-of-possession check; the port records this as a deliberate, tested departure" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:63`); the governing contract says the reference engine defines compatibility (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-...md:27`).
- Failure: in a mixed JS/Rust fleet the JS counterparty waits for a reject that never arrives; the opening stalls until the unfunded timeout (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:70`), and the silent drop also fingerprints Rust peers.
- Confidence: high that the divergence exists (stated and tested); the stall consequence is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-05 — The asset rule authorises mints through policies owned by rules that have not run yet
- Topics: SR-02.1, SR-02.2
- Evidence: the pipeline is fixed as assets, then pool, then markets (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/overlays.rs:1`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/overlays.rs:65`), yet the asset rule "delegates exceptional minting to a policy supplied by the pool and market views" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/assets.rs:160`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/overlays.rs:34`).
- Failure: the policy must preview or re-execute the later rules' transitions to know which mints to excuse; if the preview and the later rule's own execution disagree — or a later stage refuses a transaction whose mint the asset tally already excused for subsequent transactions — conservation can authorise a mint the final block never earns.
- Confidence: low (inferred) — the seam is stated, its resolution is not.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — The refund path pays "half a sat each", unrepresentable for odd collateral
- Topics: SR-02.3
- Evidence: "Refunding → redeem paired outcomes at half a sat each" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/markets.rs:260`, lifecycle at `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/markets.rs:138`), in a crate that elsewhere insists on an integer boundary (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/pool.rs:213`).
- Failure: a market funded with an odd number of sats produces a per-outcome refund that is not an integer; truncation strands a sat or rounding breaks conservation against the JavaScript reference.
- Confidence: low (inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — EVM withdrawal verification is described as checking outputs, not exact amounts
- Topics: SR-03.1, SR-03.2
- Evidence: "Preparation checks both the root and withdrawal payments before state can commit" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:468`); the rule interface exposes "coinbase allowance" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/rule.rs:62`); the invariant says only that a withdrawal is valid when the coinbase "pays the required sidechain satoshis".
- Failure: if the check is presence-or-sufficiency rather than exact equality, a producer's coinbase can over-pay withdrawals, minting overlay balance beyond obligations.
- Confidence: low (inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — Deliberate divergence from the reference's prepare/commit lifecycle
- Topics: SR-03.2, SR-03.4
- Evidence: "The crate deliberately commits only an applied block, unlike the reference's `prepare`" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:84`), while the pinned ethereumjs reference remains the compatibility contract checked by fixture replay (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:186`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/tests/oracle.rs:1`).
- Failure: any sequencing where the reference retains prepare-time state across a refused-then-accepted sequence produces different roots than the Rust crate, outside the fixture corpus.
- Confidence: low (inferred) — the divergence is stated; its consensus effect is not.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — Reserve duplicate-credit refusal is per-attestation; nothing aggregates attestations
- Topics: SR-05.1, SR-05.2, SR-05.3
- Evidence: construction "sorts credit ids through a set, refuses duplicates" within one attestation (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:499`); replay keys are "origin-appropriate" per adapter (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:436`); "neither crate activates a bridge rule" — no consumer exists to ledger consumed credits (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0003-keep-reserve-attestations-origin-neutral-and-private.md:32`).
- Failure: two validly signed attestations over overlapping wallet history, with credits keyed differently (registry re-issue, adapter revision), are each individually well-formed; a future bridge mints twice for one reserve credit.
- Confidence: medium (inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — ADR-0001 understates the oracle set
- Topics: SR-01.2
- Evidence: the ADR says CI pins "all four reference repositories" while the workflow pins six — spec, schema, blaketestnode, Hitch, Reef and teller (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-...md:27` vs `../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`).
- Failure: an operator or auditor reproduces parity against the ADR's stated set, skipping the Reef (BIP 21) and teller (JCS) oracles, and ships a parser or canonicalisation difference that the estate believed was oracle-checked.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-11 — The README's published-crate table is two releases stale
- Topics: SR-01.1
- Evidence: the README lists seven published crates with no teller row (`../sidestr-rs/README.md:41`) while the workspace carries eleven members with `webledgers-teller` publishable (`../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:3`, `../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:43`).
- Failure: an integrator scopes dependencies from the README, misjudges what is published versus private (EVM, reserve), and pins or vendors the wrong artefacts.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-12 — The distillation's own evidence pins disagree across files
- Topics: SR-01.1, SR-02.4
- Evidence: SR-02 was verified at agentbox `6466e39…` / visionflow `e49fe42…`, while SR-01, SR-03 and SR-05 cite agentbox `9fd49a9…` and SR-01/SR-03/SR-04 cite visionflow `62d16e0…` (frontmatter of the five files).
- Failure: cross-repository claims — core behaviour in SR-02 versus the chain document and board state cited elsewhere — were captured at different repository states, so the estate's evidence base can assert non-co-temporal facts about the same chain.
- Confidence: high (visible in the frontmatter)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — Reef oracle repinned between releases; wallet parity bounded to a corpus
- Topics: SR-01.2
- Evidence: Reef was "repinned from `648487a` to `2bd3cb8`" as the oracle for `sidestr-wallet`'s payment requests (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:25`), and `sidestr-wallet` 0.5.1 was released against it (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:114`).
- Failure: a client payment request outside the new pin's fixture corpus parses differently in `sidestr-wallet` than in other ecosystem wallets, and a payment is sent to the wrong recipient or for the wrong amount.
- Confidence: low (inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Multiple rules can grant coinbase allowances; composition is unstated
- Topics: SR-02.1, SR-03.2
- Evidence: `BlockRule` is "the extension seam for rule-local validation, coinbase allowances and applied-block commits" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/rules.rs:534`), and `EvmRule` independently exposes a coinbase allowance for withdrawals (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/rule.rs:62`).
- Failure: with more than one allowance-granting rule installed, additive rather than exclusive composition lets a block's coinbase collect sats beyond any single rule's authorisation.
- Confidence: low (inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Published federation, Nostr, agent and header crates have no reference oracle at all
- Topics: SR-01.1, SR-01.2
- Evidence: CI pins six references covering spec, schema, blaketestnode, Hitch, Reef and teller (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`), while the published set includes `sidestr-nostr`, `sidestr-round`, `sidestr-agent` and `sidestr-header` (`../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:3`, SR-01.1) — none of which maps to a pinned oracle; `sidestr-round` is the federation surface for signers who "order and validate every block" (`../project/agentbox/docs/adr/ADR-2096-sidestr-sidechains-are-the-sole-value-instrument.md:116`).
- Failure: a client adopts the federation or Nostr crates for interaction with the JS-produced chains; with no oracle and no stated contract, wire or ordering divergence there is invisible until a signed or relayed message is mis-handled against the live engine.
- Confidence: high that the coverage gap exists (two stated facts, compared); any specific defect in those crates is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

## solid-pod-rs / critical

### F-01 — The MCP endpoint ships a reproduced-critical WAC bypass, mitigated only by a default-off flag
- Topics: SP-08.14, SP-10.8 (F2), SP-02.3, SP-10.5
- Evidence: SP-08.14 records the README's own status section: "anonymous MCP reads / WAC sidecar bypass" as "a reproduced CRITICAL finding" with the instruction to keep MCP disabled; the stated mitigation is solely that the endpoint is off by default (`--mcp`/`JSS_MCP`, main.rs:292; dispatch at mcp/mod.rs:97, wac_check at tools.rs:83). The full tool catalogue — including `read_acl` and `write_acl` with its own lockout guard (tools.rs:583) — compiles into every server build.
- Failure: an operator enables `--mcp` because it is an advertised, first-class CLI feature; any unauthenticated caller then reads private resources and ACL sidecars through `list_resources`/`read_resource`/`read_acl`, defeating WAC entirely — the audit's finding, reproduced.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (README audit finding quoted in SP-08.14 and SP-10.8 F2)

### F-02 — The entire `/pay/*` money surface is always-on with non-atomic, single-process state
- Topics: SP-07.14, SP-07.15, SP-10.4 (W4), SP-10.8 (F4)
- Evidence: "the whole /pay/* surface is registered with the SAME gating as /pay/.info — always on, with no payments feature flag" (lib.rs:4625); `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK` is process-wide — "Two replicas serving the same pod storage share no lock" (lib.rs:200) — and "the storage API has NO cross-resource CAS primitive" (pay.rs note, SP-07.15); the README audit records "non-atomic payment state" as a reproduced critical finding (F4) and says not to carry value through these routes. Additionally the ADR-2008 Tension: D3 declares MRC20 "retired as a token rail" (adr…md:50-52) while `/pay/.buy` and `/pay/.withdraw` still execute token trades and withdrawals (pay.rs:1362, :1451; adr…md:144-145).
- Failure: any deployment that carries value: two concurrent requests (or two replicas) race the read-mutate-persist over ledger/order book/pool — double-credit, double-spend or a corrupted AMM pool; the replay guard is also process-local, so a retried request at a second replica double-applies.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE SP-07.14 N2; audit F4; ADR-2008 "Tension")

### F-03 — OIDC access tokens are accepted with no audience validation
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-10.6 (D1)
- Evidence: `validate_aud = false` (oidc/mod.rs:812); D1: "aud is NOT validated — only the claim's PRESENCE is enforced… A token minted for another audience verifies here; a deployment needing audience restriction must enforce it above this API" (SolidOidcClaims::aud has no serde default, mod.rs:670).
- Failure: a pod enables OIDC; a legitimately issued token for a *different* relying party or resource server of the same issuer — or one exfiltrated from another service — verifies here and is mapped to a WebID principal: cross-service impersonation inside one issuer's token population.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, ADR-2003, "documented and pinned by tests, not fixed")

### F-04 — COPY exfiltrates the source body with no acl:Read check
- Topics: SP-03.8, SP-10.4 (W6)
- Evidence: "COPY authorises Write on the DESTINATION only — it never checks acl:Read on the SOURCE" (enforce at lib.rs:2565, `get(source)` at :2585); the route is unconditional and un-feature-gated (lib.rs:4778), and the source's ACL sidecar is copied alongside (:2598).
- Failure: a principal granted Write on their own container sends `COPY` with a `Source` header naming a private resource they cannot read; the pod fetches the bytes and writes them — and the victim's ACL — into the attacker-writable destination. A complete confidentiality bypass for any storage-resident resource.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE note in SP-03.8; W6)

### F-05 — Glob GET merges children while ignoring their own stricter ACLs
- Topics: SP-03.9, SP-10.4 (W7)
- Evidence: "the merge gates on the FOLDER only. A child carrying its own, stricter acl sidecar is still included in the merged body" (folder gate lib.rs:2640, filter :2651, merge :2667). Note the parallel: the container `index.html` branch (SP-03.1 gates once on the container path at lib.rs:1230; SP-03.2 then "serve it verbatim", lib.rs:1248) shows no child re-gate either — the material does not say whether code re-checks; flagging it for the engineer to verify as the same class.
- Failure: a public container holds a private child; `GET /container/*` returns the private child's full body to an anonymous caller — the child-level ACL is silently void under glob.
- Confidence: high for the glob path; the index.html variant is low and inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes for glob (DIVERGENCE N2, W7); the index.html question is unmarked

### F-06 — Busy-port shift silently runs a second server instance over the same data root
- Topics: SP-02.7, SP-10.4 (W3, W4), SP-05.18, SP-08.8
- Evidence: on `AddrInUse` the server retries 11 times and proceeds with "warn port busy — shifted listener" (main.rs:155, :162) — it does not fail; base_url is re-read from the shifted port. The same material states the server's correctness depends on single-process state: the NIP-98 replay store is process-local (lib.rs:192), `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK` is process-wide (lib.rs:200), and `InMemoryUserStore` (user_store.rs:70) and `InMemoryEventStore` (relay.rs:225) hold state in RAM.
- Failure: an orchestrator or admin starts a second server against the same FsBackend root; instead of failing it shifts ports and serves. Two processes now write one storage with no shared locks — payment double-applies, credentials replay once per replica, users/events diverge — and profile URLs minted under the shifted base_url go stale after the next restart.
- Confidence: medium — inferred (each fact is stated; the combination is nowhere connected)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the port-shift design and the process-local facts are each stated, but nobody joins them)

### F-07 — Stored body and `.meta` sidecar are two separate renames; a crash leaves a mismatched pair
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-10.4 (W2), SP-10.8 (F5)
- Evidence: "The rename is atomic per file, but the body and its sidecar are two renames — a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair" (fs.rs:111, audit 2026-08-19); the sidecar carries the SHA-256 ETag and content type (fs.rs:107).
- Failure: crash or power loss between the two `atomic_write` calls: the next GET pairs a body with a stale or foreign ETag/content-type. Every ETag-keyed behaviour — the ADR-2002 cache policy (SP-03.12), client revalidation, transcoding keyed on stored content type — is now wrong for that resource, silently.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (README audit F5 / DIVERGENCE SP-06.4; W2)

### F-08 — The anchorer reads a storage failure as "no trail exists" and can destroy the anchoring key state
- Topics: SP-07.12
- Evidence: "load_trail maps every storage read error to `Ok(None)` (trail_store.rs:93-99), so a transient or permissions failure reads identically to 'no trail exists yet' — a durability fault and a genuinely fresh ticker are indistinguishable to the anchorer." `StoredTrail` includes the private key (:37) and `save_trail` writes the new state (:105).
- Failure: a transient read error during anchoring → the anchorer proceeds as fresh and saves a new trail over the old one — the previous anchoring key and chain state are overwritten, breaking the on-chain continuity the block-trail tier exists to prove and stranding UTXOs controlled by the lost key.
- Confidence: high on the stated behaviour; the overwrite consequence is inferred from the load/save semantics shown
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes ("Debt" paragraph under SP-07.12)

### F-09 — NIP-98 replay protection is process-local and restart-lossy; the ADR's second tier does not exist anywhere yet
- Topics: SP-05.4, SP-05.5, SP-10.4 (W3, W5), SP-10.6 (D6)
- Evidence: "A process-local store loses every entry on restart, so each restart reopens the window for one TTL" (SP-05.5 note); "the seam has exactly ONE implementor in this repo. The second tier… lives in nostr-rust-forum, so ADR-2006 stays 'standalone' until that repo pins a version carrying it"; W3: "a second replica shares nothing"; freshness is a two-sided 60 s window (nip98.rs:28), replay TTL 120 s (replay.rs:78).
- Failure: a restart under traffic reopens replay for every credential used in the window — capture a signed request, induce or wait for a restart, replay the write. With ≥2 replicas (F-06) each cache is independent, so one token verifies once *per replica*.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE note SP-05.5; W3; D6) — though the per-replica multiplier is only implied by W3's wording

### F-10 — A default build records zero provenance while the README promises a git-mark on every write; epoch batch-anchor failures are swallowed
- Topics: SP-01.7, SP-07.2 (N3), SP-07.7, SP-10.7 (R1), SP-10.5
- Evidence: server `default = []` (server Cargo.toml:123); "a default build records ZERO provenance marks — the no-op shim" (lib.rs:3650), against README's "every write to a pod is a git-mark commit" (R1) — "Every provenance claim carries a --features git caveat" (SP-07.2 N3). Separately, unmarked: "A failed batch anchor is swallowed — it never fails the write nor the git-mark" (lib.rs:3626), with no receipt field or alert shown for the epoch path.
- Failure: an operator runs the default binary believing the custody claim: nothing is marked, and no error says so. Or enables git with Epoch policy and a persistent mempool failure silently accumulates commits that never anchor — the "provable" chain has holes nobody is told about.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the default-off claim is a marked DOC-DRIFT (R1, INVARIANT SP-01.7); the epoch swallow is stated with no marker

### F-11 — The IdP's "forged identity" audit finding is mitigated by not exposing a router the server partly exposes anyway
- Topics: SP-10.8 (F3), SP-05.18, SP-05.21, SP-02.9, SP-05.17
- Evidence: F3: "forged IdP identity — mitigated by not exposing the optional IdP router." But the server registers `POST /login/password`, `/account/password/reset`, `/account/password/change` (lib.rs:4634, :4654, :4658), and SP-05.21 states the change route is "the IdP library function behind it" (password_change.rs:75); the login route matches the IdP credentials surface (SP-05.18). Also SP-05.18: passkey and schnorr login routes "exist but resolve to the stub implementors" (PasskeyTodo passkey.rs:157; SchnorrTodo schnorr.rs:146).
- Failure: the mitigation assumes the IdP surface is unexposed, but its credential functions are reachable through the server's own routes; if the forgery lives in token/session issuance rather than the optional axum router, it is reachable in a normal deployment. Independently, users choosing passkey/NIP-07 login silently hit null backends.
- Confidence: medium — inferred (the material does not say which surface the forgery is in)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — F3 and the stub backends are marked; the router-exposure contradiction is not

### F-12 — Quota is default-off, entirely inert on the memory backend, and shown enforced only on the PUT flow
- Topics: SP-02.5, SP-02.6, SP-03.3, SP-03.4, SP-03.5, SP-06.6
