- Evidence: `default_quota_bytes` defaults 0 = off (schema.rs:289); "data_root is captured only for the Fs arm… so the git and quota features are inert on a memory pod" (main.rs:256 note). `reserve_quota_for_size` appears in the PUT sequence (lib.rs:2354); the POST and PATCH sequences (SP-03.4, SP-03.5) enumerate auth, slug, lockout-guard and provenance steps but show no quota step — SP-06.6 shows a generic "LDP write handler" calling reserve, so this may be shared and the diagrams simply omit it.
- Failure: a memory-backend pod has no quota enforcement at all; on an Fs pod, if POST/PATCH truly bypass reservation, any Append-capable principal creates unbounded resources via POST — the resource-exhaustion bound fails on the primary creation verb.
- Confidence: high for default-off and memory-inert (stated); medium, inferred for the POST/PATCH gap (absence in the diagrams)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the memory-inert line is a plain note; the PUT-only pattern is nowhere flagged)

### F-13 — Group-based WAC grants silently never fire: the shipped resolver is NoGroupMembership
- Topics: SP-04.15, SP-04.1, SP-04.7
- Evidence: "NoGroupMembership — always false, the server default" (evaluator.rs:28); N1: "an unwired GroupMembership resolver… makes every acl:agentGroup clause deny silently — a server that forgets to pass a real resolver into evaluate_access_ctx_with_registry loses group grants without any error" (evaluator.rs:116, :139).
- Failure: an owner shares a container via `acl:agentGroup` (the standard WAC group form); every group member receives a bare 403 and nothing in logs, metrics or the denial shape says why — sharing appears broken with no diagnostic path.
- Confidence: medium — the material names NoGroupMembership "the server default" but does not show the server's construction site
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated inside an INVARIANT note as a caution; not flagged as a defect of the shipped server)

### F-14 — The pin matrix references a release this repo does not record, and breaking changes already shipped strand consumers
- Topics: SP-09.7, SP-10.7 (R4)
- Evidence: the workspace is 0.5.0-alpha.11 and the CHANGELOG's newest entry is alpha.11 (Cargo.toml:15; CHANGELOG.md:9) — yet agentbox's Nix pin is v0.5.0-alpha.12, "AHEAD of this topic's checked-out alpha.11 HEAD", and the forum pins `=0.5.0-alpha.12` "gated on that version being PUBLISHED to crates.io"; VisionClaw sits on 0.4.0-alpha.15 while the ecosystem doc still says consumers pin 0.4 (R4). alpha.11 shipped breaking removals — the public `AppState` deposit field, `--deposit-txo-standin`, `credit`/`debit` → `pub(crate)` (CHANGELOG.md:70-71, :98-101) — and agentbox's deposit proxy "will surface the new 501" (adr…md:151-152).
- Failure: two consumers build from a version whose contents this checkout cannot verify (no alpha.12 in the release history shown); consumers on the 0.4 line run a security surface that predates the fixes this material records; the agentbox payments path breaks on its next binary bump.
- Confidence: high for the facts; medium, inferred that alpha.12's provenance is unverifiable from this commit
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open/Debt bullets in SP-09.7; DOC-DRIFT R4)

### F-15 — Browser-origin protections default to "ignore Origin entirely" plus echo-any-origin CORS with credentials
- Topics: SP-04.8, SP-01.6 (O1), SP-06.10, SP-02.3
- Evidence: `acl-origin` is in no default feature set — "a stock build ignores the Origin header entirely and a cross-origin write is gated only by the agent's credential" (Cargo.toml:168; evaluator.rs:352); CORS: "an empty allowed_origins list means every Origin is echoed back — a local-dev default, not a production one" (lib.rs:3087), and wildcard+credentials "degrades to echoing the concrete Origin" with `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials` (cors.rs:238-240, :174).
- Failure: any browser-carried credential flow against a default deployment (password login/session, dev-bearer): every website can issue credentialed cross-site requests and the pod answers with the caller's own Origin plus Allow-Credentials — the CSRF defence the WAC origin gate exists to provide is compiled out and the CORS layer confirms instead of refusing.
- Confidence: medium — inferred (depends on whether any ambient browser credential is actually in play; NIP-98 signatures are not browser-ambient)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE SP-04.8 O1; "local-dev default" note SP-06.10 N2)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. The substance of the MCP critical finding — what exactly `wac_check` (tools.rs:83) gets wrong and whether any post-alpha.11 commit fixes it; I need the audit report and the MCP tool source before treating `--mcp` as anything but disqualifying.
2. Whether POST, PATCH and the MCP write tools actually bypass quota reservation — the PUT sequence and the generic SP-06.6 handler are not enough; I need the handler bodies around lib.rs:1517/1604 and tools.rs:204/:231.
3. Whether the container `index.html` branch re-checks the child's ACL (lib.rs:1248), and which IdP surface the "forged identity" finding lives in relative to the server's `/login/password` wiring — both need source and tests.
4. Cross-process behaviour of FsBackend: whether any file lock prevents two server processes on one root, and whether the shifted-port path (SP-02.7) can occur under the real deployment mechanism (Nix/systemd/orchestrator configs).
5. The provenance and contents of 0.5.0-alpha.12 (agentbox's pin) and the delta between VisionClaw's 0.4.0-alpha.15 and the security fixes recorded here (P0-1..P0-4, replay capacity refusal, sidecar elevation) — I need the crates.io index, the repo history beyond this commit, and VisionClaw's changelog.

## solid-pod-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem — solid-pod-rs production incident

All findings below are drawn from the diagrams-as-code record verified at commit `93e2200218fad37927df16a1b7784c93c475670d`. No timeline is asserted beyond what that record states. Every item must be re-verified against the code and its tests before action; where a chain requires facts the record does not contain, the confidence line says so.

The material's own audit section (SP-10.8) already records three reproduced critical findings as open at this commit — non-atomic payment state, anonymous MCP reads/WAC sidecar bypass, and non-atomic filesystem writes — plus the instruction "do not carry value through the payment routes until the findings are fixed." The three most likely root causes follow from those.

---

### R-1 — Payment state is non-atomic behind a process-wide lock; concurrent mutation double-applies or loses ledger writes
- Topics: SP-07.15, SP-07.14, SP-07.18, SP-10.4 (W4), SP-10.8 (F4), SP-09.7
- Chain of events: 1) The whole `/pay/*` surface is registered unconditionally, with no feature flag to disable it (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4625`, SP-07.14 N2). 2) A handler acquires `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK`, checks replay, mutates ledger/order/pool, persists, records replay (SP-07.15). 3) The lock is process-wide and the storage API has "NO cross-resource CAS primitive," so the guard must be held across the whole mutation — and serializes nothing outside one process. 4) Two concurrent requests in one process, or a second process over the same pod store (a second instance started against the same `data_root` silently binds a shifted port rather than failing — SP-02.7, `solid-pod-rs-server/src/main.rs:162`), interleave read-mutate-persist. 5) The stored-number ledger diverges from reality: an outpoint credited twice, or a withdrawal executed against a balance that a concurrent debit had already spent. 6) `/pay/.buy` and `/pay/.withdraw` remain live against that stored number (SP-07.18 Tension bullet), so the divergence converts directly into value paid out.
- Evidence: `solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:200` (process-wide `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK`); SP-07.15 note "the lock is PROCESS-wide. Two replicas serving the same pod storage share no lock, and the README's audit line records 'non-atomic payment state' as a reproduced critical finding"; SP-10.8 F4 "the process lock narrows it, does not close it"; ADR-2008 names fixing non-atomic payment state as a precondition of the port and nothing states what replaces the lock (`adr/ADR-2008-port-bitcoin-tx-to-rust-bitcoin-and-make-the-web-ledger-a-chain-view.md:72`); SP-09.10 NG4 — no CI job exercises deployed behaviour or crosses a restart.
- Warning signs visible today: the reproduced critical finding sits in the README itself; `.buy`/`.withdraw` still trade against the stored ledger while D4/D5/D7 are "not built" (SP-07.18); agentbox already proxies deposits into this surface (SP-09.7 debt bullet).
- Confidence: high that the non-atomicity is real (reproduced per the README); the two-process trigger via the busy-port shift is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — MCP enabled in production: the reproduced anonymous-read / WAC sidecar bypass exfiltrates private pod data
- Topics: SP-08.14, SP-08.15, SP-10.8 (F2), SP-10.5, SP-02.3
- Chain of events: 1) An operator enables `--mcp` / `JSS_MCP` to serve agent clients — it is a single default-off flag (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/main.rs:292`). 2) The README's status section records "anonymous MCP reads / WAC sidecar bypass" as a reproduced CRITICAL finding (SP-08.14 note; SP-10.8 F2). 3) An unauthenticated `tools/call` reaches `read_resource` or `read_acl`; the per-tool `wac_check` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/mcp/tools.rs:83`) does not stop the recorded bypass — unlike the LDP path, where reading an `.acl` sidecar elevates to `acl:Control` of the protected resource (SP-04.5). 4) A private resource's body, and the authorization graph of resources the caller does not Control, are returned to an anonymous agent. 5) The harvest is indistinguishable in the metrics: only SSRF blocks and dotfile denials are counted (SP-10.12 N3).
- Evidence: SP-08.14 note (README records the finding and says keep MCP disabled); `solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:370` (SP-10.8 F2 citation); endpoint registration gated only on `state.mcp_enabled` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4634`); the sidecar-elevation rule the MCP path apparently fails to apply: `solid-pod-rs/src/wac/mod.rs:252`.
- Warning signs visible today: the finding is reproduced and open; the sole mitigation recorded is the default-off flag, i.e. operational, not a code fix; no test is cited that pins the MCP path to the SP-04.5 sidecar rule.
- Confidence: high that the bypass exists (reproduced per the README); that the operator enabled `--mcp` twelve months on is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Storage writes are two renames, not one: a crash mid-write leaves a mismatched body/metadata pair and an unmarked resource
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-07.2, SP-10.3, SP-10.4 (W1, W2), SP-10.8 (F5), SP-09.10
- Chain of events: 1) A PUT under load enters `FsBackend::put`; the `.meta.json` sidecar is atomic-written first, then the body — two separate rename operations (`solid-pod-rs/src/storage/fs.rs:111`, sequence at `fs.rs:123`/`:138`). 2) The process crashes or is killed between the two renames. 3) The stored pair is now mismatched — the audit's own wording: "a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair" (SP-06.4 DIVERGENCE note); the 2026-08-19 audit records "filesystem writes violate the advertised atomic storage contract" (SP-10.8 F5). 4) Concurrent readers in the window got the old pair (by design), but post-crash readers get the corrupted pair: wrong ETag/content-type against the body. 5) Independently, a crash between `storage.put` and `git_mark_write` leaves the resource stored with no provenance mark at all (W1, `solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:1478`) — the receipt makes the window observable, it does not close it (SP-10.3 note: "The three steps are still not atomic").
- Evidence: `solid-pod-rs/src/storage/fs.rs:111` (atomic_write, two renames); SP-06.4 note quoting the 2026-08-19 audit; SP-10.4 W1 and W2; SP-10.8 F5; SP-09.10 NG4 — no CI job "crosses a restart," so the crash path is never exercised anywhere in the pipeline.
- Warning signs visible today: the audit finding is open at this commit; graceful shutdown exists for signals (SP-02.1) but nothing covers crash/kill timing; the record explicitly refuses to call the contract satisfied.
- Confidence: high that the atomicity violation is real (reproduced per the audit); the specific crash timing in production is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — COPY authorises Write on the destination only and never checks acl:Read on the source
- Topics: SP-03.8, SP-10.4 (W6)
- Evidence: the sequence shows a single `enforce_write_ctx` on the destination (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:2565`) and the note states the divergence outright; SP-10.4 W6 cites `solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:2563`.
- Failure: a principal with Write on any writable destination copies a private source resource it could never GET; the body is returned in the 201 path via `put(dest, body, …)` — a direct confidentiality disclosure that needs no exploit.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Glob GET gates only on the folder; children with stricter ACL sidecars are still merged into the response
- Topics: SP-03.9, SP-10.4 (W7)
- Evidence: the read gate runs on the folder (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:2640`), children are filtered only by RDF file type (`:2651`), and the note states "a child carrying its own, stricter acl sidecar is still included in the merged body."
- Failure: `GET /container/*` on a readable folder concatenates the bodies of private children into one `text/turtle` response — bulk exfiltration of every private RDF child in one request.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — NIP-98 replay protection is process-local; a restart or a second replica reopens the 60 s replay window
- Topics: SP-05.4, SP-05.5, SP-10.4 (W3), SP-10.6 (D6)
- Evidence: the store is process-local (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:192`); "a process-local store loses every entry on restart, so each restart reopens the window for one TTL" (SP-05.5 note); the freshness window is two-sided 60 s (`solid-pod-rs/src/auth/nip98.rs:28`); the `ReplayStore` seam has exactly one implementor, so no shared tier exists yet (`solid-pod-rs/src/auth/replay_store.rs:90`, D6).
- Failure: an attacker who captures one `Authorization: Nostr` header (a DELETE, or a `/pay` write, which authenticate via `require_did` — SP-07.14 N) replays it against a second instance over the same storage, or immediately after a restart, and it verifies as fresh.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — A default server build records zero provenance marks while the README claims every write is a git-mark commit
- Topics: SP-01.7, SP-07.2, SP-10.5, SP-10.7 (R1)
- Evidence: server default features are empty (`crates/solid-pod-rs-server/Cargo.toml:123`); with that default `git_mark_write` compiles to the no-op shim (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:3650`); SP-07.2 N3: "a default build records ZERO marks. Every provenance claim carries a --features git caveat"; SP-10.7 R1 records the README claim as drift.
- Failure: an operator deploys the default binary, repeats the README's custody claim to a client, and at dispute time there is no mark, no commit SHA, no anchor — the custody promise was never being kept, silently.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE / DOC-DRIFT)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — The mempool endpoint defaults to the public mempool.space testnet4 explorer, with one URL and no fallback
- Topics: SP-07.11, SP-07.9, SP-10.6 (D7)
- Evidence: `DEFAULT_MEMPOOL_URL` is the public testnet4 explorer (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/mempool.rs:56`); DIVERGENCE: "ONE URL, no fallback chain" (D7); deposit verification requires a live mempool UTXO read (`solid-pod-rs/src/mrc20.rs:882`).
- Failure: an unconfigured deployment anchors provenance to — and verifies deposits against — testnet4: legitimate mainnet deposits fail verification and are rejected, and any anchors written are worthless as proof; a mempool outage also halts deposit verification entirely, since there is no second endpoint.
- Confidence: high that the default and single-URL behaviour are as stated; that a deployment ran unconfigured is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: high

### F-06 — trail_store maps every storage read error to "no trail exists", so the anchorer can fork a live state chain
- Topics: SP-07.12
- Evidence: the Debt bullet: `load_trail` maps every read error to `Ok(None)` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/trail_store.rs:93-99`), making "a durability fault and a genuinely fresh ticker … indistinguishable to the anchorer."
- Failure: a transient or permissions error during `load_trail` makes the anchorer believe no trail exists; it initialises from genesis and anchors on a divergent chain while the real trail (and its UTXO state) still exists — deposit verification and balance state then disagree with what was anchored.
- Confidence: high that the error-mapping is as stated; the fork consequence follows from the anchor/verify flow in SP-07.12/SP-07.9 and is partly inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-07 — Access-token aud is not validated; only the claim's presence is enforced
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-10.6 (D1), ADR-2003
- Evidence: `validate_aud = false` (`solid-pod-rs/src/oidc/mod.rs:812`); D1: "a token minted for another audience verifies here; a deployment needing audience restriction must enforce it above this API."
- Failure: a token issued to a different resource server is accepted by this pod's OIDC path; actions are authorised under another service's token issuance — cross-service token confusion attributable to the wrong principal.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE / ADR-2003 documented)
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — Discovery advertises ES256/RS256 only while the verifier also dispatches EdDSA — invariant 6 is broken in the shipped direction
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-10.1, SP-10.6 (D3)
- Evidence: discovery list at `solid-pod-rs/src/oidc/mod.rs:184`; the verifier dispatches EdDSA (`solid-pod-rs/src/oidc/mod.rs:763`, and `:549` per SP-10.1); SP-10.1 N calls invariant 6 "currently BROKEN."
- Failure: any peer that honours this pod's discovery metadata will reject or mishandle tokens this pod accepts — a federation/authorisation mismatch that surfaces only when two Solid-OIDC deployments interoperate.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE; named broken invariant)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — The acl:origin gate is in no default feature set; a stock build ignores the Origin header entirely
- Topics: SP-04.8, SP-01.6 (O1), SP-10.5
- Evidence: `acl-origin` is not in any default set (`crates/solid-pod-rs/Cargo.toml:168`); the Ignored state note: "a stock build ignores the Origin header entirely and a cross-origin write is gated only by the agent's credential."
- Failure: a leaked bearer credential — the HMAC dev token (`verify_dev_bearer`, SP-05.1) or a forge `f1.*` push token (SP-08.11), neither of which is single-use — can be exercised from any origin with no second factor; the app-level origin defence documented in WAC simply is not compiled in.
- Confidence: high that the default is off; the exploitation chain via a leaked bearer is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — agentbox still proxies TXO deposits to a route the server deleted; user funds can arrive with no credit path
- Topics: SP-09.7, SP-07.17
- Evidence: "agentbox's payments route still proxies {txo_uri, amount_sats} to this server's /pay/.deposit and will surface the new 501 once its build exercises an alpha.11-or-newer binary" (`adr/ADR-2008-…md:151-152`); agentbox's Nix pin is already `v0.5.0-alpha.12` (SP-09.7 AB); non-MRC20 deposits now get 501 unconditionally with no payment state written (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:467`).
- Failure: a user completes agentbox's deposit flow — funds may already be sent toward a derived address — and the credit call returns 501; the software has no path to credit those funds, leaving manual reconciliation or loss.
- Confidence: high that both facts hold at the recorded pins; that a user's funds strand in between is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-11 — VisionClaw consumes 0.4.0-alpha.15, a pre-pivot line still carrying the hand-rolled Bitcoin encoding the port deleted
- Topics: SP-09.7, SP-07.10, ADR-2008
- Evidence: SP-09.7 VC bullet: "VisionClaw consumes solid-pod-rs 0.4.0-alpha.15 — the pre-pivot line"; SP-07.10 N: `tagged_hash`, `xonly_of`, the hand-rolled bech32m encoder etc. were DELETED in the rust-bitcoin port — i.e. that code is what the 0.4.0 line runs.
- Failure: whatever motivated replacing the hand-rolled BIP-341/340/bech32m code is live in the largest embedded consumer; an encoding defect there sends value to a wrong taproot address irreversibly.
- Confidence: medium — the pin and the deleted-code inventory are stated; a concrete defect in the old code is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt/Open)
- Severity: high

### F-12 — The forged-IdP-identity audit finding is open; its only mitigation is "do not expose the optional IdP router"
- Topics: SP-10.8 (F3), SP-05.16, SP-05.17
- Evidence: SP-10.8 F3: "forged IdP identity — mitigated by not exposing the optional IdP router."
- Failure: an operator wires the axum-binder IdP router (`solid-pod-rs-idp/src/axum_binder.rs:92`) for password or SSO login; the forging path the audit reproduced is reachable and an attacker authenticates as a victim WebID, inheriting everything WAC grants that identity.
- Confidence: medium — the finding is stated as reproduced, but the material gives no mechanism or file:line for it.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-13 — The server's default GroupMembership is a no-op, so acl:agentGroup grants silently deny
- Topics: SP-04.15, SP-10.12
- Evidence: "NoGroupMembership — always false, the server default" (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/evaluator.rs:28`); N1: a server that forgets to pass a real resolver "loses group grants without any error."
- Failure: a pod authorises a group via `acl:agentGroup`; every member is denied with no error, no log shape called out, and no counter (SP-10.12 N3 — only SSRF and dotfile events are counted) — a silent availability/authorisation failure indistinguishable from a hostile ACL.
- Confidence: high that the default denies; impact is a denial, not a leak.
- Marked by authors: no (flagged as an invariant warning, not as drift/debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Container GET serves index.html under the container's policy, and .prov.ttl reads resolve as ordinary reads — the child's own ACL is not consulted on these paths
- Topics: SP-03.2, SP-07.6, SP-04.3, SP-04.5
- Evidence: the container branch enforces read on the container path, then "try index.html child — serve it verbatim" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:1246-1248`); "the .prov.ttl sidecar GET is an ORDINARY LDP read" (SP-07.6); the sidecar-elevation rule covers `.acl`/`.meta` suffixes only (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/mod.rs:217`).
- Failure: a private `index.html` (restrictive sidecar) inside a public container is disclosed by a container GET; a private resource's `.prov.ttl` — authors, timestamps, commit SHAs — inherits the parent's default policy and leaks existence and writer metadata. This is the same child-sidecar class SP-03.9 flags for the glob path, on two paths the record does not flag.
- Confidence: low to medium — the gate placements are stated, but the absence of a child-ACL check in these branches is inferred from the diagrams' order, which the diagrams do assert.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — A failed epoch batch anchor is swallowed; a whole epoch's commits can lack on-chain proof with no signal
- Topics: SP-07.7, SP-07.3, SP-10.3
- Evidence: "A failed batch anchor is swallowed — it never fails the write nor the git-mark" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:3626`); Epoch policy is the mode that defers anchoring to the accumulator (SP-07.3).
- Failure: a mempool outage coinciding with an epoch fill leaves every commit in that epoch permanently unanchored; the git-marks stand and the writes report success, so the gap is discovered only when someone tries to present the on-chain proof and it does not exist.
- Confidence: medium — the swallowing is stated; whether the pending commits are retained or dropped on failure is not stated in the material.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

## visionclaw / critical

### F-01 — GPU push broadcast sends every node's live telemetry to every connected client, bypassing the pubkey ownership filter
- Topics: VC-13.3, VC-03.9, VC-13.6
- Evidence: The ownership drop-set filter (`compute_private_opaque_ids` / `apply_drop_set`, position_updates.rs:745) runs only on the polling path and initial sync. VC-13.3 states the ClientCoordinatorActor push path uses a *separate* per-client node-id filter whose default is disabled ("client.filter.enabled == false … try_send(SendToClientBinary(unfiltered_binary clone))", client_coordinator_actor.rs:371) and explicitly: "the pubkey visibility drop-set filter … gates the polling path … and initial state sync, **not this ClientCoordinatorActor broadcast**". VC-03.9 confirms client_filter.rs is a different (quality/authority) filter and "position_updates.rs is the real site". The broadcast frame is a full V3 record per node — id, position, velocity, sssp_distance, cluster, anomaly, community, centrality (VC-14.1).
- Failure: Any authenticated user — a Viewer with the lowest session — receives a 25 fps stream of positions and analytics for *other users' private nodes*, because the UpdateNodePositions→BroadcastPositions fan-out never consults ownership. The filter the system advertises as fail-closed (ADR-2003) protects only the poll path; a client that simply registers gets the leak anyway. In dev builds that allow anonymous /wss (VC-13.1), an unauthenticated client gets it too.
- Confidence: high (the path separation is stated verbatim; the leak framing is the direct consequence)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (presented as an INVARIANT description of the architecture, not flagged as a defect)

### F-02 — The docs contradict each other on whether a promoted dev-auth binary binds; the dev-runtime artifact (dev-auth, full bypass, stubbed hygiene) binds and serves
- Topics: VC-08.11, VC-09.4, VC-09.3, VC-09.7
- Evidence: VC-08.11 claims "A dev-auth binary promoted to production refuses to bind at all." VC-09.4 states the opposite for the artifact the dev launcher actually builds: profile dev-runtime "inherits release but keeps debug-assertions" (Cargo.toml:301-304, wrapper exports `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_RUNTIME_DEBUG_ASSERTIONS=true`, rust-backend-wrapper.sh:45) "so this assertion **warns and continues instead of refusing to bind**" (`may_bind_listener()` security_profile.rs:388 lets debug builds bind). In that same debug binary `enforce_release_env_hygiene` is the no-op stub (main.rs:169), `dev_full_bypass_active` is compiled in, and RBAC report mode is implicitly acknowledged (VC-09.7: "a debug build acknowledges implicitly") — i.e. auth enforcement can be turned off with no dated ack.
- Failure: Someone promotes `target/dev-runtime/visionclaw-server` (an optimized artifact, easy to mistake for release) into a production image. It boots, warns, and binds. With `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE` present every request is granted dev-admin (ADR-2039); without it, `?token=` WS carriers, insecure-defaults paths and unacknowledged RBAC report mode are all live. The only guard is the ADR-2037 CI grep over entrypoint scripts, which does not inspect artifacts.
- Confidence: high on the contradiction and mechanism (both statements are verbatim); the production occurrence is conditional on a deployment mistake
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (each side is presented as an INVARIANT; the conflict between them is unflagged)

### F-03 — `/ws/client-messages` accepts any non-empty string as a token; contents are never verified
- Topics: VC-04.25
- Evidence: "the token's CONTENTS are never verified against NostrService, only its presence, so any non-empty string passes" (client_messages_handler.rs:131-142). The in-code comment (:114-116) claims it merely "allows but logs unauthenticated connections". Once connected, the actor drains `app_state.client_message_rx` every 100 ms and pushes `{type:client_message, content, timestamp, session_id, agent_id}` frames to the client.
- Failure: Anyone who can reach the endpoint subscribes to the outbound client-message stream by sending `Authorization: Bearer x`. Whatever the hub carries — session ids and agent traffic — is disclosed without any credential check. This also diverges from the ADR-2090/2058 posture applied to `/ws/mcp-relay` and `/multi-mcp/ws`, which resolve tokens against `NostrService::get_session`.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, VC-04.25)

### F-04 — NIP-98 replay cache is process-local and fail-closed at capacity: replays work across replicas, and ~100k signed events knock out all authentication
- Topics: VC-03.4
- Evidence: Replay admission is per-process: "Process-local scope — no cross-replica replay protection (SECURITY-profiles.md)" (nip98.rs:215 `Mutex<HashMap>`). Quotas: 1000 events/pubkey per 120 s TTL (nip98.rs:178); when `cache.len() >= REPLAY_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES=100_000` after prune, admission returns `ReplayCacheFull`, surfaced as "generic auth failure upstream". Claims occur only after signature verification, but an attacker controls their own keys and request shapes.
- Failure: (a) Two replicas behind a balancer: a captured kind-27235 event, rejected as replayed on replica A, succeeds on replica B within its 60 s freshness window — the single-use guarantee the identity chain claims does not survive horizontal scaling. (b) ~100 nostr keys × 1000 fresh self-signed events in 120 s (~833 req/s) fills the cache and every login — including admins' — fails until entries expire. Fail-closed turns a resource bound into an auth outage.
- Confidence: high on both mechanisms; the DoS arithmetic is inferred from the stated quotas
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — the process-local limitation is stated as an invariant note; the cache-full fail-closed DoS is not flagged

### F-05 — Four-plus distinct read/write paths to "the graph" with no single write gateway, and no cross-store transaction or restore correctness
- Topics: VC-04.10, VC-04.20, VC-01.1
- Evidence: VC-04.10 DIVERGENCE: graph_state_handler mutates via `graph_adapter` CQRS handlers, api_handler::graph reads via `graph_query_handlers`, bots/files write via `graph_service_addr.send(AddNodesFromMetadata)` — "three distinct graph-mutation paths coexist … no single write gateway (BASELINE persistence closeout)". VC-04.20 adds a "FOURTH graph-data read path" (schema_handler's directly injected `Addr<GraphStateActor>`) and "a fifth distinct app_data binding onto the same graph". VC-01.1 DIVERGENCE: shared Oxigraph ownership "establishes no cross-store transaction, no actor reload consistency, no restore correctness".
- Failure: A node added via `POST /api/graph/nodes` is invisible to `/api/graph/data` because the two paths hold different store references; a crash mid-write leaves Oxigraph and SQLite divergent with nothing to reconcile them; a restore from backups has no established procedure to prove the stores are consistent. Users see silently contradictory graphs depending on route; there is no way to reason about which view is authoritative.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, three separate markers)

### F-06 — The human-rationale gate keys on the proposing agent's self-declared risk tier, and a not-yet-persisted case bypasses it entirely
- Topics: VC-05.7
- Evidence: OPEN note (ADR-2110 scope note, enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:332-338): "the tier consulted is the tier RECORDED on the case, which today is the proposing agent's own declared risk_tier. PRD FR3's effective_tier … has not landed. **A case with no row yet carries no tier and the gate does not fire**." Tier resolution at :393 (`declared_tier_of(proposal_json)`), gate at :406 (`check_rationale`), read-once at :516.
- Failure: An agent (or an X-Agent-Key holder) submits a high-impact change declaring `risk_tier: low`, or decides on a case id before the proposal row exists: the server-side rationale requirement — whose entire point is that the client-side gate "is a courtesy" — never fires, and the decision records with no human rationale. The governance control is enforced against the party it is supposed to constrain.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-07 — Decision attribution on the enrichment path comes from the request body under one shared static key, contradicting the ADR-048 invariant claimed for the sibling route
- Topics: VC-05.7, VC-05.6, VC-09.10
- Evidence: VC-05.6 INVARIANT (ADR-048, decision_handler.rs:145): "deciding principal is auth.pubkey, **NEVER a body field**, a caller cannot self-assert another agent's decision." VC-05.7's shared core does the opposite for the same store: "attribution — 64-hex broker_pubkey -> did:nostr+kg URN, else unattributed", with broker_pubkey a body field (decide_as_operator only falls back to the operator "if body has no broker_pubkey"). The route is gated solely by `X-Agent-Key == VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY` — one static secret shared across enrichment decisions, image-gen and canary writes (enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:171, image_gen_handler.rs:84, liveness_harness_handler.rs:36).
- Failure: Any holder of the shared key — every agent service, plus the canary and image-gen integrations — can record enrichment decisions attributed to an arbitrary victim pubkey. The broker inbox then projects those rows indistinguishably from genuine ones, and the audit trail asserts a principal that never acted. The invariant the system claims as a cornerstone holds on one route and is structurally violated on the route writing the same table.
- Confidence: high on the facts; the spoofing scenario is inferred but follows directly
- Severity: medium (high the moment the shared key leaks or any agent turns hostile)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — `PUT /api/settings/*` returns success when the SQLite persist fails; settings silently revert on restart
- Topics: VC-06.3, VC-06.1
- Evidence: After the actor update and GPU propagation, "H->>DB: set_setting('physics', …)" then "alt persist fails / DB-->>H: warn only — **the response still succeeds**" (settings_routes.rs:648-654, 200 at :672). No marker on this branch. Related: the hot-reload watcher is disabled because it caused database deadlocks (VC-06.1 DIVERGENCE), so the DB write is the only durability path.
- Failure: An operator tunes physics/security-relevant settings, sees 200 OK, and the live process behaves accordingly. The process restarts (deploy, crash, OOM) and the settings silently revert to the last successfully persisted snapshot. Because the only failure signal is a warn log, the divergence can persist for weeks before anyone correlates "the server keeps forgetting our changes".
- Confidence: high (branch is explicitly diagrammed for physics; other PUT handlers in the same file plausibly share the shape — that generalisation is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — ValidationMiddleware is never wired, but `/api/validation/stats` advertises input sanitization, size caps and injection prevention as active features
- Topics: VC-03.1, VC-04.19
- Evidence: VC-03.1 DOC-DRIFT: ValidationMiddleware (validation.rs:124) with MAX_REQUEST_SIZE 1 MiB (:22), MAX_ONTOLOGY_SIZE 10 MiB (:19), MAX_STRING_LENGTH 100 KiB (:25) is "defined and unit-tested … but NOT in this .wrap() chain — grep across src/main.rs finds no .wrap(ValidateInput::...) call". VC-04.19: the free-function validators are "reachable only via this explicit dry-run test route, never automatically on the real request path" — yet GET /api/validation/stats (validation_handler.rs:369) returns a "static capability descriptor" listing input_sanitization, schema_validation, rate_limiting, xss/sql-injection/path-traversal prevention.
- Failure: The endpoint's security-metadata response claims protections that no request receives. Oversized bodies hit handlers subject only to whatever default limits exist (unstated), and the sanitisation the stats endpoint certifies never runs. Any audit or automated compliance check that reads /api/validation/stats is told the system has input hardening it demonstrably lacks.
- Confidence: high on the mismatch; actual DoS impact depends on extractor defaults not shown
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT in VC-03.1; described plainly in VC-04.19)

### F-10 — In a release build, a successfully header-authenticated /wss session never gets its identity written into the session actor; downstream behaviour is unresolved
- Topics: VC-03.2, VC-13.2
- Evidence: DIVERGENCE (VC-03.2): the Authorization-Bearer check gates the 401/allow decision but "never writes its NostrUser into ws_server"; `ws_server.pubkey/.is_power_user` are set only by a second, redundant query-string extraction that is "hardcoded None in release" (http_handler.rs:328-341, :376-380). The authors add: "not yet confirmed whether downstream identity/visibility depends on this field or is sourced elsewhere."
- Failure: Best case, every header-authenticated release client is treated as anonymous until it sends the post-connect `authenticate` message — so drag/pin (which require `act.pubkey.is_some()`, VC-13.2/VC-16.1) fails with confusing rejections and the fail-closed visibility filter hides the user's own private nodes. Worst case, some downstream consumer reads pubkey from a different, unverified source. The system ships with an unresolved question mark over its WS identity propagation.
- Confidence: medium (the divergence is stated; which consequence materialises depends on code not shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-11 — `/pay/{resource}` debits the balance, then never delivers anything; deposits are a 501 stub
- Topics: VC-04.27, VC-09.13
- Evidence: "resource proxying is a STUB — this confirms and charges but never forwards to the underlying resource handler (pay_handler.rs:503-505)"; `POST /pay/.deposit` returns 501 "Contact the server operator for manual funding" (:487-492); the routes are mounted unconditionally and go live the moment `PAY_ENABLED=true` (VC-09.13). ADR-2111, which would delete the whole ledger, is still "PROPOSED, NOT LIVE".
- Failure: An operator flips `PAY_ENABLED` expecting the advertised L402 flow. Every resource call debits sats from a ledger that can only be funded manually, and no resource is ever proxied — a direct financial/custody incident against users, plus a support burden since the only remediation is operator-side ledger surgery on `PAY_LEDGER_DIR` files.
- Confidence: high (stated verbatim); conditional on the flag being set
- Severity: medium (high the instant PAY_ENABLED is true)
- Marked by authors: yes (described as a stub; ADR-2111 flagged as proposed)

### F-12 — The human-approval audit trail is split-brained: REST decisions carry no signed-event correlation and no handler ever calls the elevation actors
- Topics: VC-05.17, VC-05.18
- Evidence: DIVERGENCE (VC-05.17): "grep across src/handlers finds zero references to either actor type"; the REST path writes `decision_event_id:None` every time (enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:554-498/:572) while ElevationActor writes `Some(event_id)` from the signed kind-31403 (elevation_actor.rs:1749); the inbox "projects EITHER kind of row into the SAME BrokerCase shape, indistinguishable to the bridge". BASELINE-architecture.md:306 is quoted: "Current source review does not certify a complete human-approval journey."
- Failure: Asked "was this decision approved by the human panel?", the store cannot answer: rows exist with no signed event binding them, so a forged or mis-attributed REST decision is indistinguishable from a panel-approved one after the fact. Case authority, failure and restart receipts are unmodelled. For a system whose selling point is governed human approval, the audit evidence does not exist for one of its two producers.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE + quoted BASELINE closeout)

### F-13 — "Revoke" does not revoke: deleting a role row drops the user to ambient authority, and POWER_USER_PUBKEYS members stay Admin regardless
- Topics: VC-03.8, VC-03.7, VC-05.1
- Evidence: "removal is NOT revocation: deleting the row drops the target to their AMBIENT authority (Admin if power-user, else configured_default), never denies outright. Genuine revocation requires an explicit Viewer assignment" (role_store.rs:500 path); precedence rule 2 (role_store.rs:359): "Admin if POWER_USER_PUBKEYS match, no row" (nostr_service.rs:132). The DELETE response carries only a "note" warning (VC-05.1).
- Failure: Incident response: an admin "revokes" a compromised or departed user with `DELETE /users/{pubkey}/role`. If that pubkey is in POWER_USER_PUBKEYS, the user remains Admin with no row to delete; otherwise they fall back to Editor (the default) and keep WriteGraph on everything. Access is cut only if the responder knows to assign Viewer *and* edit the env var and restart — two non-obvious steps the API response merely hints at in prose.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (presented as intended ADR-2010 behaviour with a caveat note, not as a defect)

### F-14 — Escalation permanently stops the graph service with nothing that restarts it, and there is no drain on shutdown
- Topics: VC-02.4, VC-02.5, VC-02.6
- Evidence: After `restart_count > max_restarts` (default 5 within 300 s, :479-491), strategy Escalate is "top of the tree, ctx.stop()" (:795-807). VC-02.6: "children hold no back-reference — each stops when its last Addr clone drops"; "there is no drain timer and no registration gate on the live path". AppState starts GraphServiceSupervisor once (VC-02.1) and nothing in the material re-starts it; there is also no supervisor of the supervisor (ADR-2045 removed it).
- Failure: GraphStateActor panics six times in five minutes (a poison message will do it): with Escalate, the entire graph subsystem — state, physics, semantic processor, broadcasts — stops permanently while HTTP keeps serving 500s until a human restarts the process; with OneForAll, one flapping child repeatedly restarts the coordinator and drops every WebSocket registration cluster-wide. On routine SIGTERM, in-flight actor mailboxes (settings writes, decision persistence) die without drain once the HTTP graceful stop completes.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the lifecycle is fully diagrammed, but the default strategy and the absence of any external re-start are my reading of the tree topology
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — The multi-MCP discovery surface is a simulation: random synthetic failures, a discovery service never constructed, and its actor never started
- Topics: VC-05.13, VC-02.19
- Evidence: "the work inside the circuit breaker is SIMULATED: a fastrand 20 per-cent synthetic ConnectionRefused (:304-310) and a 100 ms sleep (:312), then Ok"; "MultiMcpAgentDiscovery :100 … is never constructed anywhere in src/" — its env config (CLAUDE_FLOW_HOST etc.) is "dead configuration on the live path"; separately, "MultiMcpVisualizationActor has no start() call found in src/app_state.rs or src/main.rs" (VC-02.19).
- Failure: Any dashboard or client relying on `/multi-mcp/ws` receives fabricated data shaped by a random number generator — intermittently reporting MCP services as down (synthetic ConnectionRefused) or up (the fake Ok) regardless of reality. Operators troubleshoot outages that never happened and trust availability that was never checked; meanwhile the real discovery service and its configuration rot unexercised.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in VC-05.13; coverage gap in VC-02.19)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether any downstream consumer reads `ws_server.pubkey` / `.is_power_user` (F-10) — I would need the SocketFlowServer field reads in `types.rs`/`position_updates.rs` beyond the excerpts shown, to know if release-build WS identity is broken, merely redundant, or sourced elsewhere.
2. Which handler actually answers `GET /api/health` — the collision between `api_handler`'s `/health` (mod.rs:121) and `consolidated_health_handler`'s `/health` scope is noted with "first registration wins" but neither file states the resolved registration order, and the KG watchdog's verdict depends on that payload.
3. Default payload limits on the live extractors — whether actix JSON/form defaults or per-route configs cap request bodies anywhere, which decides whether the unwired ValidationMiddleware (F-09) is a real DoS door or a defence-in-depth gap.
4. Deployment topology and proxy plumbing — replica count (decides whether the process-local replay cache is exploitable in practice), and how nginx presents client IPs (decides whether the `realip_remote_addr`-keyed 60/min rate limit is per-client or one global bucket behind the proxy).
5. Content sensitivity of the two unauthenticated-or-weakly-authenticated streams — what `client_message_rx` actually carries (F-03) and whether V3 wire ids can be joined to node labels/metadata by a low-privilege client (decides the real disclosure impact of F-01).

## visionclaw / premortem

# Post-Mortem: Production Incident

## Context

Twelve months of production operation of the VisionClaw knowledge-graph platform. The material below is drawn exclusively from the verified diagram set (VC-01 through VC-18), each claim carrying the `path:line` citation the verifying engineer should check.

---

## Three Most Likely Root Causes

### R-1 — Release-mode WebSocket identity silently never propagates after successful header authentication
- Topics: VC-03.2, VC-13.1, VC-13.8, VC-03.9
- Chain of events:
  1. A client connects to `/wss` in a release build and presents a valid `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header.
  2. The header-token check validates the session and allows the upgrade to proceed (VC-03.2, `http_handler.rs:186`–`:203`).
  3. That validated identity is **never written into `ws_server.pubkey` or `ws_server.is_power_user`** — the only code that sets those fields is a second, redundant query-string extraction (`token_from_qs`, `http_handler.rs:328`–`:341`) that is **hardcoded `None` in release builds** (`:341`).
  4. The session proceeds as anonymous (`pubkey == None`) despite the user having successfully authenticated.
  5. Downstream, the fail-closed visibility filter (VC-03.9) drops all private nodes for the anonymous session, drag/pin opcodes are rejected (VC-16.1, `position_updates.rs:960`), and any identity-dependent path operates on missing identity.
  6. The post-connect NIP-98 `authenticate` message (VC-13.1, `filter_auth.rs:10`) is a *separate* path that does set identity — but a client that authenticates via the header and never sends the post-connect message remains anonymous for the lifetime of the socket.
- Evidence:
  - VC-03.2 DIVERGENCE note: "in a RELEASE build, `ws_server.pubkey` is NEVER set on this handler, even after a client successfully authenticates via the Authorization header — not yet confirmed whether downstream identity/visibility depends on this field or is sourced elsewhere."
  - The second `get_session` call feeding `ws_server` runs only under `cfg(debug_assertions, feature = "dev-auth")` (`http_handler.rs:376`–`:380`); the release stub returns `None` (`:341`).
  - VC-13.1 shows the NIP-98 subprotocol path separately sets `pubkey`/`is_power_user` at `:392`–`:395`, but this is only for clients that sign the upgrade URL — not for Bearer-token clients.
- Warning signs visible today:
  - The DIVERGENCE flag in VC-03.2 explicitly says downstream dependence is "not yet confirmed."
  - The two identity-setting paths (header check at `:186`, query-string at `:376`) are described as "SEPARATE, redundant" — the redundancy is asymmetric between build types.
  - No test is cited that asserts release-mode `ws_server.pubkey` is set after header authentication.
- Confidence: high — the code path is explicitly documented; only the downstream blast radius is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### R-2 — Three uncoordinated graph-mutation paths plus a destructive bulk reload can silently lose user-created nodes
- Topics: VC-04.10, VC-04.3, VC-04.4, VC-01.10
- Chain of events:
  1. A user adds a node via `POST /api/graph/nodes` — this writes through `graph_state_handler` → `graph_adapter` → CQRS `AddNodeHandler` (VC-04.10, `graph_state_handler.rs:156`).
  2. Separately, an admin calls `POST /api/graph/update` — this triggers a **full bulk reload** (VC-04.3: "re-fetch, re-process, rebuild from `AddNodesFromMetadata`", `graph/mod.rs:471`).
  3. The rebuild reconstructs the graph exclusively from the metadata store. Any node added through the CQRS `graph_adapter` path that was not also persisted to the metadata store is **not present in the rebuild**.
  4. The material documents "three distinct graph-mutation paths coexist … with no single write gateway" (VC-04.10 DIVERGENCE note, citing BASELINE-architecture.md persistence closeout).
  5. No transactional or consistency guarantee spans the three paths; a node written through one path is invisible to a rebuild driven by another.
- Evidence:
  - VC-04.10 DIVERGENCE: "this handler family reaches the graph through a DIFFERENT store reference (graph_adapter, CQRS hexser handlers) than api_handler::graph (VC-04.1-04.3), which reads via graph_query_handlers, and bots/files/graph_state, which write via the ACTOR `graph_service_addr.send(AddNodesFromMetadata)` — three distinct graph-mutation paths coexist in this commit with no single write gateway."
  - VC-04.3: "/update triggers a full bulk reload (re-fetch, re-process, rebuild from `AddNodesFromMetadata`). Destructive and expensive, so power_user (Admin), not Authenticated." (`graph/mod.rs:1531`–`:1543`)
  - VC-04.4 shows `files::update_graph` as yet another rebuild path (`files/mod.rs:243`) that "calls `AddNodesFromMetadata` with metadata freshly loaded from disk via `load_or_create_metadata`, NOT the request body."
- Warning signs visible today:
  - The DIVERGENCE is already flagged in the governing document (BASELINE-architecture.md persistence closeout).
  - VC-04.20 identifies a **fourth** read path (`schema_handler` reads via directly-injected `Addr<GraphStateActor>`), further confirming no single authority over the graph.
  - No test is cited that asserts a node written through `graph_state_handler` survives a subsequent `/api/graph/update` rebuild.
- Confidence: high — the three paths and their independence are directly stated; the specific data-loss scenario on rebuild is inferred from the described architecture.
- Severity: high

---

### R-3 — `/ws/client-messages` accepts any non-empty string as a token; contents never verified
- Topics: VC-04.25, VC-13.8
- Chain of events:
  1. A client (or attacker) sends `GET /ws/client-messages?token=anything` or with `Authorization: Bearer anything`.
  2. The handler checks only that the token string is **non-empty** — it never calls `NostrService.get_session()` or any other verifier (VC-04.25, `client_messages_handler.rs:131`–`:142`).
  3. The connection is upgraded; the `ClientMessagesWs` actor is started.
  4. The actor's `start_message_stream()` (`ctx.run_interval(100ms)`) drains `app_state.client_message_rx` and forwards every message to the connected client as `{type:client_message, content, timestamp, session_id, agent_id}`.
  5. Any data flowing through the process-global `client_message_rx` channel is broadcast to the unauthenticated connection.
- Evidence:
  - VC-04.25 DOC-DRIFT note: "the in-code comment (`client_messages_handler.rs:114`–`:116`) says 'Currently allows but logs unauthenticated connections' but the code (`:131`–`:142`) actually REJECTS an empty token with 401 — **the token's CONTENTS are never verified against NostrService, only its presence, so any non-empty string passes**."
  - The same note contrasts this with other WS handlers (`mcp_relay_handler`, `multi_mcp_websocket_handler`) that DO validate the session post-ADR-2090.
- Warning signs visible today:
  - The DOC-DRIFT is flagged in the diagram itself.
  - The contrast with the ADR-2090 hardening of the other WS endpoints (VC-05.12, VC-05.13) shows this endpoint was left behind.
  - The material does not specify what populates `client_message_rx` — the sensitivity of the leaked data is unconfirmed but the exposure surface is open.
- Confidence: high — the code behavior is explicitly documented; the sensitivity of the leaked data is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

## Further Latent Defects

### F-01 — Pay resource handler charges sats then never delivers the resource
- Topics: VC-04.27
- Evidence: "resource proxying is a STUB — this confirms and charges but never forwards to the underlying resource handler (`pay_handler.rs:503`–`:505`)." Routes are mounted unconditionally and inert until `PAY_ENABLED=true`.
- Failure: An operator enables `PAY_ENABLED=true` to gate a paid resource. Users are debited from their balance, receive a 200 with `charged` and `balance` headers, and never receive the actual resource. Direct financial loss with no delivery path.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — REST-path broker decisions carry no signed-event correlation (custody gap)
- Topics: VC-05.7, VC-05.17
- Evidence: "the agentbox/operator/git-bridge REST path (VC-05.7) writes `decision_event_id:None` every time, since it never carries a signed 31403 event" (VC-05.17). The BASELINE closeout states "Current source review does not certify a complete human-approval journey."
- Failure: A disputed decision cannot be traced to a signed approval event. In a governance or audit scenario, the operator cannot prove a human approved a specific case action — the decision record exists but lacks cryptographic attribution.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — NIP-98 replay cache is process-local; no cross-replica protection
- Topics: VC-03.4, VC-03.5
- Evidence: "Process-local scope — no cross-replica replay protection (SECURITY-profiles.md)" (`nip98.rs`, replay cache note in VC-03.4).
- Failure: If the service is deployed behind a load balancer with more than one replica, a captured NIP-98 token replayed against a different replica within the 60-second freshness window passes verification on that replica because its replay cache has no record of the original use.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

- Failure: Physics simulation behavior (attraction/repulsion between domain classes) depends on which actor's upload happens last. A timing change in actor startup or message delivery silently alters the visual clustering of the knowledge graph, with no error or warning.

## visionclaw#3 / critical

### F-01 — Dev-mode compose default arms a peer-agnostic, full-bypass admin endpoint on the LAN
- Topics: VC-36.15
- Evidence: `docker-compose.unified.yml:90` defaults `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1`, so `launch.sh up dev` boots an armed backend (ADR-2108); `src/utils/auth.rs:186-201` grants every request as `DEV_MODE_PUBKEY` = "dev-mode-local-admin" (`auth.rs:79`, `main.rs:291`) with "no NIP-98, no token, no peer check". The authored caveat concedes both services load `env_file .env` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:87-89`), so the var "must never sit in a .env shared with prod — sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked." The only hard guard (`main.rs:109`) protects release binaries, not the running dev service.
- Failure: the dev backend is reachable and fully writable by any LAN peer, not just the headset host — layout resets, physics writes, query executes — all attributed to one shared identity with no per-user audit. Alternatively, a stray var in a shared `.env` either bricks a release boot or arms a dev-auth build.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (ADR-2108 flip and the `.env` caveat are authored; the LAN-wide blast radius is stated as fact, not flagged as a defect)

### F-02 — Presence socket is not proxied: any proxied production topology loses avatars, and the workaround is direct backend exposure
- Topics: VC-36.10, VC-36.3
- Evidence: "nginx :3001 does NOT proxy /ws/presence — the client points at the LAN backend directly" (VC-36.10, citing docs/XR-client.md 'Transport'). The graph socket `/wss` and presence `/ws/presence` are built from the same base (`graph_scene.gd:72-73`).
- Failure: any deployment fronted by nginx serves the graph stream but cannot serve presence to off-LAN users — multiplayer avatars silently dead. The only remediation the material implies is exposing the backend directly, which combined with F-01 gives unauthenticated admin to whoever can reach it.
- Confidence: high (fact stated; production impact inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a transport fact, no marker)

- Evidence: `NostrAuth.create()` returns an ephemeral signer when the secret is empty (`graph_scene.gd:435-437`), but "an ephemeral key cannot satisfy the presence challenge or gate drag/pin" (docs/XR-client.md INVARIANT 6). The client still constructs `Authorization: PHYSICS_BEARER` it knows the server refuses for non-loopback peers (`graph_scene.gd:1116`, known divergences bullet 5).
- Failure: operator launches the headset without the var on a non-dev backend: avatars/presence dead and every write 401s — visible post-ADR-2108 as a notice, but still a full-feature outage from one unvalidated launch parameter, with the client sending a credential it knows is dead.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (documented as INVARIANT 6 and known divergence bullet 5, not flagged as a defect)

### F-12 — Demo-mode synthetic agents share the production registry with no wire marker; Stop retires by id range alone
- Topics: VC-36.23, VC-36.5, VC-36.9
- Evidence: synthetic ids are `0x80000000 | 0xD001..0xD006` (`agent_demo_director.gd:325`), provenance is "the reserved id range 0xD001-0xD0FF alone" with "NO demo marker in names, frames, roster rows or payloads" (`agent_demo_director.gd:25, :87`); `retire_agents(demo_wire_ids())` "remove[s] record and anchor outright" (`binary_protocol.rs:1206`, `render_store.rs:908`). The server allocates agent node ids under the same `AGENT_NODE_FLAG 0x8000_0000` (`binary_protocol.rs:38`) and nothing shown enforces the low-bits reservation server-side.
- Failure: if the server ever assigns a live agent an id in 0xD001–0xD0FF, the demo's synthetic agent and the live one collide in one registry; pressing Stop retires the live agent's record and anchor, and 5 s keep-alives overwrite its evidence.
- Confidence: low (inferred — collision requires server allocation inside the range, whose enforcement is not shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — Protocol-version skew degrades to a frozen graph with only an in-HMD-invisible warning
- Topics: VC-36.6, VC-36.11
- Evidence: unknown version → "warn + ignore" (`binary_protocol.rs:715-717`); the ADR-2108 history establishes that `push_warning` output "never reach[ed] the headset" (VC-36.11), and `flash_notice` was wired only for rejected server writes (`hud.gd:970`, `graph_scene.gd:1100`).
- Failure: server upgraded ahead of the client (or vice versa): frames are dropped every tick, the graph silently stops updating, and the only diagnostic is a Godot console warning — the operator reads a stale graph as live.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — generalising the stated push_warning invisibility beyond the write path)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — The HUD has eight tabs but the visual validation fixture covers seven, and the new tab's palette is duplicated by hand
- Topics: VC-36.8, VC-36.20
- Evidence: TAB_ORDER gained the Key tab 2026-09-08, "so this HUD now has EIGHT pages, not seven" (`hud.gd:161`, VC-36.8), while the visual fixture still validates "Seven HUD tabs and radial gallery" (`xr-client/tests/visual/hud_gallery.gd`, VC-36.20). Separately marked DEBT: Key swatch constants "duplicated from their sources… a palette change must be made twice" (`hud.gd:169, :633`), mirroring SWARM_STATUS_COLORS vs `render_store::agent_status_color`.
- Failure: the newest tab is outside the visual validation boundary, and a future palette edit made once leaves the Key tab misdescribing live community/status colours — users systematically misread the colour coding the tab exists to explain.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no for the seven-vs-eight mismatch (the constant duplication is marked DEBT)

### F-15 — The governing architecture doc still records a removed auth weakness as open
- Topics: VC-36.3
- Evidence: ADR-2058 (2026-09-05) compiled the server's `?token=` query fallback out of release entirely, "surviv[ing] only dev-auth-gated with a SECURITY: warning — BASELINE-architecture.md's 'Known divergences' bullet (:248) recording it as still-open was not updated when ADR-2058 landed."
- Failure: a security reviewer or auditor working from the governing doc assesses a token-auth weakness that no longer exists in release — and, more corrosively, learns the baseline's divergence list lags the code, so the remaining bullets (including F-11's bearer path) can no longer be trusted at face value.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (explicitly described as not updated)

## Not judgeable from this material
- Reconnect semantics for both sockets: whether the client re-sends `requestInitialData`/subscribe after a backoff reconnect, and how the FreshnessTracker watermark and `awaiting_resync` state reset (including what `admit(None, kind)` does for V3) — VC-36.3/36.4 stop at the timer.
- Room/tenant scoping of `/wss` and the `0x23` `broadcast_to_all` fanout — "every /wss client" could be per-room or global; cross-tenant agent-action leakage cannot be assessed.
- Whether the 0xD001–0xD0FF agent-id reservation is enforced server-side (decides F-12 between hazard and non-issue).
- Actual network topology and trust boundary: where nginx :3001, the backend on :4000, and the dev compose run, and who else can reach that LAN — determines the real exposure behind F-01 and F-02.
- Current test outcomes and acceptance currency: GUT suite pass/fail state, the perf-regression budgets in `benches/baseline.json`, and the date of the last genuine on-headset acceptance (VC-36.20 concedes none for the spatial visuals).

## visionclaw#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — VisionClaw XR estate

**Scope note.** All findings below are drawn from the distilled material at verified_commit `af3dff3f25300cf12bceda5650688ec223270eca`. Where I reason past what the diagrams state, the confidence line says so. The incident reconstruction is: a client's knowledge graph was both **read in cleartext** and **mutated by an unauthenticated party**, and the write trail could not be attributed afterwards. Every element of that sentence maps to a defect the material already documents.

---

### R-1 — The operational backend boots with a peer-agnostic full-auth bypass armed by default
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-36.11, VC-36.3, VC-36.16
- Chain of events:
  1. ADR-2108 (2026-09-08) flipped the dev compose default to `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: "${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}"` — `launch.sh up dev` now boots an **armed** backend and the operator must opt *out* with `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=0`.
  2. The only deployment the material documents for the XR rig is that dev/dev-auth stack: the headset is pointed straight at `ws://192.168.2.132:4000`, and nginx `:3001` does not front `/ws/presence`, so clients address backend ports directly.
  3. On a dev/dev-auth build, `dev_mode_enabled()` returns true and **every** request resolves to `DEV_MODE_PUBKEY = "dev-mode-local-admin"` — "LAN-local FULL bypass, peer-agnostic: no NIP-98, no token, no peer check".
  4. Destructive writes sit one POST away on that path: `POST /api/settings/physics/reset-layout`, `PUT /api/settings/physics`, layout/radial writes — and with an empty `XR_NOSTR_SECRET` "the ephemeral signer still works".
  5. Anyone who can reach port 4000 (client office LAN, VPN, misrouted segment — the confinement is topology, not code) obtains full write access; every write logs under one shared synthetic identity, so the post-incident record cannot distinguish operator from intruder.
  6. The only hard guard is the release-binary boot refusal; the authors' own caveat — both services load `env_file .env`, "so the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod — sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" — is exactly the escape path, and the default is *not* in the common-environment anchor, so its propagation is invisible to config review.
- Evidence: docker-compose.unified.yml:90, :83-89; project/src/main.rs:109, :288, :291; src/utils/auth.rs:79, :186-201; graph_scene.gd:1131, :1175, :446-451; VC-36.10 nginx note; VC-36.16 deploy ceremony.
- Warning signs visible today: opt-out security default; bypass explicitly described as "peer-agnostic"; single shared write identity; the authors' own `.env` caveat; warn banner at main.rs:288 is the only runtime signal.
- Confidence: high — the armed default and the peer-agnostic grant are stated verbatim; the link from "deployed dev stack" to "this incident" is inferred from it being the only documented deployment.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Plaintext `ws://`/`http://` to a directly addressed backend; graph data and signed credentials on the wire
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.10, VC-36.11, VC-36.16
- Chain of events:
  1. The client default backend is `ws://localhost:4000` and the deploy ceremony pins `XR_BACKEND_WS=ws://192.168.2.132` — cleartext WebSocket on the LAN. No `wss://` endpoint appears in any cited source.
  2. `_http_base` maps `ws://` → `http://`, so every REST write — including the `Authorization: Nostr …` header and JSON bodies — also travels plaintext.
  3. nginx fronts `:3001` but "does NOT proxy /ws/presence — the client points at the LAN backend directly", so at least one socket bypasses the edge proxy and whatever TLS termination it provides, entirely.
  4. Any party on the segment reads the client's knowledge graph in flight (52-byte records carry ontology IRIs, analytics, community/cluster data), plus user poses and agent activity (0x23/0x44).
  5. A captured NIP-98 header is replayable to whatever extent the server validates freshness — the material specifies exact-URL matching but states no timestamp/nonce/expiry check anywhere (see F-13).
- Evidence: graph_scene.gd:72, :1213 (`_http_base` branches), :1108-1109; transport.rs:69 connect path; VC-36.10 nginx note; VC-36.16 launch line `XR_BACKEND_WS=ws://192.168.2.132 … XR_NOSTR_SECRET=<hex>`.
- Warning signs visible today: scheme defaults are `ws://`; the proxy-gap note is already in the docs; the signing secret is placed in the ssh launch command line (process-list visible on the headset host).
- Confidence: high that the transport is plaintext as deployed (stated); medium, inferred, that this was the exfiltration vector.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — No durable identity on the operational path: throwaway keys, a retained bearer fallback, and unattributable destructive writes
- Topics: VC-36.10, VC-36.11, VC-36.15, VC-36.12
- Chain of events:
  1. `NostrAuth.create()` returns an **ephemeral** signer when the secret is empty (graph_scene.gd:435-437); docs' INVARIANT 6 says an ephemeral key "cannot satisfy the presence challenge or gate drag/pin" — yet under the armed dev mode it "still works" (graph_scene.gd:446-451). The friction-free mode therefore runs on a per-session throwaway identity.
  2. The client still constructs the legacy `Authorization: PHYSICS_BEARER` + `X-Nostr-Pubkey` fallback when no real secret is present (graph_scene.gd:94, :450, :1116); the server refuses it "for any non-loopback peer" — but the peer-determination method is unstated, and a co-located proxy makes every remote peer appear loopback (suspected — see F-06).
  3. Reset-layout, physics PUT and layout/radial writes are reachable from HUD controls on these degraded credentials.
  4. Until ADR-2108, "every server-routed HUD write 401d into an invisible push_warning" — a long window in which blocked writes were silently discarded, so neither successes nor failures left a user-visible or attributable trail.
  5. Net at incident time: writes succeeded with no per-actor identity (R-1) or via degraded credentials, and historic denials were invisible — the operator can neither prove who acted nor reconstruct what was attempted.
- Evidence: graph_scene.gd:435-437, :446-451, :94, :450, :1116, :1131, :1175; signer.rs:194; docs/XR-client.md INVARIANT 6 and "Known divergences" bullet 5; VC-36.11 ADR-2108 note.
- Warning signs visible today: ephemeral-by-default signer; bearer fallback retained in the client; the docs' own divergence bullet 5; identity checks differ per socket (graph vs presence).
- Confidence: medium — the identity degradation and endpoint reachability are stated; the proxy-loopback escape and the attribution failure as the incident mechanism are inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — V3 frames bypass the freshness watermark entirely
- Topics: VC-36.6, VC-36.5
- Evidence: V5 frames admit with `Some(seq)` and drop `seq <= watermark` (binary_protocol.rs:551-569); V3 frames are shown as "no sequence, `admit(None, kind)`" (binary_protocol.rs:400); both share one socket and the leading-byte dispatch.
- Failure: a server, older replica, or middlebox emitting 0x03 frames delivers unordered or replayed position/analytics records that the client applies with no staleness detection; the user drags/pins against stale coordinates and the server-authoritative write lands on the wrong layout.
- Confidence: high that the asymmetry exists; medium, inferred, that a V3 emitter occurs in practice.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-02 — A sequence regression after server restart can wedge ingest in `awaiting_resync`
- Topics: VC-36.6
- Evidence: the watermark drops `seq <= watermark`; `awaiting_resync` is named at binary_protocol.rs:551-569; no resync procedure appears anywhere in the material, and `requestInitialData` is shown sent once at connect (transport.rs:45).
- Failure: a server restart resets `broadcast_seq` low; every subsequent V5 frame is stale-dropped; the client keeps rendering the pre-restart graph indefinitely while both sockets show connected — silent divergence from server truth.
- Confidence: low, inferred — sequence-reset behaviour is unstated.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-03 — Demo mode fabricates agent evidence that is indistinguishable except by id range, and keeps it alive forever
- Topics: VC-36.23, VC-36.9
- Evidence: synthetic 0x23 frames enter through the same `record_agent_action` door; "the synthetic agents carry NO demo marker in names, frames, roster rows or payloads. Provenance is the reserved id range 0xD001-0xD0FF alone" (agent_demo_director.gd:25, :87); keep-alive every `KEEPALIVE_SEC 5.0` so evidence never ages out (agent_demo_director.gd:30).
- Failure: a demo left running during a client session mingles fabricated working/blocked/done agent telemetry with live swarm data on that headset; any human reading of swarm state is misled, and only id-range inspection can tell.
- Confidence: high on the behaviour (stated); blast radius is client-local by design.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-04 — Query Execute ships with server correctness and denial states unverified
- Topics: VC-36.18
- Evidence: "server correctness and the user-visible denied/error states are UNVERIFIED. Query Execute is implemented but runtime acceptance remains open" (docs/XR-client.md "Known divergences" bullet 4); no headset/scene/shader test covers the path; Rust tests exclude Godot-facing runtime classes.
- Failure: a client runs a pattern query during an engagement; the server returns wrong or partial results, or a denial renders as silence, and a decision is made on bad data.
- Confidence: high that verification is absent; medium that results are wrong.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — MediaPipe WASM is fetched from a public CDN into the client origin
- Topics: VC-37.6
- Evidence: `forVisionTasks('https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@mediapipe/tasks-vision@0.10.21/wasm')` (useHeadTracking.ts:27); the model itself is same-origin `/models/face_landmarker.task`; webcam frames are processed by the CDN-delivered code.
- Failure: a CDN compromise, hijacked DNS/TLS, or a stale pinned version serves malicious WASM running in the client origin with access to the video stream and app state; alternatively a CDN outage kills the feature.
- Confidence: high that the fetch is as stated; the exploit is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-06 — The PHYSICS_BEARER loopback refusal may collapse behind a co-located proxy
- Topics: VC-36.11
- Evidence: "The server refuses it for any non-loopback peer" — the peer-determination method is unstated; nginx is in the stack and clients are fronted at `:3001`.
- Failure: if the bearer check reads the TCP peer and any proxy terminates on the same host, all remote callers present as loopback and the legacy bearer grants writes to anyone who can reach the proxy.
- Confidence: low-to-medium, inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, docs bullet 5)
- Severity: high

### F-07 — Out-of-range records are dropped silently at sanitize, hiding nodes client-side
- Topics: VC-36.6
- Evidence: `|coord| > WORLD_LIMIT_M = 10_000.0` → "None - record dropped" (binary_protocol.rs:34); unlike the bad-version path, no user-visible warn is shown for the drop.
- Failure: a server bug streams one out-of-range coordinate; nodes vanish from the headset with no notice and the operator acts on an incomplete graph.
- Confidence: high on the drop; medium on the silence (an absence in the material, not a proven one).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — NodePositionContext is a module singleton with no stale-reader invalidation
- Topics: VC-37.7
- Evidence: "named a context but is a module-level mutable singleton… Nothing invalidates a stale reader after a remount" (NodePositionContext.tsx:13); GraphManager publishes every frame (GraphManager.tsx:431) and BotsVisualization reads directly (BotsVisualization.tsx:29).
- Failure: after a remount or graph swap, the bot overlay reads a stale buffer/id-map and renders agents attached to the wrong nodes — wrong data on screen.
- Confidence: high (stated as DEBT with the consequence spelled out).
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — BASELINE's known-divergences bullet went stale when ADR-2058 landed
- Topics: VC-36.3
- Evidence: the note states BASELINE-architecture.md:248 recorded the server's `?token=` fallback as still-open and "was not updated when ADR-2058 landed".
- Failure: responders or a client security review working from BASELINE chase a closed hole while the actually-open ones (dev-mode default, bearer fallback) get less attention; separately, it demonstrates that security-relevant doc updates have slipped at least once.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Marked by authors: yes (drift called out in-line)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — Palette constants are duplicated between HUD and render sources
- Topics: VC-36.8, VC-36.9
- Evidence: "the Key swatch constants are duplicated from their sources by design… a palette change must be made twice" (hud.gd:169, :633); `SWARM_STATUS_COLORS` "mirrors render_store::agent_status_color" (hud.gd:169).
- Failure: a palette update lands in one place; the HUD key or roster diverges from the beam/status colour — e.g. "blocked" rendered in the done hue — and an operator misreads agent state mid-engagement.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)
- Severity: low

### F-11 — project.godot metadata declares a renderer and version that have never rendered
- Topics: VC-36.2
- Evidence: `config/features` declares 4.3 / Forward Mobile (project.godot:12) while the only build that has rendered is 4.6.1-stable on Compatibility; re-pinning needs an editor that is not installed; README:15-18 tells readers to read 4.3 as "the pinned editor of the day".
- Failure: a new engineer or CI honors the declared metadata, rebuilds on Vulkan, and gets a one-eye or black headset at a client demo; the drift also masks which renderer was actually certified.
- Confidence: high (stated DOC-DRIFT, open by design).
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)
- Severity: low

### F-12 — Godot-facing runtime classes are excluded from every test run
- Topics: VC-36.16, VC-36.18, VC-36.20
- Evidence: 141 headless Rust tests, "Godot-facing runtime classes are excluded by cfg(test) (lib.rs:38-45), so no scene/shader test runs"; VC-36.20 limits: "No fresh headset, live authenticated graph or Quest frame-budget certification".
- Failure: regressions in boot, HUD write paths, or auth UX ship undetected — precisely the classes ADR-2108/2109 later found broken (invisible 401s, 5 mm avatars).
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: no (stated as fact, not flagged)
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — NIP-98 replay freshness is nowhere validated in the cited material
- Topics: VC-36.11, VC-36.3
- Evidence: the client signs the exact URL+method (graph_scene.gd:1108-1109; signer.rs:124); no source shown states a created_at/nonce check or expiry for kind-27235 headers server-side; combined with R-2's plaintext capture this is a complete replay ingredient set.
- Failure: a captured `Authorization: Nostr …` header is replayed to re-issue a write (reset-layout, physics PUT) within whatever validation window exists.
- Confidence: low-to-medium, inferred — the omission may live in server code outside these sources and must be checked before acting.
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — The long-lived signing secret is passed on the launch command line
- Topics: VC-36.16
- Evidence: the deploy ceremony's launch call carries `XR_BACKEND_WS=ws://… XR_NOSTR_SECRET=<hex> godot …` over ssh.
- Failure: the secret is visible in the process list on the headset host and in shell history on the operator machine; anyone with local read on either obtains the graph/presence identity for any deployment where dev mode is off.
- Confidence: high that the secret is passed this way (shown); medium on the exposure consequence.
- Marked by authors: no
