### F-15 — Agent state precedence, expiry and stale/error handling remain open
- Topics: VC-36.9
- Evidence: "agent endpoints use LOCAL positions directly while beam targets are fold-remapped and drawn-gated — the closeout asks for explicit state precedence, expiry and visible stale/error handling" (docs/XR-client.md closeout 2026-09-04; render_store.rs:1435-1450); the only working-state exit besides explicit done is the 30 s evidence TTL (render_store.rs:823).
- Failure: a stale pre-expiry record shows an agent working on a node it abandoned, beams and bodies disagree, and decisions are taken on stale swarm telemetry with no visible error state.
- Confidence: high that the handling is open; medium on impact.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

## visiongraph / critical

### F-01 — The private-page disclosure boundary was never fixed, and the only recorded mitigation was retired with the Python publisher
- Topics: VG-01.4, VG-05.2, VG-05.4
- Evidence: VG-01.4 lists boundary case B3 "private-ancestor details leaking into derived public exports" as unchanged since the 2026-09-07 audit, which established "NO real private-page disclosure" (PUBLICATION-contract.md:18). The proposed fix — a "deliberate, TESTED private-ancestor disclosure policy across API/scaffold/Turtle" (ADR-VG-001, VG-05.2 A5) — is proposed/partial/inactive (docs/adr/README.md:7-8). The only documented control, `public_projection.py` with "known-private-reference filtering before inference and before every export stage" (PUBLICATION-contract.md:39), is described by the 2026-10-01 banner as a retired Python publisher, "not current operating instructions" (PUBLICATION-contract.md:3-8); ADR-VG-003 retired that publisher (ADR-VG-003-obsidian-only-authoring.md:16-24). Nothing in the material certifies the replacement Rust `vault` build carries equivalent filtering.
- Failure: a page held private whose public descendant or derived export draws on it ships details into `api/`, `ontology.ttl` or the explorer data at narrativegoldmine.com and the pod. None of the live gates (artefact presence, ≥1000 line/entry floors, credential-pattern scan) detect content-level leakage.
- Confidence: high that the control is uncertified and the case is recorded open; whether the Rust binary happens to filter is not judgeable from this material
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — B3 is marked as an unchanged boundary case (VG-01.4) and the contract body is bannered historical (VG-05.4 note3); the synthesis that the only recorded mitigation was deleted with the pipeline is unmarked

### F-02 — Truthy-string publication flags: `public: "false"` may publish a private page
- Topics: VG-01.4, VG-05.2
- Evidence: B2 — "'false' as a Python truthy value" — is recorded as unchanged since the audit (PUBLICATION-contract.md:18, VG-01.4). The fix, "require BOOLEAN publication metadata" (ADR-VG-001, VG-05.2 A2), is proposed/inactive. CLAUDE.md:10-11 states the gate is *typed* `public: true`, but no cited validator enforces the type.
- Failure: an author writes a quoted or otherwise string-typed flag; if the gate evaluates truthiness the page publishes while validation passes green — a silent privacy failure with no gate downstream that would catch it.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the audit record calls the case unchanged, but the parser was replaced Python→Rust and the current parser's coercion behaviour is not shown)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (listed among the unchanged boundary cases); the parser-swap caveat is unmarked

### F-03 — The inclusion gate is contradicted between governing documents, and un-publication is not transitive across the four consumers
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-05.1, VG-04.1
- Evidence: CLAUDE.md:10-11 says typed `public: true` is the gate and only governed ontology types feed the ontology projection; PUBLICATION-contract.md:16 still specifies VisionClaw's interface as "public OR owl-class", and VG-01.3's diagram attributes that interface to VisionClaw currently. VG-01.3 note2 states the four consumers (site/KG, Quartz, VisionClaw, agentbox) have "DISTINCT inclusion policies — changing one flag does not establish removal from every consumer". Quartz additionally applies its own `ExplicitPublish({ key: "public" })` gate over the staging tree (quartz/README.md:25-26), a third semantics.
- Failure: an operator privatises a page by clearing `public` per CLAUDE.md; the page still ingests to the VisionClaw pod if it carries `owl-class` under the contract's interface, and persists in whichever consumer keys on a different field. There is no single kill-switch; a recanted page stays live somewhere.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, VG-01.3; DIVERGENCE note2)

### F-04 — The 961 MB research-vault asset store is symlinked wholesale into the public site with no asset-level gate
- Topics: VG-01.1, VG-03.5, VG-03.6, VG-05.1
- Evidence: `knowledge/assets` is a symlink to `../working/assets`, "961 MB stored once, shared" (VG-05.1 I5; VG-01.1). The publish stages it wholesale: "Symlink knowledge/assets into quartz/content/assets" (publish.yml:202-206). `working/` is the unpublished research vault, home of podcast-evidence material (working/bases/podcast-evidence.base), yet no asset-level public gate appears anywhere in the material — the only content gates are page-frontmatter flags and the output credential scan.
- Failure: any private research asset (evidence images, scans) is served publicly merely by existing on disk, regardless of its page's flag; `check-secrets.sh www` catches credential patterns, not private content. Corollary single point of failure: the published vault is not self-contained — publishing `knowledge/` requires `working/` present, so a partial clone or future vault split dangles the symlink.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — wholesale staging is stated; the material does not state assets are ungated, but no gate is mentioned)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — the symlink is presented as an invariant to preserve ("never replace it"), not as an exposure

### F-05 — Substance gates are absolute floors far below actual content, with no comparison to the previous deploy
- Topics: VG-03.1, VG-03.5, VG-04.1
- Evidence: SUBST requires only search-index entries, api/pages count and ontology.ttl line count each ≥1000 (publish.yml:280-289) against actuals of ~8,434 classes / 265,455 triples (VG-04.1). The authors state "presence of an artefact proves nothing" (VG-03.5) and that omitting `--vault all` silently builds knowledge-only while "every later gate still passes green" (publish.yml:182-187). Precedent for green-but-empty: the retired pipeline "emits an empty bundle" (publish.yml:79); the earlier converter "shipped 0 owl:Class from 380 pages" (VG-04.1 history). No cited gate diffs counts against the previous deploy.
- Failure: a parser or config regression that drops most of `working/` or an entire domain still clears the floors and deploys; by arithmetic a >99% content loss on the visionGraph side passes. The loss is found by readers, not CI.
- Confidence: high on the floor values and actuals; medium (inferred) that no regression baseline exists — none is cited anywhere
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — "presence proves nothing" and the `--vault all` trap are marked invariants; the missing previous-deploy baseline and the floor-to-actual ratio are unmarked

### F-06 — Site and pod are built by different `vault` converter revisions from the same source sha
- Topics: VG-03.1, VG-04.1
- Evidence: visionGraph pins `VAULT_GIT_REV` to `90a7a85c`, commented "Eight-domain exporter and canonical Obsidian parser" (publish.yml:50) — output is demonstrably revision-dependent. VisionClaw's job builds `vault` from its own default-branch checkout, `cargo build -p vault` (ontology-publish.yml:145-146, 167-168), explicitly "NOT visionGraph's VAULT_GIT_REV pin". The dispatch pins only the corpus sha (publish.yml:459-465). VG-04.1's Open note: "the site and the pod release can be produced by different vault revisions from the same source sha. No record says whether that difference is intended."
- Failure: after any vault change on VisionClaw main, the pod's ontology and the site's artefacts diverge in content or format for the identical corpus revision, silently — no gate compares the two outputs.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, VG-04.1)

### F-07 — README understates the deploy triggers: editing the explorer or static docs ships to production
- Topics: VG-01.1, VG-05.1
- Evidence: README.md:125-126 lists `knowledge/**`, `quartz/**`, `ontology/**` as publish triggers; publish.yml:23-29 also fires on `publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/modern/**` and `static/**`. VG-05.1 I7: "agents NEVER push this repo — a push deploys to narrativegoldmine.com" (CLAUDE.md:23-26).
- Failure: an engineer or agent trusting the README edits explorer source or the JSON-LD context believing it non-deploying; the push runs the full pipeline and the change goes live. The pipeline gates check structure, not review.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT, VG-01.1 note3)

### F-08 — The eight-domain deployment's paper trail contradicts itself: ADR says private and not deployed, the gate enforces published and deploys on push
- Topics: VG-01.7, VG-03.5
- Evidence: ADR-VG-004:35 — "the two roots exist as private draft classes" and "the changes are not deployed"; the release gate requires each root present in the public search index (check-space-domains.mjs:22-23) and the workflow running it deploys on push (publish.yml:296-297); the ADR index says "implementation complete, activation staged" (docs/adr/README.md:10). Separately, vocabulary.yaml:125 and :316-317 still say "six domain roots" while `roots` lists eight (vocabulary.yaml:322-324).
- Failure: an operator reading the ADR or vocabulary treats the space/Earth domains and their expansion pages as private/unreleased when they are live — or tooling written against "six roots" mishandles ids 6–7. Conversely, if the ADR's state were true, deploys would be failing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, VG-01.7 note2 and note3)

### F-09 — `vault.toml` promises a committed `knowledge/index.md` that does not exist
- Topics: VG-01.5
- Evidence: vault.toml:30 declares `roles.knowledge.index = knowledge/index.md`, "generated by vault build, committed"; VG-01.5 note1 records that no such file exists at HEAD (`git cat-file -e HEAD:knowledge/index.md` fails).
- Failure: any consumer or script trusting the manifest's committed-index claim — fresh-clone tooling, index-driven linking — hits a missing entry point, and the manifest's other claims inherit doubt.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT, VG-01.5 note1)

### F-10 — The site sits at 961 MB of assets against a 1000 MB hard deploy failure
- Topics: VG-01.1, VG-03.5, VG-03.6
- Evidence: shared assets are 961 MB (VG-01.1; VG-05.1 I5), staged wholesale (publish.yml:202-206); the finalize gate warns over 900 MB and hard-fails over 1000 MB apparent size on `www` (publish.yml:331-354). Growth is by research/article batches — the gate file changed in 56 commits, "almost all of them article batches" (VG-03.5 Debt).
- Failure: the next asset-bearing batch crosses 1000 MB and every deploy hard-fails until assets are pruned or the gate moved — a publication outage with no marked owner and roughly 4% headroom today.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — arithmetic from stated numbers; the www total also includes notes and site-data, unstated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — The release gate pins exact corpus counts, and by its own numbers ~29% of the public domain pages ship empty
- Topics: VG-03.1, VG-03.5
- Evidence: the gate pins 934 expansion identities and 660 researched drafts (check-space-domains.mjs:53-54), so "every research batch must edit the release gate in the same commit"; 56 gate-touching commits, almost all article batches (VG-03.5 Debt). The same check encodes that the remaining 274 of 934 public identities "carry article text — the rest none" (check-space-domains.mjs:39-54).
- Failure: a batch that forgets the gate edit blocks the release on a ritual number change; habituated gate-bumping degrades the check into transcription of what the PR claims rather than verification of what the build produced. Separately, 274 public pages render with no content.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the pinning is marked (Debt); the empty-pages consequence and the ritualisation risk are unmarked

### F-12 — The document every topic names as *governing* disclaims being operating instructions, and no replacement exists
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-05.4, and the `governing:` headers of all five files
- Evidence: every topic file's frontmatter cites PUBLICATION-contract.md as governing; its banner (PUBLICATION-contract.md:3-8) marks the body's implementation descriptions as a retired Python publisher, "not current operating instructions". The body still asserts in the present tense a rejected JSON-LD interface (`vc:public`, :16), `public_projection.py` boundaries (:39), and a preserved `notes-mobile.css` whose file was deleted on 2026-10-01 (VG-05.4 Drift). No current contract document is named anywhere in the material.
- Failure: incident response or a new operator follows the governing doc — expects JSON-LD fences, runs `public_projection.py`, looks for the stylesheet — and misdiagnoses the live system; agents instructed to treat the contract as governing receive false rules.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift/Historical notes, VG-01.3 note1 and VG-05.4 note3)

### F-13 — No binding exists between the served bytes and the source revision that produced them
- Topics: VG-04.3, VG-05.2, VG-01.4
- Evidence: PUBLICATION-contract.md:14 — source, distribution and deployed artefact identities "must NOT be collapsed into one revision"; ADR-VG-002's single release manifest with output hashes (VG-05.2 B1) is proposed/inactive ("proposed, NOT yet activation_status: live", VG-04.3 note1); pre-split hashes resolve only in the read-only jjohare/logseq (README.md:150-156, VG-04.3 note2). The only provenance link that exists is the dispatch's `source_sha` to VisionClaw (publish.yml:459-465), which covers neither the gh-pages commit nor what a browser fetched.
- Failure: wrong or harmful content is found on narrativegoldmine.com; scoping the blast radius — which pages, which versions, from which source commit — is manual archaeology with no recorded chain.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (VG-04.3 note1/note2; VG-05.2/05.3)

### F-14 — The two explorer copies drift in opposite directions and the sibling is no longer a usable recovery path; the shipping tree's own agent instructions are wrong
- Topics: VG-06.1, VG-06.2, VG-06.3, VG-06.4, VG-06.5
- Evidence: 33 shared files differ, up from 24 (VG-06.1). Drift runs both ways: the shipping `router.tsx` gained `readBaseUrl()`/`basename` the sibling lacks (router.tsx:64-75,135); the sibling's `mesh.ts` gained `LOOM` the shipping tree lacks (explorer/modern/src/site/mesh.ts:38). The sibling is six-domain (explorer/modern/src/types/scope.ts:73-80) and "can no longer be dropped in as a replacement" (VG-06.5). The shipping tree's CLAUDE.md carries 12 references to a nonexistent `rust-wasm/` directory including build recipes (lines 25, 39-49, 96); the corrected text exists only in the non-shipping sibling (VG-06.2 note1). CAPABILITIES.md measures a pipeline the workflow no longer runs (CAPABILITIES.md:6,24).
- Failure: disaster recovery by substituting the sibling ships a six-domain explorer (domains 6/7 vanish) without sub-path routing; meanwhile agents working in the tree that actually ships follow procedures that cannot succeed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT/Debt throughout VG-06); the agent-operability consequence of the wrong CLAUDE.md is marked only as doc-drift

### F-15 — Asserted vs inferred IRI divergence across outputs, recorded open and never re-tested against the current parser
- Topics: VG-01.4, VG-05.3
- Evidence: B4, "asserted vs inferred IRI representation divergence across outputs", is recorded unchanged since the 2026-09-07 audit (PUBLICATION-contract.md:18). Neither proposed ADR addresses it directly, and the only test result cited anywhere is dated and stale ("57 passed and one corpus-size assertion failure", VG-05.3).
- Failure: the same class is rendered under different IRIs across `ontology.ttl`, `api/` and the explorer, so cross-output links break and downstream consumers double-count entities.
- Confidence: low — the finding was recorded against the retired Python pipeline; whether the Rust `vault` reproduces it is not shown
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (listed among the unchanged boundary cases)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether the Rust `vault` binary enforces a typed-boolean `public` gate and any private-ancestor/inference filtering. Every recorded boundary-case behaviour (F-01, F-02, F-15) belonged to the deleted Python parser; I would need VisionClaw's `crates/vault` source, its tests, and `docs/VAULT-corpus-format.md` (VC-21) before signing off.
2. The actual contents and referencing of `working/assets` — whether private research/evidence files reach `www`, and whether staging prunes unreferenced assets (F-04). I need the asset tree and the build's asset-handling code.
3. Branch protection and review requirements on visionGraph. The push-to-deploy exposure (F-07) and the agents-never-push rule matter differently if the default branch requires review; repository settings are invisible here.
4. Quartz's `ExplicitPublish({ key: "public" })` semantics versus the vault's typed gate — whether the second gate can admit pages the first excluded, e.g. string-typed flags (F-03). I need the Quartz configuration.
5. Current test and credential-rule currency, and the verification stamp itself: the only cited test result is dated; `check-secrets.sh --self-test` proves the harness fires, not that its rules cover present-day credential formats; and the topic files are verified at different commits (`9d667562` for VG-01/03/04 vs `015ca2c1` for VG-05/06), so the currency of every cited drift at HEAD cannot be confirmed from here.

## visiongraph / premortem

# Post-mortem — visionGraph publication system

**Basis:** the distillation record VG-01 through VG-06, at the verified commits each topic cites. Nothing else was available to this document. Every finding below is either stated in that record or a direct implication of it; where a chain of events goes beyond the stated facts it is marked *inferred*. Each item should be checked against the code and tests before action.

---

### R-1 — Private material reached a consumer whose inclusion policy differs; the publication boundary it crossed was never closed
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-01.4, VG-05.2, VG-05.3
- Chain of events:
  1. A page's privacy depends on something other than its own typed `public: true` flag — a non-boolean publication flag, or an ancestor that is private.
  2. The four consumers apply **distinct inclusion policies** (VG-01.3, note2): the vault build's `public` gate (typed `public: true`, `CLAUDE.md:10-11`), Quartz's own `ExplicitPublish({key:"public"})` over the staging tree (`quartz/README.md:25-26`), VisionClaw's documented `public` **or** `owl-class` interface (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`), and the agentbox projection. "Changing one flag does not establish removal from every consumer."
  3. The 2026-09-07 audit baseline still carries as open boundary cases: truthy string publication flags (`'false'` treated as true), **private-ancestor details leaking into derived public exports**, and asserted-vs-inferred IRI divergence (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:18`, VG-01.4, VG-05.3). Derived public exports include the search index and ontology files the explorer serves from the site root (VG-03.3).
  4. One consumer's gate admits the page or its derived details; the push deploys to narrativegoldmine.com (`CLAUDE.md:23-26`, VG-05.1 I7) and the pod receives triples.
  5. Nothing catches it: ADR-VG-001's acceptance conditions — explicit per-input disposition, **boolean** publication metadata, a *tested* private-ancestor disclosure policy — are still `proposed`/`inactive` (`docs/adr/README.md:7-8`), and the 2026-10-01 banners state the Python publisher's retirement "does NOT certify" those acceptance conditions (`ADR-VG-001-publication-policy-boundaries.md:18-21`).
- Evidence: `PUBLICATION-contract.md:16` and `:18`; `CLAUDE.md:10-11`; `quartz/README.md:25-26`; `docs/adr/README.md:7-8`; ADR-VG-001 closeout banner `:18-21`; VG-05.3's own statement that "no real private-page disclosure is established" — i.e. safety was never verified, only un-incident-ed. Precedent that corpus content has leaked into artefacts before: the credential leak at `publish.yml:429-432` (`docs/migration-2026-09-22/credential-redaction.md`).
- Warning signs visible today: the four-way policy divergence is flagged DIVERGENCE (VG-01.3 note2); the contract-vs-rules gate conflict is flagged as Tension in VG-01.3; B2/B3/B4 sit open in the audit baseline; the ADRs that would close them are inactive with banners disclaiming certification.
- Confidence: high — the divergence and open boundary cases are stated; the specific leaking page is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Same source sha, two converter builds: the pod's ontology was produced by a different `vault` than the site's
- Topics: VG-04.1, VG-03.1, VG-04.3
- Chain of events:
  1. `publish.yml` builds the site's artefacts with `vault` at the `VAULT_GIT_REV` pin `90a7a85c` (`publish.yml:50`; VG-03.1 DEBT — `VAULT_RELEASE_TAG` is empty, every run builds from source, `publish.yml:43-50`).
  2. After a successful deploy it dispatches `corpus-sync` with `source_repo` + `source_sha` (`publish.yml:459-465`).
  3. VisionClaw's workflow checks out exactly that sha but builds `vault` **from its own default-branch checkout** via `cargo build -p vault` (`ontology-publish.yml:145-146`, `:167-168`) — not the pin.
  4. Over twelve months the two revisions diverge in parsing, gating semantics, or export shape (e.g. around the provisional `disjoint-with` rule, `vocabulary.yaml:219-231`).
  5. The PUT loop (`ontology-publish.yml:348-367`) publishes the differently-built `visionflow.ttl` / context / ontology to the Solid pod under an identity that names only the **corpus** sha — which matches. The prohibition on collapsing source/distribution/artefact identities (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:14`) is documented, but the one mechanism that would bind them (ADR-VG-002's release manifest with converter identity and output hashes) is proposed, not live (VG-04.3 note1, VG-05.2).
  6. The client consumes pod data that diverges from the site — different visibility decisions, different classes — with a provenance trail that looks correct.
- Evidence: VG-04.1 "Open:" paragraph — "the dispatch pins the corpus revision but not the converter… No record says whether that difference is intended"; `publish.yml:50`; `ontology-publish.yml:145-146,167-168`; VG-04.3 note1. Supporting precedent for a converter silently producing wrong-or-empty output from a valid corpus: `md_to_ttl.py` "shipped 0 owl:Class from 380 pages" (`ontology-publish.yml:127-132` history note).
- Warning signs visible today: the Open flag itself; the empty `VAULT_RELEASE_TAG` debt; the two substance floors that differ per workflow (1000-line floors in `publish.yml:280-289`; 4000/100k in VisionClaw per ADR-2106:38).
- Confidence: high that the converter skew exists (stated); medium that it produced this incident — that the two `vault` revisions disagree on semantics is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — A partial build passed every gate and published a diminished corpus as success
- Topics: VG-03.1, VG-03.5, VG-05.3
- Chain of events:
  1. A regression (workflow refactor, parser change at the pinned revision, corpus format drift) makes `vault build` emit a partial corpus. The documented precedent class: omitting `--vault all` "silently falls back to knowledge-only and every later gate still passes green" (`publish.yml:182-187`, VG-03.5 note1).
  2. The presence gate passes — the authors' own invariant is that "presence of an artefact path proves nothing" (VG-03.5 bullet, re the retired pipeline's empty bundle, `publish.yml:79`).
  3. The substance floors are absolute counts of ≥1000 (search-index entries, api/pages, `ontology.ttl` lines, `publish.yml:280-289`) against a corpus of thousands of pages and 8,434 classes (VG-04.1) — knowledge/ alone clears them.
  4. The one gate with real totals pins exact constants (934 identities / 660 drafts, `check-space-domains.mjs:53-54`) that every research batch hand-edits in the same commit — 56 gate-editing commits, "almost all of them article batches" (VG-03.5 DEBT) — so the routine is *make the gate match the build*, and a content regression is indistinguishable from a batch.
  5. The deploy proceeds; the only runtime check cited is an explorer smoke test whose assertions the material does not detail (`publish.yml:415-419`); the `corpus-sync` dispatch then propagates the same sha to the pod.
  6. The client's material has vanished from the published graph; nothing anywhere is red.
- Evidence: `publish.yml:79`, `:182-187`, `:247-289`, `:415-419`; `check-space-domains.mjs:53-54`; VG-03.5 bullets and DEBT; VG-05.3 STALE note that "57 passed and one corpus-size assertion failure" is a dated result, "NOT a fresh census".
- Warning signs visible today: both stated invariants (silent `--vault all` fallback; presence-proves-nothing), the hand-edited pinned totals, and the explicitly stale test census.
- Confidence: high — the gate weaknesses are stated; the triggering regression is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — The shared assets symlink stages ungoverned research-vault content into the public tree
- Topics: VG-03.5, VG-01.1, VG-05.1
- Evidence: `publish.yml:202-206` symlinks `knowledge/assets` into `quartz/content/assets`; `knowledge/assets` is itself a symlink to `../working/assets` (VG-01.1; VG-05.1 I5 "961 MB stored once, shared"). The only cited publication gates are page-level frontmatter (`CLAUDE.md:10-11`); page identity is defined only for `pages/` paths (`CLAUDE.md:13-14` via VG-01.5 note3). No per-asset gate appears anywhere in the record.
- Failure: a curator drops private research material into `working/assets` (a research vault whose pages are otherwise not published); the next deploy stages it into the Quartz content tree and it ships under `/notes/assets/`. Secondary: the whole 961 MB counts toward a 1 GB hard-fail size gate (`publish.yml:331-354`) — an availability cliff as the shared store grows.
- Confidence: inferred — the staging step and symlink chain are stated; the absence of an asset gate is inferred from the page-scoped gate definitions.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (recorded as an invariant about storage, not as a disclosure risk)

### F-02 — The contract still publishes a different inclusion interface than the enforced gate
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-05.1
- Evidence: `PUBLICATION-contract.md:16` describes VisionClaw's interface as `public` **or** `owl-class`; `CLAUDE.md:10-11` says the gate is typed `public: true` and only governed ontology types feed the projection. Flagged as Tension and DRIFT in VG-01.3 (the banner at `PUBLICATION-contract.md:3-8` marks the body historical but the line remains).
- Failure: an integrator or future maintainer implements to the contract text — including `owl-class` pages lacking `public: true`, or assuming `public: false` removes a page from every consumer — and produces disclosure or silent removal.
- Confidence: high (conflict stated)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Drift)

### F-03 — The workflow deploys on triggers the README does not list
- Topics: VG-01.1, VG-05.1
- Evidence: `README.md:125-126` lists `knowledge/**`, `quartz/**`, `ontology/**`; `publish.yml:23-29` also fires on `publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/modern/**` and `static/**`. A push deploys narrativegoldmine.com (`CLAUDE.md:23-26`).
- Failure: an edit to explorer source or static context documents — believed non-deploying per the README — triggers a production deploy of work-in-progress or unreviewed code, or, during incident response, maintainers avoid `knowledge/` edits believing that avoids a deploy while shipping something else.
- Confidence: high (stated drift; consequence is its direct implication)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-04 — The release gate demands publication of pages an accepted ADR records as private drafts
- Topics: VG-01.7, VG-03.5
- Evidence: `ADR-VG-004-space-and-earth-domains.md:35` — "the two roots exist as private draft classes… not deployed"; `check-space-domains.mjs:22-23` requires each root present in the **public** search index, run at `publish.yml:296-297`, which deploys on push.
- Failure: either every deploy fails until the drafts are made public (publish outage), or someone flips them public under pressure (forced disclosure of material documented as intended-private). One of the two must already be true at HEAD.
- Confidence: high (contradiction stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-05 — No live mechanism binds the identities of what was actually served
- Topics: VG-04.3, VG-05.2
- Evidence: `PUBLICATION-contract.md:14` prohibits collapsing source/distribution/artefact identities, but ADR-VG-002's release manifest (source/corpus/pipeline/explorer/dependency/policy identity plus output hashes) is "proposed, NOT yet activation_status: live" (VG-04.3 note1; VG-05.2). Only partial bindings exist: the dispatch sha and the staged-file hash-verify (`publish.yml:314-329`).
- Failure: after any incident, no artefact reconstructs which vault revision, converter build, explorer build and dependency set produced what a client actually received; the audit cannot establish the blast radius.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (carried as proposed/not-live)

### F-06 — A long-lived cross-account token grants VisionClaw's runner read access to the private corpus
- Topics: VG-04.1
- Evidence: "GITHUB_TOKEN cannot read a private cross-account repo — needs ONTOLOGY_SOURCE_TOKEN, checked lines 65-84" (`ontology-publish.yml:65-84`). No rotation, expiry or scoping is documented anywhere in the record.
- Failure: token compromise or over-scoping exfiltrates the entire private repository — including `working/`, the explicitly non-published research vault — in bulk.
- Confidence: inferred (token existence stated; compromise scenario is not)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Failed deploys leave the pod stale with no reconciliation
- Topics: VG-03.1, VG-04.1
- Evidence: the `corpus-sync` dispatch "runs only after the deploy step succeeds, so a failed deploy never refreshes the VisionClaw release" (`publish.yml:459-465`, VG-03.1 invariant). No staleness detection is documented in either workflow.
- Failure: the site advances through many deploys while the pod serves an increasingly old ontology; a client integrating against the pod receives stale data with no error raised anywhere.
- Confidence: medium (mechanism stated; the staleness consequence is its direct implication)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (presented as an invariant safety property)

### F-08 — The build pins a single commit in another repository with no release channel behind it
- Topics: VG-03.1, VG-04.3
- Evidence: `VAULT_RELEASE_TAG` empty; every run builds `vault` from `VAULT_GIT_REV` `90a7a85c`, and "the eight-domain publish depends on that exact VisionClaw revision" (`publish.yml:43-50`). The repo family has precedent for history rewrites — the logseq split "REWROTE every commit" (`README.md:150-154` via VG-04.3).
- Failure: the pinned commit becomes unreachable (force-push, restructure, deletion) and every publish fails — total publish outage with no tagged release to fall back to.
- Confidence: medium (debt stated; the dangling-pin scenario is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-09 — Two explorer copies, no sync discipline: fixes land in the copy nothing builds
- Topics: VG-06.1, VG-06.2, VG-06.4, VG-06.5
- Evidence: 33 shared files differ, up from 24 (VG-06.1); the sibling's CLAUDE.md correction "was never propagated to the tree that actually ships" (VG-06.2 note1); the sibling's `mesh.ts` LOOM edit is missing here while this tree's `router.tsx` gained sub-path support the sibling lacks — "no shared sync discipline either way" (VG-06.4); "the sibling can no longer be dropped in as a replacement" (VG-06.5); the sibling is built by nothing (KG-04.6).
- Failure: a security or correctness fix applied to the non-shipping copy never reaches production; or during incident response someone deploys the sibling and loses the eight-domain contract and sub-path routing.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, Debt)

### F-10 — The shipping tree's own docs instruct operators to build a crate that does not exist
- Topics: VG-06.2, VG-06.3
- Evidence: 12 `rust-wasm/` references including `wasm-pack build` recipes (`publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/CLAUDE.md` lines 25, 39-49, 96); reality is the npm-pinned `@dreamlab-ai/vowl-wasm` 0.1.2 (`WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:20`); `CAPABILITIES.md:6,24` still measures the retired Python pipeline.
- Failure: an on-call engineer or agent follows the documented recipe during an incident; the commands cannot succeed, and the pressure to "make the docs true" invites vendoring an untrusted look-alike crate. Anyone reasoning about the explorer's trust boundary from these docs is reasoning about a different system.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)

### F-11 — The corpus manifest promises a committed index file that does not exist
- Topics: VG-01.5
- Evidence: `vault.toml:30` — `knowledge/index.md`, "generated by vault build, committed"; "no such file exists at HEAD" (VG-01.5 note1).
- Failure: a consumer or script following the manifest reads a missing index and either fails or silently proceeds without it; the manifest — the record calls it the formalisation of the binding contract — misdescribes the build output.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-12 — Asserted vs inferred IRI representation diverges across outputs
- Topics: VG-01.4, VG-05.3
- Evidence: boundary case B4, "asserted vs inferred IRI representation divergence across outputs", unchanged since the 2026-09-07 audit (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:18` via VG-01.4); carried open under ADR-VG-001, proposed/inactive (`docs/adr/README.md:7-8`).
- Failure: two outputs represent the same entity differently; downstream consumers that merge or dedupe by IRI split one entity into two (or merge two into one) — a correctness failure in every consumer at once, invisible to any single-output test.
- Confidence: high that the divergence is recorded; medium that it produces the failure (the mechanism of divergence is not detailed)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (open boundary case under a proposed/inactive ADR)

### F-13 — Credential control is a source-side validate plus a post-build pattern scan over `www/`
- Topics: VG-03.6
- Evidence: "`check-secrets.sh` guards the artefact precisely because `vault validate` only guards the source" (VG-03.6); the corpus has had a live credential leak reach a build artefact before (`publish.yml:429-432`, `docs/migration-2026-09-22/credential-redaction.md`); the build now multiplies representations via `--with-markdown-mirror` (`publish.yml:188-193`) and ttl/jsonld exports.
- Failure: a credential re-enters the corpus in a transformed representation (mirrored, serialised, encoded) that the pattern rules over `www/` do not match; the self-test (`publish.yml:435-437`) proves the rules still match their fixtures, not that they match the new form.
- Confidence: inferred (controls and precedent stated; the evasion scenario is not)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT marks the precedent and states the source/artefact gap)

### F-14 — The substance floors are absolute counts on a growing corpus; they decay into no-ops
- Topics: VG-03.5, VG-04.1
- Evidence: floors of 1000 (`publish.yml:280-289`) against a corpus of thousands of pages and 8,434 classes / 265,455 triples (VG-04.1); VisionClaw's floor is 4000/100k (ADR-2106:38).
- Failure: a regression that loses most of the content still clears the floor; the floors were sized once and only ratchet incidentally.
- Confidence: high (numbers stated; the decoupling is arithmetic)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the authors assert SUBST's purpose without noting the decay)

### F-15 — The exact-sha provenance guarantee of `corpus-sync` is only a default; workflow inputs outrank the dispatch payload
- Topics: VG-04.1
- Evidence: "source repo resolves inputs, then client_payload, then the jjohare/visionGraph default — `ontology-publish.yml:30`".
- Failure: anyone able to trigger VisionClaw's workflow with inputs sets `ONTOLOGY_SOURCE_REPO` to a repository they control; the build-and-PUT loop publishes that content to the Solid pod under the same release path — the provenance guarantee the dispatch exists to provide is advisory, and the pod's contents are replaced.
- Confidence: medium — the resolution order is stated; whether any trigger surface lets an untrusted party set inputs is not shown in the material (inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

## vowl-wasm / critical

### F-01 — Parser silently truncates ontologies at `max_classes`, dropping classes without any error
- Topics: VW-02.1, VW-02.2
- Evidence: VW-02.2 states `ParserConfig` (src/ontology/parser.rs:17) enforces a `max_classes` cutoff at src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64 and "a `class` array longer than the configured cap is silently truncated, not rejected." The `loadOntology` path (VW-02.1, src/bindings/mod.rs:57-72) returns success after parsing; no warning is described anywhere in the chain.
- Failure: a consumer loads a large ontology, the parser silently keeps the first N classes, and the rendered graph is missing nodes with no error, no warning, and no count discrepancy signal — decisions get made on a partial model and nobody can tell from the API that data was dropped.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — `positionsPtr()` returns a raw pointer into growable WASM memory; any allocation invalidates the JS `Float32Array` view
- Topics: VW-01.7, VW-03.6, VW-04.2, VW-04.6
- Evidence: `NggExplorer.positionsPtr()` is "raw pointer into wasm.memory.buffer" (src/bindings/explorer.rs:82,89, VW-04.2) and the worker constructs `new Float32Array(wasm.memory.buffer, ptr, len)` (VW-04.6). The "never allocates per frame" invariant (VW-01.7, explorer.rs:9) covers only frames — `loadCsr` does allocate (`positions`/`velocities`/edge `Vec`s, src/layout/csr_sim.rs:193-198, VW-03.6). Nothing in the material states pointer stability or a memory-reservation strategy.
- Failure: a second `loadCsr` (next tier) grows the linear memory and detaches every previously created view; worse with two `NggExplorer` instances in one worker, where loading into B invalidates A's live view — reads return `undefined`/NaN and the renderer silently draws garbage with no exception at the fault site.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the detachment mechanism is standard WASM behavior; the material does not show a mitigation)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — A settled `WebVowl` graph cannot be re-tuned: `tick()` early-returns and no reheat exists, while the only "restart" (`initSimulation`) re-seeds positions by default
- Topics: VW-03.1, VW-03.7, VW-03.9, VW-04.1
- Evidence: `tick()` returns early when `is_finished()` (src/layout/simulation.rs:269-271), which is a pure `alpha < alpha_min` read (src/layout/simulation.rs:332). VW-03.7 (marked DOC-DRIFT) states only `CsrSimulation`/`NggExplorer` have `reheat`; "a settled `WebVowl` graph can only be restarted via a fresh `initSimulation()`". But `initialize` seeds a fresh circular layout unless `preserve_seeded_positions` is set (src/layout/simulation.rs:101-105, VW-03.9), and no such toggle appears on the frozen JS surface (VW-04.1). Meanwhile `setCenter`/`setLinkDistance`/`setChargeStrength` (src/bindings/mod.rs:218-230) remain callable.
- Failure: a user tunes parameters after the layout settles — the calls return success and every subsequent `tick()` is a silent no-op; the sole documented recovery (`initSimulation()`) throws away the current layout and re-seeds everyone onto a circle.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT covers the missing reheat; the silent no-op tuning and the reseed-destroys-layout consequence are not marked)

### F-04 — `OWL2Validator` produces "hard errors" that never reject anything
- Topics: VW-02.6
- Evidence: VW-02.6's own invariant (src/ontology/owl2_validator.rs:37-43) says IRI-format and uniqueness failures are hard `errors` while DL/antipattern findings are warnings — and in the same breath that "`validate_block` never rejects a block outright." These two claims conflict: an "error" that cannot block anything is not an error.
- Failure: a consumer wires `validateOWL2` into a pipeline assuming `errors` are gating; malformed or duplicate IRIs pass validation and enter the graph/node_map anyway, corrupting IRI-keyed downstream logic while the pipeline reports "validated".
- Confidence: high (the contradiction is stated); medium on the downstream-consumer scenario, inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — `#![deny(unsafe_code)]` contradicts the `unsafe` SIMD kernel, and no CI job ever compiles that combination
- Topics: VW-01.1, VW-01.4, VW-03.4, VW-05.1, VW-05.2
- Evidence: src/lib.rs:141-142 declares `#!deny(missing_docs, unsafe_code)` (VW-01.1), while src/layout/simd.rs:386 is `unsafe calculate_leaf_force_simd()` under `target_feature simd128` (VW-03.4). The wasm32 CI job builds only `--features ngg1,interaction` (VW-05.1); the feature matrix runs native `cargo check` where the wasm32 path is `cfg`-ed out; the release bundle likewise builds `ngg1,interaction` (VW-05.3); and `simd` "needs simd128 target-feature" (VW-01.4), which no cited build enables. Native "SIMD" benches measure only the scalar shim (`is_simd_available()` is hardcoded `false` off-wasm32, src/layout/simd.rs:525).
- Failure: two possibilities, both defects: either an undocumented `#[allow(unsafe_code)]` exists and memory-safety-critical unsafe code ships with zero CI compilation or test coverage and its performance is validated only against a scalar stub; or the allow doesn't exist and `simd` on wasm32 — the feature's entire purpose — cannot compile, discovered only by the first person who tries it.
- Confidence: high that the combination is never compiled in CI; medium on which failure mode obtains
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — The coincident-node zero-division guard is enumerated for the scalar and CSR paths only — the SIMD leaf kernel is excluded from the invariant
- Topics: VW-03.4, VW-03.5
- Evidence: VW-03.4's INVARIANT lists exactly two guarded paths — the scalar leaf path (src/layout/quadtree.rs:384-387) and the CSR tick (VW-03.5) — substituting a deterministic nudge for `distance_sq < 0.0001`. The third leaf path, `calculate_leaf_force_simd()` (src/layout/simd.rs:386) and its shim (src/layout/simd.rs:479), is not covered by the stated guard.
- Failure: with `simd` enabled on wasm32, a leaf holding ≥4 coincident nodes (common when identical seed positions collide) divides by ~0 in the kernel; Inf/NaN enters positions, propagates through integration, and the layout is permanently NaN until reload — and per F-05 this path has never been compiled or tested anyway.
- Confidence: medium, inferred from the invariant's explicit enumeration of two-of-three paths
- Severity: medium (would be high the moment `simd` ships)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — NGG1 format compatibility with the external TypeScript counterpart is a stated requirement with no enforcing test in this repo — and the format brief has already been wrong once
- Topics: VW-03.6, VW-04.5, VW-04.6
- Evidence: the reader "must stay byte-compatible with its TypeScript counterpart `modern/src/lib/ngg1.ts`" (src/ngg1.rs:3-4) while the canonical spec `publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/FORMAT-NGG1.md` lives in the EXTERNAL visionGraph repo. The crate doc "corrects a self-inconsistent brief" on node stride — 24 bytes, not 20 (src/ngg1.rs:15-16, `N_DEGREE` offset 20 at :31): documented proof the format description has already drifted once. The CI jobs listed (VW-05.1, VW-05.2) include no fixture or cross-test against TS-produced bytes; the only cross-artifact check is a string grep of `pkg/vowl_wasm.d.ts` (VW-04.5). The consumer already hedges, resolving exports "permissively (snake_case per Rust, with camelCase fallbacks)" (physics.worker.ts:33-35).
- Failure: visionGraph evolves the writer (stride, field order, header); the npm-pinned reader misparses tiers — the magic can still match while counts and fields shift, yielding silently wrong graphs or worker crashes, and nothing in either repo's CI fails.
- Confidence: high on the stated facts; medium on the absence of any cross-repo contract test (based on the CI enumeration given)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (EXTERNAL marks provenance, not this risk)

### F-08 — `panic = "abort"`, no panic hook, stripped symbols: any Rust panic is an opaque trap that kills the instance
- Topics: VW-01.5, VW-05.5
- Evidence: `init()` is "no-op today, reserved for panic-hook install" (src/lib.rs:162,169, VW-01.5); the release profile sets `panic=abort` and `strip=true` (Cargo.toml `[profile.release]`, VW-05.5). With `panic=abort`, wasm-bindgen cannot convert panics to catchable JS errors — the module traps.
- Failure: any panic — e.g., malformed NGG1 bytes reaching an unchecked slice index (bounds handling is not described, see Not-judgeable) — aborts the worker mid-frame with a bare "unreachable" trap, no error kind, no stack, and the instance is dead; production triage has nothing to work from.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (facts stated; the trap/diagnostic consequence is standard wasm behavior reasoned beyond the material)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the init no-op is noted, its consequence is not)

### F-09 — `CsrSimulation`'s ≤1,500-node scope is a doc comment, not an enforced limit
- Topics: VW-03.5, VW-03.6, VW-03.10
- Evidence: the crate doc scopes the always-O(n²) repulsion "to ≤1,500-node tiers (T1)" and calls Barnes-Hut "a follow-up slot" (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:9-11, marked DIVERGENCE in VW-03.5). The `loadCsr` path (VW-03.6) shows parse → `load` with no node-count gate, and `SimConfig::default()` is the only configuration (VW-03.10).
- Failure: a tier file with 5,000 nodes (nothing described rejects it) means 12.5M pair computations per tick; the <10ms tick budget is blown, the worker's frame loop stalls, and the explorer degrades with no error pointing at the size cause.
- Confidence: medium (the missing check is argued from absence in the described load path)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (DIVERGENCE marks the O(n²) scope; the unenforced cap is not marked)

### F-10 — Pinning may ship disabled by default with no JS-reachable way to enable it
- Topics: VW-02.8, VW-04.1
- Evidence: `PinManager { enabled: bool }` and `pin_node` returns `VowlError::InteractionError("Pinning is disabled")` when `enabled == false` (src/graph/pinning.rs:43-46, "independent of graph state"). The frozen JS surface (VW-04.1) enumerates `pinNode/unpinNode/isPinned/resetPins/getPinnedCount` — no `enablePinning`/`setPinningEnabled` appears. The default value of `enabled` is not stated anywhere in the material.
- Failure: if `PinManager` is constructed with `enabled == false` (the typical Rust `bool` default), every `pinNode()` call from JS fails with "Pinning is disabled" and the frozen 0.1.0 surface offers no remedy — the entire pinning feature is dead on arrival in the published bundle.
- Confidence: low, inferred (default unstated; the surface list could be abridged)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — The only verified test evidence covers default features; the shipped bundle's features and any browser execution are untested by the cited runs
- Topics: VW-01 (audit qualification), VW-05.1, VW-05.2
- Evidence: the audit note states `cargo test --locked --offline --lib` "passes 136 default-feature tests. That run covers native logic, not optional `ngg1`, `markdown-ontology`, `interaction`, browser loading or a deployed consumer." The wasm32 CI job is build-only (`cargo build --target wasm32...`), and the wasm-pack job verifies `.d.ts` symbol strings — no job in the material executes the compiled WASM in a JS environment.
- Failure: a regression in the `ngg1`/`interaction` binding layer (type marshalling, `Vec<f32>` copying, `loadCsr` glue) passes every green CI check and ships to npm; the downstream physics worker is the first thing to exercise it, in production.
- Confidence: high (as far as the material shows)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (the audit qualification states it directly)

### F-12 — All error kinds are erased to strings at the boundary
- Topics: VW-01.6, VW-02.1, VW-03.6
- Evidence: "`VowlError` never crosses the boundary, only its rendered message" (VW-01.6, `From` impl src/error.rs:43); each load stage maps to `JsValue::from_str` (src/bindings/mod.rs:59,63,67); `Ngg1Error` is mapped "via to_string" (src/bindings/explorer.rs:42-44).
- Failure: JS consumers cannot distinguish a parse failure from invalid data, a layout error, or a binding bug; any conditional handling or retry policy degenerates to string-matching against messages that are free to change between patch releases of a frozen-surface crate.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — `checkNodeClick` uses a fixed world-space radius of 20.0 for every node
- Topics: VW-04.3
- Evidence: hit tests are built with `radius=20.0 fixed` (src/bindings/mod.rs:501-513), with the cited comment "In a real implementation, this could be configurable or per-node" (src/bindings/mod.rs:503-504). The API is ray-based (`ray_origin`/`ray_direction`), i.e. a camera/3D context where world-space size maps to varying screen size.
- Failure: zoomed out, every node subtends far less than 20 world units on screen and picking becomes impossible; zoomed in or in dense clusters, the constant radius grabs the wrong node; nodes with different visual sizes are indistinguishable to the picker.
- Confidence: medium (the zoom interaction is inferred from the ray API; the fixed constant is stated)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (acknowledged in the cited code comment)

### F-14 — Two layout engines share a design but no interface: fixes must be mirrored by hand, and the drift has already started
- Topics: VW-03.4, VW-03.5, VW-03.7
- Evidence: "`CsrSimulation` deliberately does not [implement `LayoutAlgorithm`]" (VW-03.7); the coincident-node nudge exists as two independent implementations (src/layout/quadtree.rs:377-389 and src/layout/csr_sim.rs:219-226); alpha annealing is implemented twice; `reheat` exists on only one engine — an asymmetry the authors themselves flag as DOC-DRIFT.
- Failure: a physics defect fixed in `ForceSimulation` (damping bug, better nudge, annealing correction) silently fails to reach `CsrSimulation` and vice versa; the already-present reheat asymmetry is the demonstrated first instance of this class of divergence.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: partially (the divergence is documented as deliberate; the duplication-as-defect-risk is not flagged)

### F-15 — Every stated performance target is unenforced, and one of them benchmarks a feature that is not in the shipped bundle
- Topics: VW-01.4, VW-05.4
- Evidence: `phase3_benchmarks.rs` header states tick <10ms (8ms target), `getGraphData()` <5ms, `checkNodeClick` <1ms/1000 nodes, bundle <1.5MB — described as "aspirational annotations in the source, not enforced by any CI assertion" (VW-05.4). `getGraphData()` requires `debug-serde` (VW-01.4), which is not in the published bundle (`ngg1,interaction` only). Bundle size is additionally guarded only by convention, since `wasm-opt` is deliberately disabled (VW-05.5).
- Failure: a change regresses tick time past 10ms or grows the bundle past 1.5MB and every CI check stays green; the serialisation target is measured on a configuration (`debug-serde`) that no consumer ever receives, so it certifies nothing about production.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes ("aspirational annotations … not enforced" is the authors' own framing; the feature-mismatch aspect is not marked)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. NGG1 reader robustness on malformed input: whether `loadCsr` validates buffer length against header counts and node stride before slicing, or whether truncated/misaligned bytes panic (which, with `panic=abort`, is fatal per F-08). Need `src/ngg1.rs` and its tests.
2. The default value of `PinManager.enabled` and whether any constructor or binding sets it — this alone decides F-10 between "dead feature" and "non-issue". Need `src/graph/pinning.rs` and the `WebVowl` constructor.
3. Whether `loadOntology` on a reused `WebVowl` resets simulation alpha, `PinManager` state (stale pinned ids against a new graph), and filter/visibility state. Need `src/bindings/mod.rs` in full.
4. Whether any wasm-runtime test exists at all (wasm-bindgen-test, headless browser, or a consumer-side e2e in visionGraph) — the material shows none, but absence from a distillation is not absence from the repo; this decides how much weight F-11 carries.
5. Whether the crate reserves or pins linear memory (or otherwise guarantees `positionsPtr` stability across `loadCsr`) — need `src/bindings/explorer.rs` and the memory strategy to confirm or retire F-02.

## vowl-wasm / premortem

# Post-mortem — vowl-wasm production incident (twelve-month review)

Basis: the distilled VW-01…VW-05 material only. No source beyond the cited paths/lines was available; every finding below is flagged where it is inferred rather than stated. All claims should be re-verified against the code before remediation, as instructed.

---

### R-1 — Large ontologies are silently truncated at `ParserConfig.max_classes`, with no error on any path
- Topics: VW-02.2, VW-02.1, VW-04.1
- Chain of events: a client ontology whose `class` array exceeds the configured cap is parsed by `StandardParser::parse_classes`; classes beyond the cap are dropped; `GraphBuilder::from_ontology` builds a graph over the truncated set; `parser.validate` sees only what was parsed; `loadOntology` returns success to JS; the client views, filters and computes statistics over an incomplete graph, with no error, warning or count signal anywhere.
- Evidence: "a `class` array longer than the configured cap is silently truncated, not rejected" (src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64; `ParserConfig` at src/ontology/parser.rs:17). Only `VowlError` is mapped to `JsValue` at the boundary (src/bindings/mod.rs:59,63,67) — truncation produces no `VowlError`. The frozen 0.1.0 surface (VW-04.1) exposes no partial-load or limit-exceeded signal.
- Warning signs visible today: the silent-truncation behaviour is documented as current fact, not flagged as a defect; the default `max_classes` value is not stated anywhere in the material, so operators cannot know the threshold they are running against.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high.

### R-2 — NGG1 binary ingest: cross-repo byte-format drift and no described bounds validation on length fields
- Topics: VW-03.6, VW-03.5, VW-04.6, VW-05.2
- Chain of events: the canonical NGG1 spec lives in the external visionGraph repo; the TypeScript writer and this Rust reader evolve independently with no shared conformance test cited; a writer-side change (record size, field offset, or endianness — e.g. a `DataView` write omitting the little-endian flag) ships; this reader, which decodes strictly little-endian at fixed offsets (`from_le_bytes`, header 32 B, stride 24 B), either misinterprets fields — wrong ids/degrees/seed positions rendered as a valid-looking graph — or reads a header `node_count` that exceeds the remaining bytes, panicking; under `panic = "abort"` (VW-05.5 profile) the wasm instance dies inside the worker.
- Evidence: "this reader must stay byte-compatible with its TypeScript counterpart `modern/src/lib/ngg1.ts`" (src/ngg1.rs:3-4); "the node record is fixed at **24 bytes**, not 20 — the crate doc corrects a self-inconsistent brief" (src/ngg1.rs:15-16, `N_DEGREE` offset 20 at src/ngg1.rs:31) — documented proof the format's own brief was wrong once; constants `NGG1_HEADER_SIZE=32`, `NGG1_NODE_STRIDE=24` (src/ngg1.rs:21-24); position/velocity buffers sized straight from the parsed graph (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:193-194) with no validation step described between `Ngg1::parse` and allocation.
- Warning signs visible today: the prior 20-vs-24-byte self-inconsistency; spec custody outside this repo; the recorded audit ran only 136 default-feature tests and explicitly did not cover `ngg1` (VW-01 audit note), while `ngg1_explorer_test` is silently skipped without `--features ngg1` (VW-05.2).
- Confidence: medium — the drift risk, prior doc error and external custody are stated; the absence of length-field validation and of a shared test is inferred from the material's silence.
- Severity: high.

### R-3 — Zero-copy position view over `wasm.memory.buffer` goes stale when linear memory grows
- Topics: VW-04.2, VW-01.7, VW-04.6
- Chain of events: the worker constructs `new Float32Array(wasm.memory.buffer, ptr, len)` from `positionsPtr()`/`positionsLen()`; a subsequent `loadCsr` of a new tier reallocates position/velocity `Vec`s inside wasm (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:193-198), which can grow the linear memory; the previously constructed JS view over the old buffer detaches (reads return undefined / writes throw) or, if the worker caches the pointer rather than the view, points at freed/reused offsets; the client sees frozen or wrong node data with no error from the crate. The per-frame no-allocation invariant (VW-01.7) holds *between* reloads, not across them — the reload itself is the allocation event.
- Evidence: `positionsPtr`/`positionsLen` expose a raw pointer and length over `wasm.memory.buffer` with no generation or validity token (src/bindings/explorer.rs:82,89); the worker's view construction is shown (VW-04.6 step 6); nothing in the material states the worker re-derives pointer and view after each `loadCsr`. The `writePositionsInto` doc explicitly distances itself from this path (src/bindings/mod.rs:369-374).
- Warning signs visible today: the API hands out a raw pointer whose lifetime contract is entirely implicit; the worker comment about "SharedArrayBuffer or transferable double-buffer" (VW-04.6) shows buffering is unresolved at the consumer.
- Confidence: medium; inferred — the pointer contract and the reload-time reallocation are stated, but the worker's caching behaviour and whether builds use shared memory are not shown.
- Severity: high.

---

### F-01 — QuadTree subdivision can recurse without bound on exactly-coincident nodes
- Topics: VW-03.3, VW-03.4
- Evidence: `insert` pushes into a leaf until capacity, then `subdivide` redistributes into 4 children (src/layout/quadtree.rs:184-238); no maximum-depth guard appears in the described flow; the codebase elsewhere explicitly expects coincident positions to occur (scalar leaf nudge, src/layout/quadtree.rs:377-389; CSR nudge, src/layout/csr_sim.rs:219-226).
- Failure: two nodes with identical coordinates always fall into the same child quadrant; every depth exceeds capacity → unbounded recursion → stack overflow panic → `panic = "abort"` kills the instance mid-layout.
- Confidence: medium — the recursion path is fully described; the absence of a depth guard is inferred from silence.
- Severity: high.
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-02 — Crate advertises `#![deny(unsafe_code)]` while shipping `unsafe` SIMD kernels
- Topics: VW-01.1, VW-03.4
- Evidence: `src/lib.rs:141-142 #!deny(missing_docs, unsafe_code)` (VW-01.1) versus "unsafe `calculate_leaf_force_simd()` src/layout/simd.rs:386" (VW-03.4). Both can only compile if `simd.rs` carries a module-level `#[allow(unsafe_code)]`, which the material never mentions.
- Failure: memory-adjacent unsafe code sits behind the non-default `simd` feature, invisible to any audit that trusts the crate-level deny; a future kernel edit ships under an intact "no unsafe" claim.
- Confidence: high that the contradiction exists (both facts stated); the allow-attribute is inferred.
- Severity: medium.
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-03 — `checkNodeClick`: fixed 20.0 hit radius and silent `None` on malformed input
- Topics: VW-04.3
- Evidence: radius hard-coded to 20.0 in the binding (src/bindings/mod.rs:501-513), with the source's own comment "In a real implementation, this could be configurable or per-node" (src/bindings/mod.rs:503-504); wrong-length ray arrays degrade to `console.warn` + `None` (src/bindings/mod.rs:485-491).
- Failure: hit-testing selects the wrong node (or none) whenever visual size ≠ 20; malformed input is swallowed as a no-hit instead of surfacing an error.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: low.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt — acknowledged in the cited code comment).

### F-04 — The OWL2 validator can never reject: DL violations and antipatterns are warnings only
- Topics: VW-02.6, VW-02.2
- Evidence: stated invariant — IRI-format/uniqueness are hard errors, "DL-compliance and antipattern findings are `warnings` only … `validate_block` never rejects a block outright" (src/ontology/owl2_validator.rs:37-43); the batch path only merges results (src/ontology/owl2_validator.rs:441, :48).
- Failure: an ontology carrying DL antipatterns loads and renders as authoritative; downstream consumers treat invalid constructs as validated because "a validator ran and passed".
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium.
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-05 — Feature-gated tests silently vanish from a plain `cargo test`
- Topics: VW-05.2, VW-01 (audit note)
- Evidence: `required-features` ties `markdown_parser_test`/`owl2_validation_test` to `markdown-ontology` and `ngg1_explorer_test` to `ngg1` — "a plain `cargo test` without those flags silently skips them" (Cargo.toml `[[test]]` blocks); the recorded audit ran only 136 default-feature tests, explicitly excluding `ngg1`, `markdown-ontology`, `interaction`.
- Failure: an engineer verifies locally, sees green, and ships a change that breaks the NGG1 reader or validator; only the CI `--all-features` run can catch it, and only when it runs.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium.
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-06 — No re-anneal path exists for `WebVowl` graphs
- Topics: VW-03.7, VW-03.9, VW-04.1
- Evidence: labelled DOC-DRIFT — only `CsrSimulation`/`NggExplorer` expose `reheat` (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:368, src/bindings/explorer.rs:106); "a settled `WebVowl` graph can only be restarted via a fresh `initSimulation()`".
- Failure: after user edits or pinning, re-layout requires full re-init; with `preserve_seeded_positions` off this circularly reseeds (src/layout/simulation.rs:101-105), discarding the current arrangement unexpectedly.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: low.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift).

### F-07 — Panics abort the whole instance and no panic hook is installed
- Topics: VW-01.5, VW-05.5
- Evidence: `init()` is "no-op today, reserved for panic-hook install" (src/lib.rs:162 per VW-01.5); `[profile.release] panic = "abort"` (VW-05.5).
- Failure: any internal panic (F-01 overflow, F-02 kernel defect, an unwrap in parse) aborts the module as an opaque trap with no stack; the worker dies unrecoverably and triage has no diagnostic.
- Confidence: high (both facts stated); panic frequency inferred.
- Severity: medium.
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — "reserved" slot).

### F-08 — Error taxonomy is erased at the WASM boundary
- Topics: VW-01.6, VW-02.8
- Evidence: every binding returns `Result<T, JsValue>`; the typed `VowlError` "never crosses the boundary, only its rendered message" (VW-01.6; `From` impls at src/error.rs:43,49; `to_string` mapping at src/bindings/explorer.rs:42-44).
- Failure: JS cannot distinguish e.g. `InteractionError("Pinning is disabled")` (src/graph/pinning.rs:43-46) from a parse failure; consumers string-match English messages to branch, breaking on any rewording.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: low.
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-09 — `writePositionsInto` partial-write contract invites stale position tails
- Topics: VW-04.2, VW-04.1
- Evidence: writes `min(out.len(), n*2)` and returns the count written (src/bindings/mod.rs:376, :395-407); the index-stability contract (VW-04.1) lets JS zip `ids[i] ↔ xy[2i]` once per load, so a stale tail is silently misattributed.
- Failure: a consumer supplies an undersized buffer and ignores the returned count → the array tail keeps last frame's (or seed) coordinates → a subset of nodes renders at stale positions with no error.
- Confidence: medium — contract stated; consumer misuse inferred.
- Severity: medium.
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-10 — SIMD and scalar leaf-force paths can yield different layouts for the same graph
- Topics: VW-03.4, VW-03.10
- Evidence: dispatch selects the SIMD kernel when the feature is on and `sources.len() >= 4` (src/layout/quadtree.rs:341-343); only the scalar fold is labelled "deterministic" (src/layout/quadtree.rs:377-389); `is_simd_available()` differs by target (src/layout/simd.rs:516,525); no bit-equality claim between paths appears anywhere.
- Failure: the same ontology lays out differently on simd vs non-simd builds (or wasm vs native benches) — non-reproducible renders and benchmarks that do not compare like with like.
- Confidence: medium; inferred — numerical reordering is the standard consequence; no equivalence test is cited.
- Severity: low.
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-11 — `CsrSimulation`'s ≤1,500-node O(n²) scope is a doc comment, not an enforced cap
- Topics: VW-03.5, VW-03.10, VW-03.6
- Evidence: "always O(n²) — the crate-level doc … scopes this to ≤1,500-node tiers (T1) and calls Barnes-Hut for this path 'a follow-up slot', not yet implemented" (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:9-11); no node-count rejection is described in `load_csr` (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:182-200).
- Failure: a producer emits a larger tier (or a corrupt header inflates `node_count`); per-tick cost grows quadratically past frame budget → the worker stalls or is killed by the consumer → the client sees frozen or missing data.
- Confidence: medium — scope is stated as documentation; absence of enforcement inferred.
- Severity: medium.
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence/Open — "follow-up slot, not yet implemented").

### F-12 — Weekly advisory cadence leaves up to a seven-day vulnerable-dependency window
- Topics: VW-05.1
- Evidence: `cargo-deny` runs on the weekly cron only, "because it checks a live advisory database — a green build can go red without a code change" (ci.yml:10-11); the push/PR job list does not include deny (VW-05.1).
- Failure: an advisory lands the day after a release; the next published bundle ships the vulnerable dependency for up to a week before any gate observes it.
- Confidence: high (cadence stated); exploitability inferred.
- Severity: medium.
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-13 — `render::SvgRenderer` is compiled into every build but unreachable from the published API
- Topics: VW-01.1, VW-04.5
- Evidence: "exists but is not wired to any `#[wasm_bindgen]` binding — a native-only diagnostic path … unreachable from the published JS API" (src/render/mod.rs:20 per VW-01.1); the CI bundle check verifies exactly seven explorer symbols (VW-04.5), so nothing exercises this path.
- Failure: dead, always-compiled surface that bit-rots silently while docs or claims may still cite an SVG export capability that JS consumers cannot reach.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: low.
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-14 — Pin set plausibly survives `loadOntology` graph replacement
- Topics: VW-02.8, VW-02.1, VW-04.1
- Evidence: `load_ontology` stores metadata and graph on `self` (src/bindings/mod.rs:71-72); `PinManager` state is an id-keyed `HashSet<String>` with an explicit `reset()` (src/graph/pinning.rs:156); the material nowhere states `load_ontology` clears pins.
- Failure: a reused `WebVowl` instance loads a second ontology; overlapping ids (common IRI-local names) arrive pre-pinned at the previous ontology's coordinates — frozen, mislabelled nodes with `isPinned()` true and no user action.
- Confidence: low; inferred from the material's silence.
- Severity: low.
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-15 — The feature matrix compiles each configuration but never tests it; the bundle check covers only seven symbols
- Topics: VW-05.2, VW-05.1, VW-04.5
- Evidence: the features job runs `cargo check --all-targets` per configuration (VW-05.2) — its stated purpose is catching unused imports invisible to `--all-features`; tests run only under `--all-features` in the separate test job (VW-05.1); the release bundle gate asserts exactly seven `.d.ts` symbols (VW-04.5).
- Failure: a behaviour bug that manifests only in one feature combination (compiles fine, behaves differently) passes the matrix; a rename of any export outside the seven checked symbols ships to the worker consumer undetected until runtime.
- Confidence: medium.
- Severity: medium.
- Marked by authors: no.

---

Verification notes for the reviewing engineer: (1) the `max_classes` default value and whether any caller raises it is the single highest-value check for R-1; (2) for R-2, diff `src/ngg1.rs` constants against `publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/FORMAT-NGG1.md` and `modern/src/lib/ngg1.ts` at the currently pinned package version, and grep the reader for length-field bounds checks; (3) for R-3, inspect whether `physics.worker.ts` rebuilds its `Float32Array` after every `loadCsr`, and whether the build uses shared memory; (4) for F-01, search `src/layout/quadtree.rs` for a max-depth constant — the material shows none; (5) for F-02, confirm the module-level `#[allow(unsafe_code)]` that must exist in `src/layout/simd.rs` for the crate to compile.
